# Investoyard — Project Guide (CLAUDE.md)

> Auto-loaded each session. Investoyard is an **independent project** (it was split out of an
> unrelated `D:\FINWAVE AOF` account-opening-forms project on 2026-06-21 — they are NOT related).

## What this is
**Investoyard** — a B2C **IPO information + application** platform for India (Mainboard + SME).
Independent product that consumes the operator's existing **merchant-banker IPO-distribution
empanelment** (Axis Capital / Nuvama / JM Financial) as its application rail. Brand: indigo
`#3c2e7e` + gold `#ffcb32`. Tagline: "Investing in IPOs, has never been this easy."
Operating entity: **Safal Capital Services Private Limited**.

Three business models, one platform: **M1 B2C (live)** → **M2 broker white-label (partner Print-PDF API + self-onboarding shipped)** → M3 B2B2C.

## Status (as of 2026-09-30) — LIVE, deployed on this Windows server
- **Web**: https://investoyard.com — Next.js **static export** served by IIS from `apps/web/out`. Deploy = `cd apps/web && npm run deploy` (no pool involved).
  - **`npm run build` IS NO LONGER THE DEPLOY, AND THE REASON IT CHANGED COST 25 MINUTES OF DOWNTIME** (2026-10-16). Next CLEARS its export directory before writing it, and while that directory was the live one a clear that died half way took the site off the air: `ENOTEMPTY: rmdir out\hi\ipos` — a file handle held inside `out/hi/ipos`, almost certainly IIS serving from the folder Next was deleting. All 2,601 pages had generated fine; the export then emptied `out/` of its `index.html` and `web.config` and failed. **`out/` is gitignored, so there is no restore — only a full rebuild.** Note the build still exited with pages generated, so a log that greps only for `Generating static pages (n/n)` reads as a success.
  - **THE BUILD NOW WRITES TO `apps/web/out-staging` AND CANNOT TOUCH WHAT IIS SERVES.** With `output: 'export'`, Next treats `distDir` AS the export directory — `hasCustomExportOutput` in `next/dist/export/utils.js` is literally `output === "export" && distDir !== ".next"`, and it then pins `config.distDir` back to `.next` for its own manifests, **so the ~180 MB webpack cache is NOT affected** (verified: `.next/cache/webpack` survives a staged build). One line in `next.config.mjs`, no IIS change, no elevation, reversible.
  - **`scripts/publish-web.mjs` GATES THE PUBLISH, and the page-count floor is the check that earns its keep.** Existence checks catch a build that died; they do not catch the catastrophic one, which is `safeGet` swallowing a dead API and collapsing every dynamic route to the `no-ipos` placeholder — **that build exits 0 and its log looks perfect**. So the gate requires ipo pages ≥ 80% of what is CURRENTLY LIVE, plus `index.html` ≥ 1 MB, `web.config`, `_next/static`, and a real `<title>`. It exits **1** on refusal, which is what makes `npm run deploy` fail rather than report success. Both refusal paths are tested; neither touches `out/`.
  - **TWO ROBOCOPY PASSES AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING**: `/E` copies new and changed files with no deletions, THEN `/MIR` purges stale ones. A single `/MIR` can remove a hashed chunk before the HTML that replaces it has landed, and a visitor mid-publish 404s an asset. `/R:3 /W:2` retries, because a locked file is exactly the failure class this exists for — robocopy retries where Next's `rmdir` aborted. **Robocopy's exit code is a BITMASK, not a status** (0–7 all mean success; bit 0 = files copied, bit 1 = extras removed), so treating non-zero as failure fails every publish.
  - **The first request after a publish can be slow enough to time out** — the root was measured at >15 s once immediately after a publish and 200 on every probe after. Same cold-path behaviour already documented for a new route, now reachable on an existing one because the file was just replaced. Do not read one `000` from curl as an outage; re-probe.
  - **THE SITE MOVED FROM `newipo.finwave.co` TO `investoyard.com` ON 2026-10-15, and the API DELIBERATELY DID NOT MOVE WITH IT.** `newipoapi.finwave.co` still serves every API call: partner integrations already hold that base URL and the shipped mobile build is compiled against it, so retiring it breaks both with nothing gained — the consumer never sees the API host, and **neither partner PDF prints a domain at all any more**, so no document goes stale either. Moving it later means standing up `api.investoyard.com` **alongside** it, never instead of it.
  - **THE CANONICAL HOST IS BAKED IN AT BUILD TIME, so a domain move is a REBUILD and not a server setting.** `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_ORIGIN` → `apps/web/lib/site.ts` → `metadataBase`, every `canonical`, every `og:url`, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt` **and the pre-paint own-host script**. One static export carries exactly ONE canonical host; until the rebuild lands, every page on the new domain still tells Google it is a copy of the old one.
  - **`OWN_HOSTS` IN `site.ts` IS THE LIST, AND A RETIRED HOST MUST STAY ON IT.** The pre-paint script sets `data-foreign-host` on anything not in that list, which hides `[data-own-only]` — our own About, Terms, Privacy, Disclaimer and footer identity. So dropping the old host from the list does not merely stop redirecting it, it makes **our own legal pages render as though nobody had published them** on any cached page still being served from it. Same reasoning for the CORS `DEFAULT_ORIGINS` in `main.ts`: a browser holding a cached page from the old host fires its API calls from the **old origin**, and a CORS rejection is not something a browser can follow a redirect out of — it is a blank, unbranded site. **Both lists carry all four hosts; prune them only once no cached HTML can plausibly survive.**
  - **The 301 lives in `apps/web/public/web.config` as the FIRST rewrite rule, and the order is load-bearing.** The four SPA fallbacks below it carry `stopProcessing`, so a request for an unbuilt `/ipos/X` on the old host would be **rewritten and served with a 200** — a live page on the domain being retired, and a second indexable copy of the catalogue. The rule folds `www.investoyard.com` and both `newipo.finwave.co` hosts into the canonical one, and doubles as the plain-HTTP upgrade. `localhost` is deliberately NOT matched: this file ships inside `out/`, and redirecting a local copy to production makes it impossible to inspect.
  - **`investoyard.com` IS ON GODADDY NAMESERVERS AND POINTS STRAIGHT AT THE ORIGIN — no Cloudflare** (operator's call, 2026-10-15). Two consequences worth knowing before anyone "fixes" it: the cached-522 and stale-cache behaviour written up below **cannot happen on this domain**, and the **100 MB Cloudflare request-body cap does not apply**, which is the thing that had blocked 120 MB registrar uploads. What is given up is CDN caching and a hidden origin IP. ~~Note the asymmetry while both domains are live: a request to `newipo.finwave.co` is still a Cloudflare request~~ **FALSE AS OF 2026-11-02 — `newipo.finwave.co` AND `newipoapi.finwave.co` BOTH RESOLVE STRAIGHT TO 103.107.27.38 TOO**, so the cached-522 / stale-cache / 100 MB-body analysis below applies to only ONE live host. **CORRECTED SAME DAY: I first wrote that NOTHING here is Cloudflare-fronted and that was an overreach — `console.finwave.co` resolves to 104.21.20.144 / 172.67.192.248, Cloudflare anycast, and this box serves it.** So that reasoning is live for exactly that host and dead for the other five. See the certificate section near the end of this file; **re-check which hosts are proxied before reasoning from any of it.**
  - **There was NO TLS certificate for `investoyard.com` on this box** (only `*.finwave.co`), and HTTPS cannot be bound without one — the first SNI test to the new host returned a **404 from the default site**, not the IPO home page, because nothing was bound for it. `api.investoyard.com` also resolves to **103.50.163.230, which is not this server**, with no wildcard behind it; that record is wrong rather than absent, so it must be repointed, not merely created, if the API ever moves.
  - **THE DEPLOY IS NOT INVISIBLE TO VISITORS, and "instant" was the wrong word** (diagnosed 2026-10-09 after hitting it twice in one afternoon). A build rewrites all ~2,596 pages plus every hashed asset in `out/`; a request landing in that window stalls, **Cloudflare records a 522 — and then SERVES THAT CACHED 522 for that URL after the origin has fully recovered.** Proof: the origin answered `/subscription/v2/` in **140 ms** while Cloudflare returned 522 for `?symbol=SAIURJA`, and the identical URL with a cache-buster (`&cb=1`) loaded instantly. **A 522 that survives a healthy origin is a CACHED 522, not a cold start** — append a query parameter to tell the two apart. **BUT THE QUERY-PARAMETER TEST FAILED ON 2026-10-14 AND THE REASON MATTERS: this zone's cache key IGNORES the query string.** A brand-new `/contact/` 522'd, `?cb=…` 522'd identically, and **143 ms is far too fast for a connection timeout** — which is itself the tell. **The discriminator that WORKED is a different PATH to the same file**: `/contact/index.html` served 200 while `/contact/` 522'd. Related and worth knowing before anyone panics after a deploy: **a first-ever request to a cold path is slow enough to cause this by itself** — `/CONTACT/` measured **19.6 s** on its first hit — so a new route will 522 briefly and clear on its own, which it did. This is a better explanation of the operator's historical intermittent 522s than the idle timeout alone, and it is unresolved: the idle-timeout fix below does not touch it.
  - **THE DOMINANT CAUSE OF THE INTERMITTENT 522s IS AN IIS/CLOUDFLARE KEEP-ALIVE RACE, AND IT IS A THIRD CAUSE — not the cold start, not the cached 522** (diagnosed 2026-10-12 from an operator report that it happens on EVERY site on this box, on any click). **IIS/http.sys closes an idle keep-alive connection at `webLimits/connectionTimeout`, default 120 s; Cloudflare holds origin connections for reuse for 900 s.** So Cloudflare routinely sends a request down a socket IIS shut minutes earlier, gets nothing, waits, and returns 522.
    - **THE TELL IS A GAP IN THE IIS LOG ACROSS THE ERROR INSTANT.** At 10:25:38 UTC the static log ran `10:24:44 /admin/catalog/edit/ 200` then jumped to `10:25:57`, and the API log `10:24:07` → `10:25:57` — **the request never arrived at either site.** The identical URL then served in 33 ms. **Same URL working, failing, working is what separates this from a CACHED 522**, which keeps failing on that URL until a cache-buster; and the spinner-then-error delay is Cloudflare waiting on a dead socket, where a cached 522 returns instantly.
    - **Measured: 832 `Timer_ConnectionIdle` closures in 10 hours, 774 of them from Cloudflare ranges**, steady at 25–94/hour. No queue-full, no app-pool fault, no port exhaustion (162 established, 17 TIME_WAIT).
    - **IT IS A SERVER FAULT, NOT AN INVESTOYARD ONE**, which is why it follows the operator across sites: **six of the seven active sites are 98–100% Cloudflare-fronted** and share ONE http.sys connection pool and ONE timeout. W3SVC1 is the only site not behind Cloudflare and is the only one that cannot show a 522 at all. **One elevated command fixes every site**: `appcmd.exe set config /section:system.applicationHost/webLimits /connectionTimeout:00:20:00` — 20 min beats Cloudflare's 15, so Cloudflare always closes first.
    - **DO NOT diagnose this from `127.0.0.1`, and do not trust a probe to the public IP either.** The origin answers every admin endpoint in 26–162 ms including the 1.2 MB `/ipos?all=1`, so local curl says "healthy" while users get 522s. And this box sits behind NAT at **192.168.46.88** — the public `103.107.27.38` is NOT bound locally, so curling it from here fails on **hairpinning** and looks exactly like a dead origin. **The evidence that settles it is the IIS log gap, not a reachability test.**
    - **`scripts/check-origin-connections.ps1` + the `Investoyard-OriginConnectionCheck` scheduled task** (daily 09:00, runs as `finwave`) watch for the setting being lost — an IIS repair or config restore putting it back to 00:02:00. Appends one row per day to `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\origin-connection-health.csv`, verdict HIGH over **100** closures. **The threshold is absolute rather than a rate because the fix near-ELIMINATES the closures rather than reducing them** — pre-fix days ran 75 to 1,547 purely tracking traffic (weekends lowest), so a percentage would have been noise, while post-fix should be near zero. Same shape as watching for a WAS 5186 after the app-pool idle fix.
- **API**: https://newipoapi.finwave.co/api — NestJS via **iisnode** from `apps/api/dist`. Deploy needs the **app-pool handshake** (see below).
- **IIS app pools — the names are NOT what you expect.** `Investoyard` serves the **STATIC SITE**; `InvestoyardAPI` serves the API. Read that twice before changing a setting: the pool called "Investoyard" is the one that has nothing to do with `apps/api`. `appcmd` needs an **ELEVATED** shell (`list` returns Access Denied otherwise), and `applicationHost.config` is unreadable without it too — the System event log is the way to see pool behaviour from a normal shell.
  - **Both pools ran with IIS's default 20-minute IDLE TIMEOUT until 2026-09-24, and it caused two separate faults.** On the static pool it produced the operator's **522s**: after an idle shutdown the next visitor paid a cold start measured at **10.6 s** (4–129 ms once warm), and a low-traffic site makes most visitors that first request. On the API pool it was worse than slow — **the subscription poller lives inside that Node process, and IIS idle timeout counts REQUESTS, not activity**, so a `setInterval` does not keep the worker alive. `InvestoyardAPI` idled at 07:42 on 23-09 and stayed dead through the 10:00 window opening; nothing was polled until a visitor happened to wake it. **A background job cannot live in a pool IIS is allowed to shut down.**
  - **Both now run `idleTimeout:00:00:00` + `startMode:AlwaysRunning`**, with the rolling 29-hour recycle replaced by a fixed **04:00** schedule (`recycling.periodicRestart.time:00:00:00` + a `schedule.[value='04:00:00']` entry) — six hours clear of the poll window, and on the static pool it stops a random-hour recycle handing someone that 10.6 s again. `logEventOnProcessModel:"IdleTimeout"` is on, so a regression shows up in the System log as a WAS **5186**.
- **DB/Redis**: portable Postgres :5433 + Redis :6379 — **NO Docker**. After any reboot: `scripts\db.ps1 start` + `scripts\redis.ps1 start` (or `scripts\start-services.bat`). A downed DB looks like a CORS/500. **Both can be registered as Windows services so neither needs the reboot ritual — see below.**
  - **REDIS CAN NOW BE REGISTERED AS A SERVICE TOO — `scripts\redis.ps1 register` (ELEVATED), service name `investoyard-redis`** (added 2026-11-06, after Redis went down and took the OTP store with it; same shape as `investoyard-postgres` and for the same reason — a console-started process dies with its console and does not come back after a reboot). `redis.ps1` is service-aware the way `db.ps1` is: `start|stop|status|register|unregister`, driving the service when one exists and the binary directly when it does not, so it behaves the same either side of registration and `start-services.bat` needed no change. Automatic start plus restart-on-failure 5s/10s/30s, and `register` **checks elevation BEFORE touching anything**, so a refusal costs nothing — verified: no conf written, no service created, Redis still answering.
    - **LIVENESS IS `redis-cli ping`, NEVER THE PORT AND NEVER THE SERVICE STATE.** An orphaned process keeps the listening socket, so an open port is what a wedged Redis looks like — the same fault that had `start-services.bat` reporting Postgres UP while it refused every query. And a service can report **Running** over a Redis that answers nothing, which is the `pg_ctl status` lie in different clothes; `status` names that state explicitly rather than trusting `Get-Service`.
    - **THE SERVICE RUNS AS `NT AUTHORITY\NetworkService`, not LocalSystem**, and the port's own installer grants it read/write on the data directory. That is lower-privilege than Postgres gets — and correctly so: Postgres is safe as LocalSystem only because `pg_ctl` spawns it with a RESTRICTED token and it aborts if it holds administrator rights. Redis has no such self-protection.
    - **THE CONF IS GENERATED FROM THE SAME `$port` / `$data` THE CONSOLE PATH PASSES AS FLAGS**, into `.redisdata\redis-service.conf`. Not the shipped `redis.windows-service.conf`, which enables RDB snapshots: this Redis is a pure cache (`--save ""`, `--appendonly no`) and registering it must not quietly turn it into a store somebody then has to back up. One source for both start paths, so they cannot drift about the port, the directory or persistence.
    - **A BACKSLASH IN A QUOTED CONF VALUE IS EATEN, AND IT STOPS REDIS STARTING.** Redis processes escapes inside double quotes and knows neither `\U` nor `\f`, so `dir "D:\Investoyard\.redisdata"` arrives as `D:Investoyard.redisdata` — measured, as *"Can't chdir to 'C:UsersfinwaveAppData…'"*. **The conf is written with FORWARD SLASHES**, which Windows accepts in a path and Redis passes through. Same family as the four shell-eats-a-backslash traps already in this file, in a new place.
    - **THERE IS DELIBERATELY NO `bind` LINE, and adding one would refuse the API's own connection.** The console start passes no bind, so Redis listens on `0.0.0.0` **and** `[::]` — and the API connects over **IPv6 loopback**: `netstat` shows an ESTABLISHED `[::1]:6379`, because `localhost` resolves to `::1` first on this box. That is the same resolver order that made a web build fetch a different application on port 3000. `bind 127.0.0.1` would therefore break the one client Redis exists for, and Redis 5 has no optional-bind syntax (`-::1` is 6+), so naming both stacks would make it refuse to start on a host without IPv6. `protected-mode yes` is what limits exposure. **Tightening it to loopback-only is a security decision for the operator, not a side effect of registering a service.**
    - **`-Encoding utf8` WOULD BREAK THE CONF**: PowerShell 5.1 writes a BOM, and Redis reads those three bytes as part of the first directive. Written `-Encoding ascii`; a test asserts the file's first byte is `#`.
    - **`sc` IS POWERSHELL'S ALIAS FOR `Set-Content`** — the restart-on-failure line must call `sc.exe`, and the spaces after `reset=` and `actions=` are required by its parser.
    - **The handover drops the keyspace and `register` says so before doing it**, naming the key count: there is no persistence, so the BullMQ queue metadata is recreated on the API's next connection, an unconsumed OTP is lost (the investor asks for another) and the visitor salt rotates early. Nothing that needs a backup.
    - **PROVED ON A THROWAWAY PORT, NOT BY READING THE CONF.** `scratchpad`-style probe, 10 checks: no BOM, Redis starts from the file, `save` resolves EMPTY, `appendonly no`, port and dir taken from the conf, set/get with expiry, **answers on `[::1]`**, nothing persisted to disk, and the live instance on 6379 untouched. **Its first two runs failed and both were the harness** — a "sleep" that spawned `node -e "setTimeout(()=>{},500)"` and so never waited at all, and then the backslash fault above. **A conf that looks obviously right is exactly the thing to start once on a spare port.**
  - **Postgres RUNS AS A WINDOWS SERVICE** — `investoyard-postgres`, LocalSystem, auto-start, restart-on-failure 5s/10s/30s (registered 2026-09-23). It has no console, so the Ctrl+C that killed it three times that day cannot reach it, and it comes back after a reboot on its own. `db.ps1` is service-aware (`start|stop|status|register|unregister`): it drives the service when one exists and falls back to `pg_ctl` when it doesn't, so it behaves the same either side of registration. **`register`/`unregister`/`Restart-Service` need an ELEVATED shell** (SeCreateServicePrivilege); `register` checks that BEFORE stopping anything, so a refusal costs nothing. LocalSystem is safe because `pg_ctl` spawns postgres with a RESTRICTED token — Postgres aborts if it holds administrator rights, which is also why this goes through `pg_ctl register` and not `sc create`.
  - **Under the service, `pg_ctl status` LIES — use `pg_isready`.** The postmaster runs as LocalSystem and a standard user cannot open a handle to it, so `pg_ctl status` reports *"no server running"* for a database answering every query. `db.ps1` briefly announced ZOMBIE over a healthy service because of it. Liveness is now defined as **accepting connections** everywhere it matters, and that check is the FIRST guard in `Clear-PgZombie` — a zombie is by definition a server that does not accept connections, so a healthy service can never match whatever `pg_ctl` thinks. (`pg_isready` reports *accepting connections* even when auth fails — it connects as the OS user, so `role "finwave" does not exist` in the log is that probe, and is exactly the answer a liveness check wants.)
  - **Server logs moved to `.pgdata\log\postgresql-YYYY-MM-DD.log`** via `logging_collector = on` (2026-09-23; daily / 50 MB rotation, settings appended at the end of `postgresql.conf`, backup `postgresql.conf.bak-20260923`). **`.pgdata\log.txt` is FROZEN** at the last console-started server's shutdown — do not read it for anything recent. A service sends stderr to the Windows event log, where every line renders as *"The description for Event ID '0' in Source 'PostgreSQL' cannot be found"* because `pgevent.dll` is not registered; the collector sidesteps that and survives re-registration. `log_min_messages` is `warning`, which still captures crashes — **LOG outranks WARNING** in the server-log ordering.
  - **A crashed Postgres leaves a ZOMBIE**, and `db.ps1 start` now clears it automatically (2026-09-23). Symptom set: `pg_ctl status` says **"no server running"**, yet a `postgres` process is alive, **5433 is still LISTENING**, and `start` warns *"another server might be running"* then fails with *"could not open log file … Permission denied"* — the orphan holds both the socket and the log. `Clear-PgZombie` acts only on that exact three-way combination (no postmaster **and** orphan processes **and** the port held); anything looser would be killing a healthy server, and it removes `postmaster.pid` only once both are provably gone. WAL replay on the next start is the normal, non-destructive recovery; nothing committed is lost.
  - **Check Postgres by CONNECTING, never by probing :5433.** An orphaned backend keeps the listening socket, so an open port is exactly what a crashed server looks like — `start-services.bat` used to test the port and reported **UP** for a database refusing every query, while also telling the operator that the zombie's warnings meant *"Postgres was ALREADY running - that is fine"*. Both are gone. The check is now `pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1 -p 5433` (expect *accepting connections*) and `redis-cli -p 6379 ping` (expect *PONG*), and the API is the final word: `curl -H 'Host: newipoapi.finwave.co' http://127.0.0.1/api/health` must be **200**, not 503. **THAT CHECK ONLY WORKS WHILE THE IIS BINDINGS ARE "All Unassigned"** - pin one to the LAN address and this returns a 404 from the DEFAULT site while the API serves perfectly over 443, which is exactly how 2026-11-03 opened with a false report that the API was down. A 404 here means *check the bindings*, not *the API is dead*.
  - **Never `npm run build` the web while the API is down** — the static export is generated from the LOCAL live API (`API_INTERNAL_URL=http://localhost:3000/api`, via the Host-header forwarder). Against a dead API it bakes an empty catalog AND collapses every dynamic route to the `no-ipos` placeholder, wiping ~1,000 real IPO pages. The build log naming 1,000 `/ipos/[symbol]` paths is the proof it had real data.
  - **THE PDF GENERATOR NEEDS THE DEV SERVER, AND THE DEV SERVER POISONS THE NEXT PRODUCTION BUILD. THE ORDER IS FIXED: render the PDFs → STOP the dev server → clear `.next` → build.** `next dev` leaves **DIRECTORIES** named `ARMOUR.html` under `.next/server/app/apply/`, and the export then fails `EISDIR: illegal operation on a directory` on every one of them — 2,601 pages generate, the export dies, and `out/` is left EMPTY. Clearing `.next` and starting the dev server again just recreates them, which is how this cost two builds in one afternoon.
    - **Clearing `.next` also drops Next's FONT cache, and the next build can fail transiently on `next/font`** (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading '1')` in `google/loader.js`). It is not a code fault and not a Google change — every font URL was verified to still end `.woff2`. **Retry the build**; it succeeded first retry both times.
    - **`/api/ipos` has crossed Next's 2 MB fetch-cache ceiling** (2,113,173 bytes) and now logs *"items over 2MB can not be cached"* on every build. Harmless today — it is a warning, the build completes — but it means the catalogue fetch is re-requested rather than cached, and it was invisible until `.next` was cleared for the first time in months.
  - **A build that ran while the DB was dying POISONS `.next/cache`, and plain rebuilds replay it.** `safeGet()` uses a cacheable `fetch` (required by `output: export`), so a failed response is cached like any other and **two clean rebuilds against a healthy API reproduced the damage exactly** (2026-09-23). The cure is to clear the fetch cache before rebuilding — the comment on `safeGet` says so, and it is easy to read past. **Clear `apps\web\.next\cache\fetch-cache` only** (~3 MB), not all of `.next\cache`: the sibling `webpack` folder is ~180 MB of compilation cache and nuking it costs minutes for nothing. **Any rebuild meant to pick up changed DB data needs this** — not just one after an outage.
  - **The failure is PARTIAL and silent, which is what makes it dangerous.** The build does not fail: `safeGet` swallows the error and returns its fallback, so the log still prints ~1,000 routes and looks perfect while individual pages lose whole sections. On 2026-09-23 four IPOs that were OPEN shipped detail pages with no Live Subscription section at all. **Route count is not proof of a good build** — spot-check a page's content: `grep -c timesSubscribed apps/web/out/ipos/<slug>/index.html` should be non-zero for anything currently subscribed.
- **Mobile**: Expo **SDK 58** (react-native 0.88, react 19.3 — **this line said SDK 54 until 2026-11-04 and every version figure in it was two majors stale; the Expo Go narrative below predates the upgrade and needs re-checking, not following** — see *Phase 0 of the Claude Design handoff*) — ~~Play-Store Expo Go works~~ **IT NO LONGER DOES, AND IT WAS ONLY EVER GOING TO BE TEMPORARY** (2026-10-23). **Expo Go on the store carries ONLY THE NEWEST SDK**, so it moved to 57 while this project was pinned to 54 AT THE TIME, and the app refuses to open with a version mismatch. **A note that a store app "works" is a note with an expiry date nobody wrote down.** Install the matching build from `https://expo.dev/go?sdkVersion=54&platform=android&device=true` (SDK 54 is Expo Go **54.0.8**, still published) and turn OFF auto-update for it — otherwise the store replaces it again. `scripts/start-mobile.ps1` prints the project SDK and that link on every start. **The lasting fix is a development build**, which pins the SDK and makes the Expo Go version irrelevant; it is the EAS item already listed under Open items, and this is what makes it urgent rather than tidy. Dev: `cd apps\mobile; $env:REACT_NATIVE_PACKAGER_HOSTNAME='103.107.27.38'; $env:EXPO_OFFLINE='1'; npx expo start --clear`.

### API deploy handshake (CRITICAL)
iisnode locks `dist` + the Prisma engine DLL while the pool runs. **User** stops the pool → then:
`npx prisma generate` (only if schema changed) → `npx tsc -p tsconfig.json --outDir dist` → **user** starts the pool.
- `npx prisma db push --skip-generate` is SAFE while the pool runs; `prisma generate` is NOT.
- New-Prisma-model code shows "property does not exist on PrismaService" typecheck errors until the handshake — expected, not a bug.
- **NEVER run root `npm run build` (turbo) while the pool runs** — nest's clean step guts `dist` and causes a delayed outage. Build workspaces individually.
- **The PII VAULT KEY is the single point of total data loss. It lives at `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\pii-vault.key`** (since 2026-09-28) — **outside** the build output, because it used to default to `process.cwd()/.pii-vault.key` and under iisnode that is `apps/api/dist`. One `rm -rf dist` deleted it and the app silently minted a replacement, came up healthy, and re-keyed the vault under everyone: the SMTP password, the Anthropic key and the exchange rail credentials all stopped resolving, which is why the subscription poller died with an authentication error.
  - **The key existed in FOUR places and only one of them mattered — check them all before declaring loss.** A first pass concluded 605 investor PAN tokens were gone. They were not: **`D:\Investoyard\.pii-vault.key` (Aug 29) is the original**, left behind by a run whose cwd was the repo root, and it decrypts **601 of 631** tokens and reproduces their stored `panHash` **397/397 on sample**. `apps/api/.pii-vault.key` (Jul 28) decrypts nothing. The minted `dist` key covers the ~26 written after the incident. Three profiles (ASH PATEL, ABHI PATEL, HIRAL SONI, all created in early August) open under no key at all and were **already broken before this** — unrelated. The lesson is the diagnosis, not the deletion: **`cwd` moves between deployments, so a cwd-relative secret scatters copies across every directory the app was ever started from.** Enumerate them and test each against real ciphertext before telling anyone data is lost.
  - **THREE distinct keys exist on this box, and only one decrypts anything.** Audited 2026-09-28 — do not re-derive this: **#1 `D:\Investoyard\.pii-vault.key` (Aug 29) is the ORIGINAL** and is what `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\pii-vault.key` now holds · **#2 `apps\api\.pii-vault.key` (Jul 28) decrypts NOTHING** · **#3 `apps\api\dist\.pii-vault.key` (Sep 28)** was minted during the incident and covers only what was written after it. A key file is 64 bytes and they are indistinguishable by eye — **fingerprint them (`sha256` of the trimmed contents) rather than guessing from a filename or a date.**
  - **A backup named after a FILE is wrong the moment that file's contents change.** `pii-vault.key.backup-20260928-145551` was taken before the primary was swapped, so it promised a copy of `pii-vault.key` while holding key #3 — restoring it in an emergency would have re-broken exactly what the swap fixed. Deleted; its bytes survive as `archive-minted-sep28.key`, and the live backup is **`BACKUP-of-primary-original-aug29.key`**, verified identical to the primary. **Name a key backup after the KEY, never after the path it happened to sit at.**
  - **`find /` in Git Bash does NOT span Windows drives** — a search for stray key copies returned empty because `/` is the Git install root, not `D:`. It looked like proof that no other copy existed; there were five. Use PowerShell (`Get-ChildItem -Recurse`) with explicit roots for anything filesystem-wide on this box, and **never read an empty result from a search you have not confirmed can see the data.**
  - **`C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\` holds the primary, a correctly-named backup, and an archive of every key found** (`archive-original-aug29.key`, `archive-appsapi-jul28.key`, `archive-minted-sep28.key`). The primary is the **Aug 29 original**, which is what restored duplicate detection for the 601 — **all of which were deleted later the same day** as test data, so the specific exposure is closed and the rule below is what carries forward.
  - **Key churn is how duplicate profiles got created, and it WILL recur if the key changes again.** `(tenantId, panHash)` is UNIQUE, but the same PAN hashed under a different key yields a different hash, so the index never fires and a second profile is created silently. Eight PANs were each holding two profiles by the time every token could be read under one key. **`InvestorProfile` is empty as of 2026-09-28 but does not stay empty** — `getOrCreateClientProfile()` mints one per (userId, PAN) on every partner print, so the table repopulates from the next print onwards and all of this applies to the new rows. `scripts/restore-pan-tokens.js` (dry-run by default) reports stale hashes and collisions; it has nothing to act on today.
  - **Minting is now OPT-IN.** A missing key file makes the app **REFUSE TO START** unless `PII_VAULT_ALLOW_MINT=true`. Silent minting is what turned a deleted file into unrecoverable data — the process looked healthy while re-keying the vault under everyone. A missing key is either a first install, which an operator can declare once, or an emergency that should stop the process. Both branches are tested: missing + not allowed → refuses with the reason; key present → starts and mints nothing.
  - **The legacy `cwd/.pii-vault.key` path is still READ and still tried on DECRYPT**, so an untouched deployment keeps working and its key can be migrated; it logs a warning naming both paths. `apps/api/.pii-vault.key` (dated Jul 28) is a **stale artefact from a run with a different cwd — it decrypts nothing and is NOT a backup.**
  - **`panHash` is derived from the master key** (`createHmac('sha256', masters[0])`), so a re-key silently breaks duplicate detection: the same PAN fingerprints differently and never matches a stored profile. The **self-PAN rule — one public application per PAN per IPO — stops being enforced against every pre-existing profile, with no error anywhere.** That is the most dangerous consequence of losing this key, well beyond the credentials: a lost credential announces itself, this does not. It is why the key now has an archive and why minting is opt-in.
  - **Back it up OFF this machine.** A dated copy sits beside it (`pii-vault.key.backup-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS`), which survives a bad command but not a lost disk.
  - **THE NSE RAIL CREDENTIAL IS SEALED UNDER KEY #3 — THE ONE MINTED DURING THE INCIDENT — AND DECRYPTS ONLY BECAUSE THAT STRAY FILE SITS INSIDE `dist`** (found 2026-10-13 while probing the live catwise feed). A probe run from the repo root and from `apps/api` both died with `Unsupported state or unable to authenticate data`; the identical probe with `cwd = apps/api/dist` opened it first time. **The live poller works for exactly that reason and no other** — iisnode's cwd is `apps/api/dist`, so `apps/api/dist/.pii-vault.key` joins the master list as a legacy fallback. So the boot-test warning documented below as a mere ARTEFACT has a sharper edge than that note implies: **a clean rebuild that removes `dist/.pii-vault.key` would break the poller's EXCHANGE AUTHENTICATION, not just lose the upload store.** Re-saving the credential in admin → Exchange Rails re-seals it under the current primary and removes the dependency — **one operator action, and it has not been done.**
  - **A cwd-relative secret makes the WORKING DIRECTORY part of your auth story.** Three keys, three cwds, three different answers to "can this credential be opened" — and the one that works is the one nothing documents as load-bearing. When a decrypt fails, test every cwd before concluding the ciphertext is lost.
- **`dist` IS NOT A DISPOSABLE BUILD DIRECTORY — it holds LIVE OPERATOR DATA.** `UPLOAD_DIR` defaults to `join(process.cwd(), 'uploads')` (`upload.module.ts`) and under iisnode `process.cwd()` is the entry script's folder, so every DRHP, ASBA template, logo, registrar file and allotment archive lives in **`apps/api/dist/uploads`** — 107 entries as of 2026-09-28, plus `allotment-archive`, `ipo-import` and `catalog-update`. **Never `rm -rf dist`.** Compile straight over it with `tsc --outDir dist`; tsc overwrites what it emits and leaves everything else alone. Attempted on 2026-09-28 and only the IIS worker's file ownership stopped it — every `uploads/` and `iisnode/` path returned *Permission denied* while the compiled JS, which the operator DOES own, was deleted. Recovery was one `tsc` away, but with looser permissions that command destroys the upload store. It is also the real reason the turbo-build warning above matters: nest's clean step targets the same directory.
- Seed is CLEAN-SLATE — never run against prod. Use `npm run ensure-roles` for additive role changes.
- **ALWAYS boot-test before handing the build back**: **`SUBSCRIPTION_POLL_DISABLED=true ALERTS_DISABLED=true PORT=3999 node dist/main.js`** and look for "Nest application successfully started". `tsc` passing proves NOTHING about dependency injection
  - **Set those two env flags, and that is not tidiness** (learned 2026-10-07). Without them the boot test starts a SECOND subscription poller against the production database — benign only while the pool is stopped, and the `dist-check` pattern boot-tests while the pool is UP, which during market hours means two pollers doing `deleteMany` + `createMany` on the same rows.
  - **It also logs `[SubscriptionPoll] Unsupported state or unable to authenticate data` every 10 s, and that is a BOOT-TEST ARTEFACT, not a fault.** It is Node's GCM auth-tag failure: the process's cwd is `apps/api`, so the legacy vault path resolves to `apps/api/.pii-vault.key` — **key #2, the one that decrypts nothing** — and the exchange rail credential will not open. **The live poller is unaffected**; the way to prove that is a recent `subscriptionAsOf`, not the boot log (RKFAL was polled three minutes before the test that produced this warning). So the boot test **cannot exercise the rail-credential path at all** — it proves DI and routing, which is what it is for.
  - **Grep the boot output for the success line specifically, not `head -5`.** Redis's *"highly recommended to use a minimum Redis version of 6.2.0"* notices print between the last route and the success line and pushed it out of view once, which read as a failed boot. — a controller injecting a service whose module wasn't imported fails at BOOT and takes down **every** endpoint with a 500, not just the new route. That happened (`TemplateTestController` → `WhatsappService`, `WhatsappModule` not imported; it is not `@Global`).
- **`web.config` has `loggingEnabled="false"` — there are no iisnode log files.** To see why the API is failing, run the compiled app directly; the Nest error prints in one shot.
- To prove a fix **while the pool is still up**, compile to a scratch dir INSIDE the repo (`--outDir dist-check`, so `node_modules` still resolves), boot-test that, then delete it. `dist` is locked while iisnode runs.

## What's built (beyond the original slice)
- **Apply system**: shared **bid engine** (`packages/shared-types/bidEngine.ts` — presets, custom-by-₹Cr, category/caps as rule inputs incl. admin-configurable `upiCap`); UPI apply flow (Retail | HNI | Shareholder tabs, per-family-member overrides, single itemized consent); separate **Print Forms** flow; per-IPO operator gates **Start Bid / Start Print**; PAN-duplication per bucket (public vs shareholder/employee).
- **Multiple demat accounts per applicant** (2026-10-08, sir's requirement 4): one PAN may hold several demats and the investor picks which one an allotment credits to, at apply time. New `DematAccount` table (`@@unique([profileId, depository, dpId, clientId])`) + nullable `Application.dematAccountId`. **`packages/shared-types/src/demat.ts` is the ONE resolver** — `dematAccountsFor()` (profile's own columns = the DEFAULT, always first, then the extras), `dematForApplication()`, `dematLabel()`; 19 tests.
  - **PRESENCE IS `clientId` ALONE — a CDSL account has NO DP ID** and stores `dpId: ''`. The first cut tested `dpId && clientId`, which **dropped every CDSL account**, so a CDSL profile resolved to no demat at all and the rail builder would have thrown. Latent only because all 230 profiles are NSDL. This rule is written in three places in the codebase and is the single easiest thing to get wrong here.
  - **An unknown or retired `dematAccountId` resolves to `null`, NOT to the default.** Silently falling back would send the exchange a bid crediting a demat the investor did not choose. `bid-operations.service.ts` **throws** instead: *"the chosen demat account could not be resolved"*.
  - **`onDelete: Restrict` and RETIRE (`active: false`), never delete** — a submitted bid names its demat to the exchange, so losing which one it was would rewrite history. A typo is retired and re-added; there is deliberately **no EDIT** in either UI.
  - **It does NOT let anyone apply twice**: the self-PAN rule is keyed on `panHash`, never on a demat. Both UIs say so in that wording.
  - **The picker only appears when the applicant holds more than one** — one account is not a choice. Web is a `<select>` in `ApplyWizard`; mobile is a *"Credit shares to"* chip row (no native select). `DematPanel` exists twice — `apps/web/components/Account.tsx` and `apps/mobile/components/DematPanel.tsx` — **keep them in step by hand**, same standing rule as the split UPI input.
  - `createBulkApplication` is wired with the same ownership guard but **nothing calls it from either UI** today.
- **Print engine**: fillable-**AcroForm** filling by field name (MANIPAL-style blanks: `SYMBOL.pdf`/`_SA`/`_SHA`, duplicate-named counterfoil fields, maxLength truncation, amount-in-words) with coordinate-overlay fallback; form numbers from per-IPO PDF series. **Print = no business validation** (operator policy); rail applications keep all SEBI checks.
- **Partner API (M2)**: `POST /partner/v1/print-forms` — per-tenant API keys (`X-Api-Key keyId.secret`), partner sends FINAL data printed verbatim, PII vaulted per tenant, same PDF series; admin: key panel on tenant page, **API Docs / API Calls / Print Report (CSV)** under "Partner API". **Records-only channel** — the profile is created once per PAN then never touched (2026-09-16 operator fix, replacing an `upsertClientProfile` that overwrote the shared row on every call). Each print's exact received payload is stored on `Application.partnerPayload` (JSON, immutable); the PDF is filled from THAT payload — not from the shared profile — so a repeat print for the same PAN with corrected details still produces a correct PDF without rewriting what earlier prints were based on. `ApplicationsService.buildAsbaFromPayload` is the shared per-applicant filler (AcroForm → overlay → placeholder), used by both `generatePartnerFormsPdf` (real prints, allocates form no from the IPO series) and `previewFormsPdf` (`formNo:"TEST"`).
- **Partner API — Docs Test button**: `POST /admin/partner-api/test-print` — the docs page (`/admin/partner-api/docs`) has an inline tester. IPO dropdown (filtered to `startPrint:true`) + editable applicants JSON + Run → dry-run through the real print engine using the operator's SESSION (no API key needed, nothing persisted, no form numbers consumed). Response viewer + Download PDF so the operator sees the exact template the partner would receive. Docs also list the six fields the DTO always accepted but were missing (address, city, state, pincode, email, branchName) and the footnote that `ifsc`/`upiId` are now silently stripped by class-validator's whitelist.
- **Partner API — View modal on Print report**: `GET /admin/partner-api/print-detail?id=<uuid>` — click the eye icon on any row in the Print report to open a modal reconstructing the exact request received: Applicant / Demat / Bank / Bid / Meta sections, PAN masked (`ABCDE**34F`), bank account shown as `•••• 4567`. Superadmin only sees Batch id + Application ref (tenant admins see the request-side data only). Reads from `partnerPayload` when present; legacy rows (created before the snapshot column existed) fall back to the shared profile with a small "Legacy print" note. **URL is `print-detail?id=…`, NOT `prints/<uuid>`** — the UUID-in-path pattern under `/prints/` was intercepted upstream by Cloudflare/WAF and returned 404 before reaching iisnode; the literal path + query-string ID sidesteps that.
- **Partner API — Report UI**: `apps/web/app/admin/partner-api/{calls,prints,keys}` all follow the IPO catalog pattern (toolbar outside the card, `.card-head` with `.count-badge`, segmented time range `7 / 30 / 90 / 365`). Status chips use the compact `.st` family (11.5px, 3×10px padding) — not `.pill` (which is a 14px button style — reads as an oversized button inside a table row, and the report pages used to). Added `.st.bad` (neg-soft / neg) and `.st.lock` (indigo + lock icon) to globals.css. When a partner login views these pages, the tenant dropdown is replaced by a **locked scope chip** (`Scope: <PartnerName>`) using `.st.lock` — same info the old hidden control carried, now visible.
- **A partner print is INDEPENDENT of the client profile it came from** (2026-09-28). `Application.investorProfileId` is **nullable with `ON DELETE SET NULL`**, every print carries its own `partnerPayload`, and `printsReport` / `printDetail` / the CSV all read the **payload first** with the profile as fallback only. Before this, `printsReport` read `r.profile.fullName` and `r.profile.panTokenRef` DIRECTLY — so clearing out test clients would have blanked all 1,328 print rows, not just the old ones, and the FK (`NOT NULL` + `RESTRICT`) refused the delete anyway. **All 633 client profiles and the 3 non-print applications were deleted; 1,328 prints survive, fully detached.**
  - **103 legacy prints predated the payload column** and were the reason the profiles were load-bearing. `scripts/backfill-legacy-print-payloads.js` reconstructed them from profile + application, tagged `_reconstructed` so a rebuild is never mistaken for what a partner actually sent.
  - **Making the relation optional found five call sites that assumed a profile exists** — allotment matching (skips), the consumer application list (empty name), and the rail bid builder, which now **throws**: a bid needs the applicant's PAN, depository and DP ID, and those are not in a print payload. Failing loudly beats sending the exchange an incomplete bid.
  - **Select JSON-null columns in RAW SQL.** Prisma reads a `Json` field's `null` as JSON-null, not SQL NULL, so `{ partnerPayload: { equals: null } }` matched **zero** of the 103 rows and the first dry run cheerfully reported nothing to do.
  - Backups of the pre-delete tables: `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\backups\`.
- **Partner API — Calls carries FOUR summary tabs** (operator ask, 2026-09-28): `Calls` (the log) · `By partner` · `By day` · `By month` · `By IPO`, over `GET /admin/partner-api/calls-summary?by=…` with a CSV per grouping. A **`GROUP BY` in the database**, never row-pulling — the window reaches 365 days and the table only grows. Partner filter and time range are shared across tabs, and only the open tab fetches.
  - **Day and month bucket in IST** (`at AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata'`). A day boundary has to be the OPERATOR's day; grouping the raw timestamp files a 3 a.m. call under the previous date and rolls a month over 5½ hours early. Only safe because those columns became `timestamptz` the same week.
  - **Labels come from the owning tables, never the call row** — a `tenantId` is a uuid and `ipoSymbol` is stored as the partner sent it. An IPO row with no name is one we do not carry and renders **"not in catalogue"**, which is itself the diagnosis for every failure on it.
    - **…and the By-partner tab showed the uuid anyway until 2026-09-30, because only the API half was done.** `tenantNames` had always resolved the name; the table rendered `r.key` in the first column and showed `label` only as a SECOND column gated on `by === 'ipo'`. By day and month the key IS the label (a date) and by IPO the label has its own column, so **partner was the single grouping where the key is unreadable** — and the one nobody checked. **A resolver is only half a fix; look at what the column actually prints.** The uuid is now the `title`.
  - **The Partner tab hides itself for a partner login**: `scoped` means every row is theirs, so the grouping would restate the total already in the card head.
  - **The Total row took THREE goes, and every one was a CSS scoping fault** (2026-09-30 → 2026-10-01). First `resv-total` was borrowed for it — a class scoped to `.resv-table`, so **it matched nothing** and the row rendered as an ordinary body row. Then `.tbl-total` copied `resv-total`'s border and background but **not its padding**, and `.table tbody td { padding: 11px 18px }` is scoped to **tbody** — so a `tfoot td` inherits none of it: "Total" sat left of the partner name, the figures pushed right of the body columns, and the last two cells collided as `102 ms1/10/2026, 11:15:57 am`. The rule now mirrors `tbody td` explicitly. **A total row lives in `tfoot`; grep whether the table's own padding rule can even reach it.**
    - **`FailPct` was extracted at the same time** because the body rounded to whole numbers above 10% while the Total called `toFixed(1)` — the same 228/1,842 printed as *12%* in one place and *12.4%* in the other. **One renderer, or two renderers will disagree.**
    - Verification note worth keeping: a first check grepped the FIRST match across all of `out/` and reported the padding missing. **`out/` keeps stale hashed assets** — check the file the page actually references, not whichever one matches first.
  - **Profiling the data first changed the design.** Of 1,486 calls, **215 had failed and they were concentrated, not scattered** — five symbols failing 100% of the time, and **161 of those only because `startPrint` was off**. That is why the failure count and rate sit beside the volume rather than behind a drill-down. **`endpoint` and `applicants` were deliberately NOT made dimensions**: every row is `print-forms` and every row is 1 applicant, so both would have been columns that only repeat the call count.
- **Partner self-onboarding (M2)**: public `/partner` landing → short `/partner/apply` behind the ordinary OTP session → admin queue **Partners / Branches → Applications** (approve · request changes · reject) → emailed one-time link → `/partner/activate` sets the password. Applications live in their OWN `PartnerApplication` table, never as pending tenants — a half-real tenant would surface in the tenant tree, settings cascade, RLS scoping and reports. Approval calls the existing `AdminService.registerTenant()` (hence `AdminModule` now **exports** `AdminService`), so code/admin-user/role are created exactly as before; approving a **branch** requires a `parentSlug` because registerTenant refuses one without it. **Approval always returns `activationLink` + `emailSent`/`emailDev`** and the review panel shows it with a Copy button — SMTP is configured in admin → System Settings → Provider Keys → Email and when it is absent `EmailService` only LOGS, so without this an approved partner would be silently stranded.
- **White-label published identity (M2, phase 1 of 3)**: a white-label domain used to serve **OUR** identity — the footer said Investoyard, the `Organization` structured data on **every page** declared `legalName: Safal Capital Services Private Limited` with our logo and our url, there was **no contact page at all**, and the footer linked `/partner` (our funnel) and `/admin` (our operator login) from a partner's customer-facing site. Fixed end to end 2026-10-14.
  - **THE ONE RULE: A WHITE-LABEL NEVER INHERITS OUR IDENTITY.** `packages/shared-types/src/tenantContent.ts` is the ONE module (**33 tests**) — `resolveIdentity` substitutes our constants for the **DEFAULT channel alone**; for anyone else the only thing supplied is `tradeName`, from the tenant's own name, which their header already shows. `legalEntity()` returns **undefined** rather than falling back to a brand name, and the footer then prints **nothing**. **A page that says nothing about who runs it is an obvious gap someone fixes; one naming the wrong company is a misstatement nobody notices.**
  - **`contentGaps()` is what insists**, and **BLOCKING is not a severity gradient**: registered company name + grievance officer name + email. Without a legal name the footer has no accountable entity; without a grievance officer a SEBI-registered broker's clients have nowhere to escalate — **and the only alternative on offer would be ours**. Advisory: support email/phone, SEBI registration, registered address. **The admin panel renders the SERVER's gaps**, computed by that same resolver, so it cannot drift from what the footer prints — same reasoning as the subscription readiness card calling `offeredFromIpo()` rather than re-testing its conditions.
  - **`TenantContent` is a ROW PER KEY, never one JSON blob on `Tenant`.** This project has twice paid for two writers racing over one unschema'd JSON column — the IPO form deleting the poller's `extra` keys, and the poller reverting the operator's — and both are the same fault, last write wins the whole object. Separate rows mean saving `contact` cannot touch `identity`, **with no whitelist to keep in step**. Keys today: `identity`, `contact`; the rich-text keys join without a migration.
  - **`mergeContentPatch` — a blanked box DELETES, it does not store `""`.** The form posts every box it renders, so an untouched-empty and a cleared box arrive identically; storing the empty string leaves a tail of `""` that reads as PRESENT to anything checking only for the key. A field the patch does not mention is left alone.
  - **Carried on the PUBLIC `/tenants` registry the site already fetches once on mount** — identity and contact are a handful of short strings, so the footer renders on the first paint with no second request. **The fold DROPS an unrecognised key** rather than spreading it: that endpoint is public and unauthenticated on every white-label domain, and a key some later feature writes must not start appearing there the day it is added (verified by writing an `about` row and confirming it never surfaced). **Rich text deliberately will NOT come this way** — a reader of the home page must not download every partner's legal prose.
  - **THE IDENTITY LINES WAIT FOR THE HOST TO RESOLVE** (`useTenantResolved()` in `TenantProvider`). The static export's first paint is ALWAYS the default tenant, so on a partner's domain *"Operated by Safal Capital Services Private Limited"* would be painted and corrected a tick later. **A brand colour flashing is cosmetic; a legal operator flashing is the exact misstatement the store exists to prevent.** Cost: that one below-the-fold line appears a tick late on our own site too. It always resolves — `fetchTenants` falls back to the built-in registry when the API is unreachable.
  - **The Organization JSON-LD is REWRITTEN IN PLACE, not replaced** (`TenantSchema.tsx`, keyed on `ORG_JSONLD_ID`). The server render stays, because it is correct for our host and our SEO depends on it; on a partner host the client rewrites that one tag. **Rewriting rather than adding a second tag**, so a crawler never sees two Organizations — the wrong one would still be on the page. On our host it resolves to the default and is a **no-op**. `legalName`, `url` and `logo` are **deleted** when the partner has not supplied them, never left at ours.
  - **KNOWN RESIDUE**: our legal name survives ONCE in a partner page, inside Next's **RSC hydration payload** (the serialised props of the server-rendered component). Not visible text, not inside the `application/ld+json` tag — which is correctly rewritten — so a structured-data crawler sees the corrected version. Removing it means giving up our own server-rendered structured data; it should go with the canonical/og pass.
  - **`canonical` and `og:url` are STILL OURS on a partner domain** — baked by `metadataBase` at build time. That is phase 3 and is NOT done; the canonical is the damaging one, since it tells Google a partner's pages are duplicates of ours.
  - **SUPERADMIN ONLY**, matching branding and the custom domain. A partner reads their own block and their own gaps through My Organisation but cannot change them — the legal name, SEBI registration and grievance officer are what the empanelment was approved against, the same reason `updateTenant` freezes PAN/GSTIN/entityType after approval. One line to loosen.
  - **Routes are `PATCH /admin/tenants/:slug/content/{identity,contact}`, ONE PER KEY** so the body is a typed DTO and not an index signature — **class-validator's whitelist is the only thing between a posted key and a public response.** Checked through Cloudflare against the known-good sibling `PATCH /admin/tenants/:slug`: both 401, so no path intercept of the kind that forced `print-detail?id=`.
  - **OUR OWN contact details are NOT entered** (2026-10-14). `/contact` on our domain shows the operating entity and the SEBI/SMART-ODR escalation links only. `noreply@investoyard.com` is the SMTP from-address, **not a support address**, and was deliberately not used. Enter real ones at admin → `/admin/tenants/view?slug=investoyard`.
  - **PHASE 2 IS LIVE.** Written 2026-10-14 and deployed by a later handshake; **confirmed 2026-10-20** — `GET /api/tenants/investoyard/page/terms` and `/about` both answer 200 on the running pool. About · Terms · Privacy · Disclaimer are per-tenant rich text, and **an unsupplied legal page renders "not yet published", NEVER ours**: serving our Terms under a partner's brand, to their clients, is a contract naming the wrong company offered to people who never dealt with us. The three legal pages are **BLOCKING** in the gap list; About is advisory, because a shop window is not a contract.
    - **TWO KEY LISTS, AND THE SPLIT IS LOAD-BEARING.** `TENANT_CONTENT_KEYS` (identity, contact) is what the PUBLIC `/tenants` registry folds on, and that registry is fetched by EVERY page load to resolve the brand. `TENANT_PAGE_KEYS` (the four rich-text pages) is deliberately separate and served one at a time by `GET /tenants/:slug/page/:key` — adding a page key to the first list would make a reader of the home page download every partner's legal prose. Same `TenantContent` table, so no migration.
    - **`hasPage()` EXISTS BECAUSE AN EMPTY TIPTAP DOCUMENT IS `<p></p>`, NOT `''`.** Treating that as published would ship a blank legal page AND clear the gap that should still be open — the worse of the two failures, since nobody is ever prompted about it again.
    - **THE SANITISER MOVED TO `packages/shared-types/src/richText.ts` SO IT COULD BE TESTED** (29 tests). It is injected with `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` on PUBLIC pages, which makes it a security boundary, and **`apps/web` has no test runner at all** — the same reason `offeredFromIpo`, `parseAnchorRows` and `offerTotals` were moved out of the files that hid them. `apps/web/lib/richClean.ts` is now a re-export.
    - **`cleanRichKeepLinks` is a MODE, not a second function.** `cleanRich` strips `<a>` because its callers render pasted merchant-banker prose where a link is junk; a legal page's links are the point — ours carry SEBI SCORES and the ODR portal, and silently deleting a partner's link to their own grievance portal is worse than keeping it. **ALLOW-LIST, never a `javascript:` deny-list**: the tests cover `JaVaScRiPt:`, a leading space, an embedded tab and newline, `data:`, `vbscript:` and the protocol-relative `//evil.test`, which slipped through the first `/` branch until a test caught it. **A DROPPED OPENING TAG MUST DROP ITS CLOSING TAG** — the first version left a bare `</a>` behind, which is why it keeps a stack rather than running two `replace` calls.
    - **THE FIRST CUT GATED THE WHOLE PAGE ON `useTenantResolved()` AND COST OUR OWN SEO.** The built HTML for `/terms` carried **31 characters of markup** — the heading and nothing else — with the entire document sitting only in Next's RSC script payload. **View-source showed an empty legal page.** The fix: the server renders our copy inside `[data-own-only]`, the FIRST client render matches it exactly (or React reports a hydration mismatch), and a partner host swaps it out in an effect.
    - **WHAT STOPS THE FLASH IS AN INLINE SCRIPT IN THE ROOT LAYOUT, NOT REACT.** It compares `location.hostname` against the build-time site host and sets `data-foreign-host`, which a CSS rule turns into `display:none` on `[data-own-only]` — all during parse, before paint. React only knows the host after hydration, which is already too late for a contract naming the wrong company. Deliberately NOT a tenant lookup: "this is not our domain" is enough, and it is the only thing knowable without a request.
    - **Routes**: public `GET /tenants/:slug/page/:key` (both segments are words from a fixed list, clear of the UUID-in-path shape Cloudflare intercepted under `/prints/`); admin `PATCH /admin/tenants/:slug/page/:key`, superadmin only. **ONE route with the key in the path**, unlike identity/contact which get a route each — those carry a typed DTO per shape, every page has the identical one-field body.
    - **The HTML is stored as written and sanitised ON READ**, matching every other rich-text field here. Sanitising on write would freeze today's rules into the data: a later tightening would protect new pages and leave every stored one as it was.
    - **Admin: ONE editor and a key selector**, not four stacked — TipTap is a heavy mount and this screen already carries four panels. The selector chips each page as *published* or *empty* from the SERVER's gap list, so the panel cannot form its own opinion of "published".
    - **FAQs deliberately still inherit** — generic help text answered with real IPO fields, making no company claim.
- **Partner-facing PDFs — THREE documents, ONE stylesheet, one generator.** `/platform` (distribution partners) · `/brokers` (SEBI-registered stock brokers) · `/partner-api` (the API reference). All three import `apps/web/app/platform/platform.css`, so the cover, `@page`, and the hiding of the site header/footer are shared **by construction rather than by three people remembering to match them**. `node scripts/build-platform-pdf.js <platform|brokers|api>` renders each through its own `@media print` rules — **the PDF is the page printed, never a second document**, so a figure cannot disagree between them. Never hand-edit a PDF; the next run reverts it.
  - **THE PDF IS A SUBSET OF THE PAGE, SELECTED IN THE MARKUP.** `/partner-api` keeps its overview, onboarding paths and support prose on the WEB page — it is public and indexed and that is where a prospective partner lands — while the PDF is the endpoint contract alone. The sections that do not print carry **`pa-noprint` in the JSX**, not a selector in the stylesheet, so *"what is in the PDF"* is readable where the content is.
  - **A SCREEN RULE BROKE A PRINT COVER, and the tell was two files away.** The shared cover is `height: 267mm` inside a 269mm `@page` box; `.pa` carries `padding: 2rem 0 4rem` for the screen, and that ~8.5mm pushed the cover's **footer bar onto a page of its own** — page 2 at **7% fill holding one line**. The first diagnosis (blurb too long) was wrong and shortening it changed nothing. **Reset the wrapper's padding in print**, and when a cover overflows look for a rule written for the screen before editing the content.
  - **LOOK AT THE RENDERED PAGES — a proof sheet is the only thing that finds a layout fault.** Render the PDF to canvas with pdf.js and screenshot it. **RENDER THE PAGES CONCURRENTLY**: sequentially, the cover gradient's render promise **never settles** (`Unsupported ShadingType: 1`, which pdf.js logs as a warning and then hangs on), so the sheet stops at page 1 and **looks exactly like a one-page PDF**. A `try/catch` does not help — it never rejects. Fire all pages together and race a timeout.
  - **MEASURE THE FILL, do not eyeball it.** Per page, `max(pageHeight − textItem.transform[5]) / pageHeight` is how full it is, and `max(transform[4])` against the right margin is whether anything is clipped. That is what caught a 5-page draft whose last page held only the disclaimer — *"ends on half a page of white space"*, the fault v2.0 was rebuilt to fix, wearing different clothes.
  - **VERIFY A CONTENT CHANGE BY EXTRACTING THE FINISHED PDF'S TEXT**, never by trusting the edit. `pdfjs-dist` is already a dependency; a script run from `apps/api` can assert that every removed phrase is absent and every replacement present. This is the same rule as re-reading a record after writing to it.
  - **BROKER DOC LANGUAGE: USE THE TRADE'S OWN WORD, not a plainer synonym of your own** (operator, 2026-10-14, giving *"Correct a live bid" → "Modify a live bid"* as the exemplar — **Modify is an NSE action code**, New/Modify/Delete, so a broker's desk reads it without translating). The same pass replaced ten more: *Chase a stuck block* → **Unblock stuck funds** · *Keep the front end you have* → **Keep your own app** · *not just a screen* → *not just a website* · *encrypted PAN fingerprint* → *matched on the encrypted PAN* · *compliance function* → *compliance team* · *before a window shuts* → *before an issue closes*. **And the cover had regained the exact jargon v2.0 was rebuilt to remove** — a sub-line reading *"isolation at the database"*, the descendant of the *"RLS / Isolation in the database"* figure that became *"Zero / Engineering required"*. **A phrase removed from a headline comes back in a caption; grep the whole document for it.**
  - **NEITHER PARTNER PDF CARRIES A DOMAIN ANY MORE** (operator, 2026-10-14). The broker document lost its *"Apply online at newipo.finwave.co/partner"* close and both footer mentions; the API reference replaced the host in **FOUR printed places** — Base URL (*"Issued with your API key"*), the cover footer, and the curl / Node / Python examples (`https://<your-base-url>`). **All four had to move together**: replacing only the Base URL row would have left the same host in three copy-paste examples, i.e. removed nothing. The placeholder is deliberately UNMISTAKABLE — a plausible-looking hostname gets pasted into code and half-works. The call to action now lives in the covering email (`docs/broker-outreach-email.md`), where it cannot go stale.
  - **A COVER FIGURE MUST NOT CLAIM WHAT THE BODY NO LONGER OFFERS.** When live subscription left the broker offer, the cover still led with *"5 sec — refresh at the close"* and *"both exchanges reconciled into one table"*, which are subscription claims. When the catalogue reads left the API PDF, its cover still counted *"3 Endpoints"*. **Re-read the cover after every content cut.**
- **GMP entry for non-admins** `/gmp/entry` (`robots: noindex`): open + upcoming IPOs in one table with an input per row, latest-wins, published immediately. Access = a `GmpContributor` row OR a role carrying `gmp.submit`. Every save is attributed (`IpoGmp.submittedBy/submittedByName`) and appended to `extra.gmpLog`. Admin → IPO Management → **GMP Log & Contributors** is the audit trail + allow-list (add by mobile; the person must have signed in once). Entry points: button on that admin page, card on the consumer Account page when `gmpAccess()` allows.
- **Excel catalog import** (admin → IPO Management → Import from Excel): reads the operator's data-entry workbook (row 1 bands · row 2 HEADERS · row 3 grey EXAMPLE · data from row 4; column map in `apps/api/src/modules/ipo-import/workbook-map.ts`). **Two-step**: upload VALIDATES only and shows to-import / skipped / errors by row; nothing is written until confirmed. **Existing symbols are skipped entirely** — an import can never modify live IPO data. Imported rows land `hidden` and stay out of every public read until published (**H badge** in the IPO list). BUYBACK/OFS sheets are counted and parked. Guarded undo deletes hidden, importer-created rows with no applications/watchlist/allotment.
- **`Ipo.hidden`** is a REAL column, not a JSON flag: `NOT (extra->'k' = true)` is NULL-not-TRUE for rows lacking the key and once hid the whole catalog (`GET /ipos` returned 0 of 11). `GET /ipos` takes `limit`/`offset` (cap 2000) and excludes hidden; admin opts in with `?all=1`.
- **Front site**: **brand banner** (headline + the mixed-script statement `Demat कहीं भी, Apply इधर ही`, only the Devanagari spans carry `lang="hi"`; tagline + 8 capability chips spotlighting one at a time) holding 9s each vs 5s for data slides, with a progress bar; **IPO slides** carry pastel **category lanes** (Retail/sHNI/bHNI → shares + lot range, amount, For 1×, Est. gain at GMP) over a candlestick backdrop. Also: Today strip, time-aware status chips + calibrated demand words (SME vs Mainboard thresholds), live nav with signals + IPO command panel, hubs **/gmp /subscription /allotment /performance /calendar /news /glossary**, GMP-accuracy section, public allotment checker (PAN), SEO listing pages (/ipos/open|upcoming|listed, /sme), trust pages (/about /faqs /terms /disclaimer /privacy-policy), WhatsApp CTA (tenant setting `whatsappChannel`), news engine (admin-written posts, TipTap rich-text editor shared with the IPO form), lifecycle reminders (server watchlist + hourly alert sweep: open/closing/allotment/listing).
- **Masters** (admin): Lead Managers · Registrars · IPO Categories · Issue Types · Relationships · **UPI Handles** (validates profile UPI IDs) · **Anchor Investors** (picked per IPO with ₹ amounts).
- **Allotment imports** (admin): registrar allottee files (DBF/XLSB/XLSX/CSV, ≤100 MB; IIS limit raised to 200 MB in `apps/api/web.config`) per IPO — parsed in a **spawned worker process** (a 1M-row XLSB needs ~3 GB heap / 25 s; NEVER parse in the iisnode event loop), batch-inserted into `AllotmentRecord` (raw PAN by operator decision — admin-only data), auto-matches OUR applications by PAN → status + registrar rejection reason (`Application.allotmentReason`) through the standard recordAllotment path. **Allotment List** search: partial PAN + amount with `>/</=`. DB size bounded by **archive-then-purge**: gz NDJSON on disk, one-click restore, auto-sweep 60 days after listing. Public `/allotment` checker answers ANY PAN from registrar rows when we hold no in-house application.
- **Day-wise trends**: admin GMP saves append `extra.gmpLog`; subscription poller appends `extra.subLog` (daily EOD, 14-day cap) AND `extra.subLogHour` (rolling 72-entry hourly window = 3 days × 24 hours, deduped by `d+h` — the latest snapshot in each hour bucket wins). `enrich()` shows REAL data or nothing for live rows (the synthesized-series fallbacks were only ever reachable when MOCK rows carried no `extra` object, and MOCK is empty as of 2026-09-16).
- **`SERVER_OWNED_EXTRA` — the IPO form used to DELETE the poller's history on every save** (`ipo.service.ts`, fixed 2026-09-24). `update()` writes `extra: dto.extra` **wholesale**, and the admin form PATCHes an object built from its own field state, so every key the form has never heard of vanished. Measured: VARMORA ran 22–24 Sep and held **one** day — the one the poller had rewritten since the save; ROBOKIDZ ran 19–23 Sep and held two; seven records saved inside one minute all came out with a single day. The day-wise table then labelled its only row **"Day 1 (Last)"**, which is how the operator found it — the label was right, the data under it had been erased. Four keys are protected: **`subLog`, `subLogHour`, `gmpLog`, `gmpSourceId`**. Deliberately a **whitelist, not a deep merge** — `{...existing, ...dto}` would also stop the form ever deleting one of its OWN fields. **Add to the list whenever a background job starts writing a new `extra` key.** Lost days are not recoverable. **The same fault ran in the OPPOSITE direction until 2026-09-25** — the poller reverting the operator — see the jsonb-merge note under Subscription poller.
- **To prove an `extra`-preservation fix, watch `subLogHour`, never `subLog`.** The daily row is rewritten by the next poll within 5 s, so "1 before, 1 after" proves nothing. The hourly window holds one bucket per hour since 10:00 IST, so a wipe rebuilds exactly ONE; VARMORA kept all **7** across a real `ipo.update` (confirmed in `AuditLog`, 10:36:49) and that is the whole proof.
- **Applications-for-1×** is derived from the issue's REAL reservation table + lot size + bid-engine floors (retail 1 lot · S-HNI >₹2L · B-HNI >₹10L); app-wise uses each category's actual subscription, and the operator's `extra.applicationsReceived` (applications RECEIVED; `noOfApp` is the legacy name) wins over anything derived. **Since 2026-10-21 there is a MIDDLE term and the derivation is gone**: operator's stated figure → the EXCHANGE's own `total.bidCount` → nothing. The old fallback multiplied forms-for-1× by the SHARE-wise multiple, which is not a count of applications — see the write-up under *Visitor tracking*. Needs **Reservation table + Issue Size**; **Est. gain at GMP** needs a GMP value. Both columns render with a dimmed `—` when pending rather than disappearing.
- **UNDER REVIEW — two parallel candidates.** Both keep the live surface untouched so the operator can compare, and BOTH must be collapsed once decided:
  - **Banner**: 4 brand slides (dark hero · dark promise wall · pastel hero · pastel promise) + each of the first `PASTEL_TWINS` IPOs rendered twice, dark then pastel, on identical data. Constants to edit when cutting: `BRAND_SLIDES`, `LIGHT_BRAND_SLIDES`, `PASTEL_TWINS` in `HeroBanner.tsx`. **Every slide must measure 302px** — the track takes its tallest child, so one oversized slide silently stretches the whole banner (this happened: 302 → 430).
  - ~~**Home layout** `/home2`~~ **DECIDED 2026-09-02** — the explorer layout won and is now `/`; the previous layout is preserved at `/homeold` (`robots: noindex`). The `v2` flags on `IpoCard`, `IpoCompareTable` and `HomeView` still exist because `/homeold` is their last caller — delete the route and the flags together. Original description: `/home2` (`robots: noindex`, `HomeView({ v2 })`): one filter line + a 304px sticky rail (Live Subscription · Exp. Premium · today's date + counts · IPO Calendar · Allotment · Android app teaser), 2-up cards, `IpoExplorer2.tsx`. Gated by flags on shared components — `IpoCard({ v2 })`, `IpoCompareTable({ shortNames })` — all defaulting false.
- **`shortName()`** (`packages/shared-types/src/format.ts`) strips a TRAILING Limited/Ltd/Pvt Ltd on top of `titleCase()` — for cards, banner slides and tables. The full legal name stays in the `title` tooltip and on the detail page (identity + SEO). Only trailing, so "Limited Liability Partners Ltd" keeps its mid-name word.
- **`packages/shared-types` compiles to `dist`** (`main: dist/index.js`). Editing `src/` is NOT enough — run `npm run build` in that package or web/mobile typecheck fails with "has no exported member".
- **IPO archive** `/ipos/archive` — year · month · board · search over the comparison table. The main list carries what's in play plus the **last 6 months**; search still reaches the whole catalog.
- **Live-slug contract** (static export): dynamic pages need a `/X/live` client fallback + IIS rewrite in `apps/web/public/web.config` — exists for `ipos`, `apply`, `print`, `news`.
- **Mobile (Expo)**: apply/print flows + **4 tabs** — Home · **Applied** · Insights · Account (cut from five on 2026-11-04; **Profiles is a stack screen under Account** now — see the tab-bar section). Insights = allotment checker (any PAN) + GMP / Live Subscription / Listing Performance / News / Glossary / Calendar. Home is cards-first and **now carries the Moments hero ABOVE those cards** (2026-11-05; the operator reaffirmed cards-first that morning and asked for the hero the same afternoon — it was added, not substituted) when Phase 2 of the design handoff started** — which is why a Moments engine exists with nothing mounting a hero; see *Phase 2 of the handoff, without the hero*. **Watchlist is a second stack screen under Account** (2026-11-05). Slim header with **Live Subscription** pill, inline **search**, calendar (gold dot when today has events), alerts. Cards/detail/hubs render day-wise trends, anchors, DRHP-RHP links, reminder bells; News carries the banner carousel; Account links the web trust pages.
- **Home shows a FIXED 16**, then one `Browse all IPOs` link to `/ipos/archive` — no `View more` ladder (operator, 2026-09-02). The 16 come off the top of `compareForList`, so they are the most ACTIONABLE issues, not the most recent. The board chips (All · Mainboard · SME) are faceted off **those sixteen** and filter WITHIN them; counting the whole recent window instead put "SME 42" over a page showing eleven.
- **The countdown dial branches on STAGE, not status** (`CountdownDial.tsx`). Branching on `ipo.status` — which knows only listed/open/other — made every closed issue count down to its OPEN date, sit past the target and render the "opens imminently" state: **23 cards showed SOON · OPENS**, including all 20 Awaiting Allotment and every Allotment Out. Each stage now has its own next event (awaiting → allotment date, allotment out → listing date) and its own settled state when there is no date to count to. Ring text over 7 characters drops to 7px (`.rc span.long`) — the ring is 56px with a 5px stroke, so "AWAITING" at 8px ran through the arc.
- **The IPO card's key row is width-budgeted** (`.ic-keys`, rebuilt 2026-09-23). It is **283px at the two-up breakpoint** and it holds the date pill plus the subscription reading, so anything added there has to be measured, not eyeballed. What went wrong and what it now costs:
  - **`fmtRange` prints the year ONCE, at the end**, and the month once when both dates share it — `19–23 Sep 2026` · `28 Sep – 2 Oct 2026` · `30 Dec – 2 Jan 2027`. Spelling both ends out (`19 Sep 2026 – 23 Sep 2026`) measured **188px of the 283**, leaving 85px for a subscription chip whose SHORTEST form (`0.08× building up`) needed 110px — so **every** subscribed card wrapped to a second line and stood ~48px taller than its neighbours. An IPO window is a handful of days; the closing year dates both ends unambiguously. 188px → 127px.
  - **The demand reading is a figure plus ONE SOLID ICON** — `264.7× 🔥` — no bar, no words (operator decision). `DEMAND_ICON` maps the tier to `clock-fill` / `check-fill` / `trending-fill` / `flame-fill`, coloured muted / muted / green / **gold** along the old bar's ramp. The bar (`.ic-track`) is gone: it read the same tier a second time. At 14px beside a bold figure a 1.75px OUTLINE reads as a smudge, so the three glyphs needing interior detail carve it with `fill-rule: evenodd` rather than overpainting in the surface colour — the shape then survives any background the card is later given.
  - **The words live in the tooltip and the accessible name**, never on the row. Hover/tap/focus opens the portal `Tooltip` with the category split — QIB, NII with its two HNI rows indented beneath, Retail, Total, as-of date. Rows are re-sorted into that order because `subscriptionTable` returns the API's order, which puts the synthetic `nii` roll-up AFTER Retail, where a flat list reads it as one more category rather than the subtotal of the two rows above it.
- **`demandWord()` / `demandTier()` in `format.ts` are the ONE demand ladder** — `Building up · Steady · Strong · Very strong`, thresholds `[1,3,10]` mainboard / `[1,10,50]` SME. Read by the web card, the compare table, the banner and all three mobile sites. The words lost their `demand` noun on 2026-09-23 (it repeated what the `×` already says, and at 18 characters `Exceptional demand` was wider than the room the card had); `IpoCard` kept a lowercase copy of the thresholds until then and now delegates. **Longest entry is 11 characters — keep it that way.**
