- **`AllotmentImport.headers` — the file's OWN header row, stored verbatim, and this is the part that matters.** Registrars ship far more columns than we map and name them differently from one another, so the honest way to add a field is to read what the document says rather than guess. **One real upload now names every column, permanently, for every registrar.** The worker reports it on stdout as `H <json>` beside the existing `P` and `DONE` lines; a malformed one is swallowed, because a diagnostic must never cost an import.
- **Export — `GET /admin/allotment/records.csv`, STREAMED and cursor-paged in 5,000-row batches.** Verified on VNL: **2,26,552 rows, 18.98 MB, several batches**. A single `findMany` — or a real `.xlsx`, which must be held whole in memory — is the same mistake that already forces the PARSER into its own process. CSV with a **UTF-8 BOM**, because without it Excel reads the system codepage and mangles non-ASCII names.
  - **ONE filter builder, `recordWhere()`, shared by the list and the export**, so a download is the rows on screen rather than a second opinion about what the filters meant. Proved rather than asserted: a `status=allotted` export returned **84,694** against a list total of **84,694**.
  - **Escaping checked across the whole table, not a sample**: all **2,26,553** lines carry exactly 12 columns under quote-aware counting, so no registrar name with a comma shifts its row.
  - The button is an **`<a href>`, never a fetch** — a 1M-row IPO runs to hundreds of MB and must not sit in a browser variable.
- **`tmName` is APPENDED at index 11 of the worker's row tuple, never inserted.** An archive written before this date has 11-element rows, so `r[11]` is undefined and `|| null` makes it a null column rather than a crash. Restore was checked against that shape.
- **Upload limit 105 MB → 126 MB, and the worker heap 4 GB → 6 GB WITH IT** (operator ask, 2026-10-13: "allow up to 120 MB"; 126 is 120 plus headroom so a file the operator calls 120 MB is never refused on a rounding difference).
  - **RAISING THE LIMIT ALONE WOULD HAVE MADE FAILURES WORSE.** A 1M-row XLSB already needs ~3 GB, so a file half again as large would have cleared the upload check and then died INSIDE the parse worker — trading a clear "too big" at the door for an opaque failure after a multi-minute upload. The box has 24 GB with ~14 GB free and the worker is short-lived.
  - **IIS was never the constraint and is unchanged at 200 MB.** Proved rather than assumed: a **115 MB POST reached the Nest guard (401, not IIS's 404.13) in 1.9 s**, before and after the change.
  - **CLOUDFLARE CAPS REQUEST BODIES AT 100 MB BELOW ENTERPRISE, so 120 MB may never arrive** — every site on this box is Cloudflare-fronted. The evidence is local, not theoretical: a **70.4 MB `.xlsb` landed in `allotment-tmp` on 2026-10-13 with NO `AllotmentImport` row and a truncated zip** (`Bad compressed size: 0 != 431`), i.e. the transfer was cut off **well under the old 105 MB limit**. Going past 100 MB needs an Enterprise plan, a grey-clouded upload host, or chunked upload — **it is not a number in our source**, and the comment on `MAX_FILE` says so.
  - **THE UI NOW ENFORCES 120 WHILE `MAX_FILE` IS 126 AND THAT IS DELIBERATE** (2026-10-27, see *An allotment amount that would not fit in a rupee column*): the picker refuses above 120 MB, flags the 100-120 MB band as at risk from the Cloudflare body cap, and `MAX_FILE` keeps its 126 MB of headroom so a file the operator calls 120 MB is never refused on a rounding difference. **The limit was stated in FOUR places and all four moved**: `MAX_FILE`, the `web.config` comment, the import page's docblock and its visible `sub` line, plus the API client's JSDoc. **A stated limit that disagrees with the enforced one is how an operator learns to distrust the screen.**
- ~~**Still open**: *"show all the columns I upload"*.~~ **ANSWERED 2026-10-28 — 23 columns in the file, 12 mapped, 11 dropped; operator says none for now. See the section below.** It was deliberately NOT guessed

## Domain & compliance rules (MUST follow)
- **Self-PAN rule:** every RAIL application uses that person's own PAN + demat + bank/UPI; one public (retail/HNI) application per PAN per IPO **plus** one per reserved quota (shareholder/employee). Print-PDF paths are validation-free by operator policy.
- **GMP** = unofficial/unregulated → always with the disclaimer; OFF for gmp-disabled tenants; no kostak/sauda quotes.
- **Distribute + inform, don't advise** — no recommendations, ratings or review scores (no RIA registration).
- **DPDP** — just-in-time collection, explicit consent (single-checkbox itemized wording is in use — flag for counsel), PII vault-tokenized.
- **Partner-commission module DEFERRED** until the SEBI referral/AP regime settles.

## Application rail
- NSE adapter: verified WEB API v1.20.5. **BSE iBBS is NOT a blind template** (checked 2026-09-02): the document arrived and the adapter matches it field for field — action codes `N`/`M`/`D`, the endpoints we call (`/login`, `/ipoorder`, `/ipoorderbulk`), and the payload keys (`actioncode`, `applicantname`, `applicationno`, `asba_upiid`, `cuttoffflag`, `clientbenfid`, `dpid`, `quantity`, `rate`, `scripid`). It was written against this same document family, not guessed. What it lacks is a UAT run, plus endpoints the doc offers that we never implemented: **`ipoupistatus`** (the UPI-status pull the Refresh button needs), `ipoappenquiry` / `ipodpupienquiry` for reconciliation, `ipodeltadownload`, and the **IPO Bid Upload API** (v1.05.4) that would give us the file channel. UAT and production base URLs are both stated in it. ALL exchange docs — the NSE zip and both BSE PDFs — live at `D:\Claude\IPO Application\`, deliberately OUTSIDE the repo: they are vendor-confidential and the BSE pair is footer-marked BSE-INTERNAL. Member creds live in admin Exchange Rails (subscription polling always uses the operator's own member; bidding routes by the IPO's active online-series member).

### UPI payment status — the live status an investor sees (2026-10-08)
- **`Application.upiStatusText` + `upiStatusAt` existed since the initial commit and NEITHER ever worked.** The column documented as *"the exchange's own UPI wording, kept verbatim"* was written `String(record.upiPaymentStatus)` — the numeric CODE, so it held `"100"` — and only by the operator's manual Refresh. `upiStatusAt` was written by **nothing at all**, and the admin Bidding Report has been rendering a permanently-empty as-of value. **The authoritative PUSH path (the exchange calling us) wrote neither.** A field existing in the schema and typed into a report column is not evidence that anything fills it — **grep for the WRITER before exposing a column to customers**, or `"100"` is what the investor reads.
- **`UPI_STATUS_LABEL` / `upiStatusLabel()` in `rail-callback.dto.ts` is the ONE code→sentence map**, verbatim from **NSE WEB API v1.20.5**, the `upiPaymentStatusFlag` table on `/v1/apppaystatus` (the spec is on this box at `D:\Claude\IPO Application\_nse_extracted\eipo_web.txt` — **read it rather than inventing wording**; the example quoted in this file for years, *"Request sent to Investor UPI App for Acceptance"*, is NOT what the spec says, which is just *"Request Sent"*).
  - **It exists because `mapUpiStatus` is deliberately lossy**: eleven codes collapse onto five of our statuses, and **the three rejections an investor can act on differently all land on `rejected`** — *Rejected by Investor* (they declined it), *Rejected due to invalid UPI* (a fixable typo), *Rejected by Sponsor Bank*. So the sentence is stored BESIDE the status, never derived from it, and the UI renders both.
  - **An unknown code returns `null`**, never a stringified number and never a guessed phrase: showing an investor `"47"` reads as a fault in the app and a guess could be wrong about their money. `mapUpiStatus` already keeps an unknown code non-terminal, so the bid is unaffected and the UI falls back to our own status line.
  - NSE's vocabulary. **BSE's per-bid pull (`ipoupistatus`) is still unimplemented — confirm its codes against this table rather than assuming they match**; only the NSE adapter populates `upiPaymentStatus` today.
- **Both paths now do ONE identical write** through `ApplicationRepo.updateStatus` (patch type `StatusPatch`, exported from rail-adapters). The refresh service used to follow up with a second `application.update` of its own — which is where the stringified code came from — while its docblock claimed *"identical writes, identical event log rows"*. **A docblock asserting two paths agree is not a mechanism that makes them agree.**
- **The sponsor bank's own `upiPayReason` WINS over the code wording** when it sends one: it is the more specific of the two and the one an investor can act on. Blank/whitespace falls back to the label, never to an empty string.
- **`rejectionReason` was being passed and dropped on the floor.** `handlePayStatus`/`handleDpStatus` have always passed a reason and `PrismaApplicationRepo.updateStatus` wrote only status + amountBlocked + the event row, so a rejection's cause was readable only inside `ApplicationStatusEvent.detail` JSON. Now written, and **on failures only** — a stale reason beside a confirmed bid reads as a problem.
- **A malformed callback used to return 500.** A body missing `symbol`/`applicationNumber` reached `findBySymbolAndApplicationNo(undefined, undefined)` and Prisma threw on the `symbol: undefined` lookup — where the exchange's own doc requires `{ status: "failed", reason }`. **A 500 also tells an exchange to RETRY**, which is wrong for a body that can never succeed. `badKeys()` refuses before any database call.
  - **A MISSING flag is not an UNRECOGNISED one, and the distinction is the whole point.** `mapUpiStatus(NaN)` and `mapDpStatus(undefined)` both return a real status (`mandate_pending` / `submitted`), so a malformed callback would have quietly overwritten a live bid with a status derived from garbage. Missing/non-numeric is REFUSED; an unrecognised NUMBER still maps to pending, deliberately — that is forward compatibility with a code the exchange adds later.
- **Investor UI, web + mobile**: the sentence plus `As of <fmtDateTime> · from the exchange`, beside our own status line. **Tone comes from `status`, never from reading the sentence.** Web is `.upi-line` / `.upi-dot` in globals.css (new names — the existing `.upi-*` family is the profile form's split input) with the COLOUR on the 7px dot, not on text over a tint, because `--pos` on `--pos-soft` is 3.50:1 and fails AA. Mobile is `upiRow` in `app/(tabs)/applications.tsx`. **Keep the two in step by hand**, same as the split UPI input.
- **No consumer-facing Refresh button, deliberately.** `fetchTransactions` pulls a whole delta window per call, so one investor tap = a full exchange fetch — the load pattern the refresh service's docblock already warns about. The right fix is the per-bid pull (`ipoupistatus`), unimplemented. The push path makes polling unnecessary.
- **A dead check found in passing**: `missingDetails` tested `a.applyMethod === 'upi'`, a value the `ApplyMethod` enum (`native | pdf`) has NEVER held — so *"UPI ID missing"*, the one piece of advice an investor can act on, could never appear. `native` IS the UPI-ASBA path.
- **39 → 50 tests in rail-adapters**, incl. `rail-callback.service.spec.ts` with a fake repo asserting what each callback writes. They earned it immediately: a later edit of mine deleted the `app` lookup from both handlers and the spec caught it on the next run.
- **Proving a column write without fabricating data**: read a real row, write inside an interactive `$transaction`, read back, then THROW to roll back, then re-read outside to confirm it is clean. Worth it here because `upiStatusAt` is `timestamptz(3)` and this project has already been burned by a naive/aware mismatch on exactly this class of column — measured **0 ms drift**.
- **UNPROVEN END TO END, and it cannot be proved yet**: all 1,651 applications are print-path (`pdf`) with **0 exchange application numbers and 0 status events**, so no UPI event has ever flowed. The writes and wording are under test; the investor line renders only once a sentence exists. Blocked on NSE UAT creds like the rest of the bidding surface.

### UPI ID change, and the bid submission builder (2026-10-08)
- **`toSubmission()` IN `bid-operations.service.ts` RETURNED `any`, AND UNDER THAT `any` NO BID COULD EVER HAVE BEEN PLACED.** Against the `BidSubmission` contract the adapters actually post: **no `upi` field at all** — so `toAddPayload` throws *"Either upi or bankAccount is required"* **before any network call** · `clientId` where the contract says **`clientBenId`** · flat `quantity`/`price` where it says **`bids: Bid[]`**, so `req.bids.map` throws · **`pan: profile.pan`, a column `InvestorProfile` DOES NOT HAVE** (only `panTokenRef`/`panHash`), so always `undefined` · no `bidReferenceNumber`, which the spec requires on anything but a `new`. **Five fatal faults in one object literal, none visible to the compiler, none reachable by a test.** The lesson is the `any`: a return type of `any` on a function whose whole job is to satisfy a contract removes the only check that mattered.
- **`buildBidSubmission()` in `packages/rail-adapters/src/submission/build-bid-submission.ts` is now the ONE builder** — pure, over plain values, **24 tests from the spec's own sample request** (`/v1/transactions/add`, the UPI-flow example). It was untestable *because* it needed a Prisma row; moving it out is the same step that made `offeredFromIpo`, `parseAnchorRows` and `offerTotals` testable. **It REFUSES rather than sending a payload the exchange will reject**, with wording that names the applicant and the fix — the adapter's generic error arrives after a session has been opened. A malformed PAN is masked in the message (`NO****23`); never log a whole PAN.
- **PII is resolved JUST-IN-TIME and never stored on the operation row**, so a retry sends whatever the profile says now. **A failed vault resolve becomes an EMPTY STRING on purpose** — the builder's refusal names the applicant, which beats a vault stack trace on a closing-day queue.
- **`mayChangeUpi(status, atExchange)` in `bidRules.ts` has THREE outcomes, and the middle one is why it is a function**: not at the exchange → a plain edit, nothing to tell anyone · at the exchange, mandate not accepted → **queue a MODIFY carrying the new UPI** · **funds already blocked → REFUSED**, because the block sits in the account behind the OLD UPI ID and the exchange has no concept of moving it; the refusal names the alternative (withdraw and re-apply). **Category plays no part** — fixing a mistyped UPI ID is not a revision of the bid, so the ICDR up-only rule governing `mayReviseTo` does not apply and an HNI may do it. 6 tests.
- **`upi` IS AN APPLICANT-LEVEL FIELD** on `/transactions/add`, beside `pan` and `dpId` rather than inside the bid — which is the whole reason "change the UPI on a placed bid" is expressible at all: a modify restates it. (The spec also requires `timestamp` echoed from the last response on a modify; **not yet carried — check this before UAT.**)
- **THE UPI ID LIVES ON THE APPLICANT, NOT THE APPLICATION.** Changing it changes it for every bid that applicant holds, so restating only the one the investor clicked would leave a **SIBLING** bid at the exchange carrying a payment instruction we had silently replaced. `changeUpi` restates every live bid of that applicant and the reply says how many. **Order matters**: the rule is checked BEFORE the vault is touched, because refusing after writing would leave the profile holding a new UPI ID while the exchange still names the old one.
- `ProfilesService.setUpi` is the ONE way in, so the `name@handle` shape and the admin-managed handle allow-list are not re-implemented on the applications side (`assertUpiAllowed` stays private). `ApplicationsModule` imports `ProfilesModule` for it; **no cycle, but Nest DI cycles only show at BOOT** — that is what the boot test is for.
- **`UPI_CHANGEABLE` exists twice** (web `Portfolio.tsx`, mobile `applications.tsx`) and excludes `upi_blocked`/`confirmed`: **a button that can only fail is worse than no button.** `rejected` IS included — a rejected mandate is the main reason to need this. The action is deliberately NOT on the allotment-checker tab, which is a read-only lookup surface.
- **Verified read-only against 323 real applications: ALL refused, for exactly one reason — no UPI ID and no bank account.** Correct: they are partner-API print records and the print path never touches the rail. Two things that probe taught: **a vault token is `v2:provider:wrapped:iv:tag:enc`**, envelope-encrypted with a wrapped DEK, so treating the ref as raw base64 ciphertext fails on every row and looks exactly like key loss — **instantiate the compiled `PiiVaultService` instead of reimplementing it**; and **do not strip an identifying tail with `\([^)]*\)$`** — an applicant name can END in a parenthetical (`SHAH NARENDRAKUMAR (HUF)`), so that regex eats the name and leaves the id. Slice from the literal marker instead.
- **STILL UNPROVEN AT THE EXCHANGE** — blocked on NSE UAT creds. The difference from before is that it is no longer *guaranteed* to fail.

### Unblock request to the sponsor bank (2026-10-08)
- **THE FIELD ALREADY EXISTED — check before adding one.** A single `Ipo.sponsorBank` column was started and REVERTED on finding `extra.sponsorBank`, labelled *"Sponsor bank(s)"*, **filled on 60 IPOs** and genuinely **PLURAL**: `"Axis Bank Limited, HDFC Bank Limited and ICICI Bank Limited"` is one real stored value. A column would have been a second home for data that already has one — **the `issueSize` / `extra.issueSizeCr` desync all over again** — and the schema now carries a comment where the column would have gone, so the next person does not re-add it. The free-text box became a **tick-list of master names that still writes the same comma-separated string**, so legacy values stay editable (one record is `" KOTAK BANK"` — leading space, caps).
- **ALL of an issue's sponsor banks are emailed, not one.** A UPI mandate is raised on exactly ONE of them and **we do not record which**, so guessing would mail a bank that never held the money. The application number identifies the mandate, so the bank holding it can act and the others see in seconds that it is not theirs. **Routing to one bank needs a new field fed by the rail** — that is the only way this changes.
- **`mayRequestUnblock()` in `packages/shared-types/src/unblockRules.ts`** (20 tests) — money actually blocked · **allotment PAST** · SEBI's T+1 grace elapsed (`UNBLOCK_GRACE_HOURS`) · nothing already refunded · a **24-hour cooldown** (`UNBLOCK_COOLDOWN_HOURS`). **The guard that matters most is the one an anxious investor hits first**: before allotment the block is CORRECT, and mailing a bank about it teaches them to ignore us. On a **partial allotment it asks about the DIFFERENCE** (`blocked − allotted − refunded`), not the whole block.
- **`matchSponsorBanks()` in `packages/shared-types/src/sponsorBank.ts`** (17 tests, **every fixture a real stored value**) — splits on commas, semicolons, `&` and a standalone `and`, then matches the master case- and whitespace-insensitively because the values were typed by hand. **It does NOT guess at a near match**: `"HDFC"` will not become `"HDFC Bank Limited"`, because emailing a bank about an issue it never sponsored is worse than reporting the gap. **Unknown names and rows with no email are reported SEPARATELY — they have different owners** (the issue's data vs the master's), and a **partial match still sends**, because the bank holding the mandate may be among the ones that did resolve. Known limit: a bank whose own name contains "and" would be split; none do.
- **A FAILED SEND MUST NOT CONSUME THE COOLDOWN** — a misconfigured SMTP would otherwise lock an investor out of asking for their own money for a day. And **`dev: true` is reported honestly** when email is unconfigured (composed + logged, not delivered): saying *"sent"* leaves someone waiting on a reply to a mail nobody received.
- **PAN IS SENT MASKED** (`ABCDE**34F`). The application number and UPI ID go in full and should be enough to locate a mandate; a full PAN adds little and would then also sit in **our own message log in clear** (the log stores the body). **One line to change if a bank says they need it** — operator decision.
- **The master reuses the generic masters controller as an `ORG_KIND`**, so `SponsorBank` needed no DTO changes and inherited the full contact set plus the bulk upload. `masters.sponsor-banks.manage` goes to Admin **and Staff** — Staff because the IPO form's picker needs inline add, the same reason registrars got it.
- **Availability is decided SERVER-SIDE** and carried on `ApplicationView` (`unblockAvailable` / `unblockReason` / `unblockStuckAmount`). The UI must not re-derive *"is the bank late yet"* — a second opinion on a 24-hour grace period is how a button that can only fail appears.
- **The master is SEEDED and name matching is complete — only the EMAIL ADDRESSES are missing** (2026-10-08). `scripts/seed-sponsor-banks.js` created the five banks the catalogue already names — **ICICI · Kotak Mahindra · HDFC · Axis · Yes Bank** — each named after its **most common stored spelling**, which is what made 45 of the 60 IPOs resolve without touching their data. `scripts/canonicalise-sponsor-banks.js` then fixed the other 15, every one failing on an abbreviation rather than a different bank (`"KOTAK BANK"` · `"AXIS BANK"` · `"ICICI BANK"` · `"HDFC Bank Ltd"` · `"KOTAK MAHINDRA BANK LTD"` · `" KOTAK BANK"`). **Coverage is 60 of 60.** Backup of the 15 old strings: `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\backups\sponsor-bank-canonicalise-*.json`.
  - **Fix an abbreviation in the DATA, never by loosening the matcher.** The tick-list writes canonical names from now on, so that was a one-off catch-up, and the matcher stays strict for the case it exists for.
  - **A rename script must only write when a NAME changed.** The first version rejoined on `", "` unconditionally and would have rewritten **38** records — most only turning `"A and B"` into `"A, B"` on rows that already resolved. That is cosmetic churn on operator-entered data and it **buries the 15 that matter** in a 38-record diff.
  - **`0 of 5 rows have an email address`, so the feature still refuses every request** — now with *"We have no email address on file for …"* rather than *"not in our records"*. **Addresses must come from the operator; never invent one** — a wrong address sends an investor's request, with their name and masked PAN, to a stranger. Add them at admin → Masters → Sponsor Banks.
- **SMTP HAS STILL NEVER BEEN PROVEN END TO END**, and the whole feature is an email — see the SMTP line under Open items.

## Open items
- ~~**ONE PARTNER ADMIN CARRIES `providers.manage` AND THE OTHER FIVE DO NOT**~~ **CLOSED 2026-10-21 — all six hold it and the white-label gate behind it is gone.** The note read as drift between partners; it was not, and checking why is what turned up a live hole in every slug-less admin route. Full write-up in *`providers.manage`, and a hole in every slug-less route*. Three things came out of it, in the order they happened: the premise was wrong (it is a WHITE-LABEL permission, so four were correct to lack it) · the operator granted it to all six anyway · the operator then removed the white-label gate, so for all six it now opens the save and not merely the screen.
  - **This bullet used to be welded onto the one below it by a MISSING NEWLINE**, so the `RAIL_CALLBACK_AUTH` item had no bullet of its own and its three sub-points read as belonging to this one. Repaired here. **A list item that swallows the next is invisible in the rendered file and obvious in the raw one** — worth a glance after any hand-written insertion.
- ~~**RAIL_CALLBACK_AUTH IS COMMENTED OUT IN apps/api/.env**~~ **ENABLED AND VERIFIED 2026-10-22, on the operator’s instruction.** The exposure was real and current rather than theoretical: an unauthenticated `POST /api/v1/apppaystatus` reached the handler and answered `{"status":"failed","reason":"Application no does not exist"}` — a BUSINESS answer, which is what an unguarded route looks like from outside.
  - **VERIFIED AS A TRUTH TABLE, because a guard that answers 401 to EVERYONE is indistinguishable from a working one.** No header → **401** · wrong header → **401** · **valid header → 200** with the ordinary business answer. The valid case is the one that mattered: the guard checks `base64(SHA256(SHA1(member password)))` against every active NSE credential and **skips any whose secret will not decrypt** — so had the credential been unreadable it would have rejected the EXCHANGE too, a door locked from both sides and invisible until UAT. The header was computed in-process from the vault and never printed.
  - **IT NEEDED NO APP-POOL HANDSHAKE, and that applies to ANY `.env` change.** No code changed, so nothing had to compile — only the Node worker had to restart. **`.env` is NOT in `watchedFiles` (`web.config;dist\*.js`), so editing it alone changes nothing**; touching `web.config` recycles the worker and health was back in **~5 seconds**. Same lever as the `enableXFF` change earlier that day.
  - **`.env` IS READ EVEN THOUGH `cwd` IS `dist` AND THERE IS NO `dist/.env`** — established by experiment, because the naive reading says the opposite: `ConfigModule.forRoot()` carries no `envFilePath`, so it resolves `.env` against `process.cwd()`, and the uploads prove that is `apps/api/dist` (today’s files land in `dist/uploads`). It works regardless. **Do not reason about where this app’s env comes from — change it and probe.**
  - **THE `JWT_SECRET` IN `.env` WAS A 26-CHARACTER DEV PLACEHOLDER AND IT WAS THE LIVE SIGNING SECRET — ROTATED 2026-10-22.** It both signs and verifies every session token on the platform, customers and operators alike, so anyone who guessed that string could mint a valid token for ANY user id including a superadmin; the only thing left standing between them and the account was `JwtAuthGuard`’s subject lookup, which checks the user exists and is active and nothing more. Replaced with 48 random bytes. **Limited, but only by luck: the exact string is in no tracked file** (`git grep` finds nothing, and `.env.example` says `"change-me"`), so it was weak rather than published.
    - **SESSION LENGTH CUT 30 DAYS → 7, the same day and by the same lever** (`JWT_EXPIRES_IN` in `.env`, worker recycled, no handshake). A 30-day bearer token is a long window on a platform with **no refresh and no revocation list**: nothing renews a session and nothing can end one early, so the expiry IS the whole session policy.
    - ~~**ONE KNOB SERVES THREE LOGINS**~~ **SPLIT 2026-10-22, same day.** Customer OTP and customer password keep `JWT_EXPIRES_IN` (7d); `operatorLogin` now reads **`JWT_EXPIRES_IN_OPERATOR`** — code default **12h**, and **production runs 24h** (operator raised it the same day, once they saw what signing in twice a day felt like). The split exists because a value tight enough for an admin console punishes the wrong people: **29 of the 30 recent sign-ins were operators**, and **11 operators hold a password against only 5 customers**, so an operator re-logs in instantly while most customers need an SMS that costs money. **The code falls back to 12h rather than to the shared value**, so a fresh install gets the tighter figure for admins instead of inheriting the customer one.
    - **BOTH HALVES PROVED ON REAL TOKENS FROM THE REAL ROUTES, because 'it compiled' says nothing about which branch runs.** Operator login minted **12.00 hours**, customer signup **7.00 days**. Neither probe used a known credential: the customer one seeds `otp:<requestId>` into Redis so no SMS is sent, the operator one creates a throwaway account with the app’s own `hashPassword` and deletes it in a `finally`. Verified afterwards that **0 probe accounts survived and the user count was unchanged at 38**.
    - **A SESSION LENGTH IS PROVED ON A REAL TOKEN, not on the env value.** `expiresIn` is parsed by `ms`, which accepts far more than it looks (`7days` is as valid as `7d`), and a truly malformed string throws at SIGN time — which breaks every login rather than failing visibly. Measured instead: a token minted by the live API read **issued 10:31:33Z, expires 7.00 days later**. Minted without sending anything by seeding `otp:<requestId>` into Redis directly, which is all `readOtp` reads; the throwaway account was deleted and `MessageLog` confirmed **0 messages** in the window.
    - **HOW TO TEST WHICH SECRET A RUNNING PROCESS USES, WITHOUT FORGING ANYTHING USABLE — the reusable part.** Sign a token for a subject that CANNOT EXIST (a random uuid) and send it to a guarded route. `JwtAuthGuard` verifies the signature FIRST and only then looks the subject up, and it answers the two failures differently: **signature good + subject unknown → *"Session is no longer valid"*, signature bad → *"Invalid token"*.** So the response separates the secrets while the token is useless as a credential and no real account is touched. The same oracle run backwards is what PROVED the rotation: old secret → *Invalid token*, new secret → *Session is no longer valid*.
    - Cost, measured before doing it rather than guessed: **30 distinct accounts had signed in within the 30-day token life** (29 of them operator logins) out of 38 users, and **no stored artefact uses this secret** — all five call sites are session tokens, so nothing but sessions broke. The secret is never printed; the previous value survives only in a timestamped `.env.bak-jwt-*`, which is the one rollback path because `.env` is gitignored.
  - **`PII_VAULT_KEY` IS A PLACEHOLDER TOO AND THAT ONE IS BENIGN — do not "fix" it by rotating.** The vault’s PRIMARY is the key FILE (`PII_VAULT_KEY_FILE`, default `<cwd>/.pii-vault.key`); the env value is only a **decrypt fallback**, so it wraps nothing new and changing it would strand whatever it can still open. The boot line *"local (key file; 3 fallback key(s) for decrypt)"* is what says which is which. **Two placeholders in one file, one critical and one inert — read how each is CONSUMED before acting on either.**
  - **Not exploitable today, and check before repeating that**: zero applications carry an `applicationNumber`, so `findBySymbolAndApplicationNo` can never match and every call returns *"Application no does not exist"*. It goes live the moment real bids carry exchange numbers — which is the same moment the rail does — and **those numbers are not secret** (they are on the ASBA form and in the investor's email). The exposure is setting a real bid to *funds blocked* / *rejected* / *released* and firing a push notification to that investor.
  - ~~**Enable it BEFORE UAT**~~ **DONE 2026-10-22.** The reasoning held exactly as written — cheapest with no integration to break — and the *"fails closed"* half is precisely why the valid-header case had to be proved rather than assumed.
  - **There are TWO `RailCallbackController` files and only one is mounted.** `apps/api/src/modules/rail/rail-callback.controller.ts` carries `@UseGuards(RailCallbackGuard)`; the copy in `packages/rail-adapters/src/callbacks/` has both the decorator AND its guard class commented out and is **not registered anywhere**. Reading the package copy produced a false alarm that the endpoints had no guard at all — **confirm which file `rail.module.ts` lists before judging the auth on a route.**
- ~~**EVERY IPO DETAIL PAGE SCROLLS SIDEWAYS ON A PHONE**~~ **FIXED AND LIVE 2026-10-22, and the guess in the original note was WRONG.** It read *"almost certainly the `.subv2-seg` shape — `max-width: 100%` was the lever there"*. Measured on the page: **`max-width: 100%` on the tab strip changed the overflow by ZERO**, 239px before and after. **A diagnosis that names a previous bug is a hypothesis, not a finding** — this file now holds one case where that lever was the answer and one where it was not, and only measurement tells them apart.
  - **IT WAS THE GRID TRACK, AND THERE WERE TWO CAUSES FOR ONE SYMPTOM.** `.detail-layout` carried a bare **`1fr`**, which is `minmax(auto, 1fr)`, and `auto` as a MINIMUM resolves to **min-content** — so the single mobile column grew to its widest descendant, **600px inside a 347px parent**, and pushed the page. The tab strip already had `overflow-x: auto` AND `min-width: 0`; neither can help, because a track sized to min-content never gives a scroller the chance to scroll. `minmax(0, 1fr)` on both rules took the page from **239px to 16px**. The file already stated this mechanism one screen away, for `.subv2-det-in` — *"a grid track’s automatic minimum is its CONTENT, the row did not scroll, it pushed"* — so this is the second instance, not a new discovery. **Grep for `grid-template-columns: 1fr` before trusting any layout to shrink.**
  - **THE REMAINING 16px WAS A DIFFERENT BUG IN THE SAME BAR.** `.applybar` holds an info block, a watch button, a PDF button and Apply: **~386px of children, gaps and padding inside a 347px bar**, with `.btn` set `white-space: nowrap` so the label could not give way. **Stop at the first cause and you ship half a fix** — the page still scrolled.
  - **FOUR CANDIDATE FIXES ALL MEASURED 0 WITH THE SHIPPED ENGLISH LABEL; ONLY ONE SURVIVED A LONGER ONE.** Shrinking the info block left **124px**, tightening the button padding **146px**, letting the bar wrap cleared it but grew the sticky bar to **119px tall**. Letting the BUTTON TEXT wrap measured **0 both ways**. That test is not academic: the app offers six languages and **four have no dictionary**, so the label grows the day Hindi reaches this string. **Stress a width fix with a longer string, or you have only fixed English.**
  - **AN ELLIPSIS ON THE PRICE WAS TRIED AND REJECTED ON SIGHT.** It cleared the overflow and rendered the band as **"₹1065–…"**. A price with its upper end cut off is worse than a price on two lines — **a fix that cures a scrollbar by hiding data is not a fix**, which is why the verification pass checks for elements whose content exceeds a box that CLIPS it, separately from ones that scroll.
  - **VERIFIED BY SWEEP, NOT BY LOOKING AT THE ONE PAGE**: 18 consumer pages × 1440 and 375, on the published build — **36 of 36 with zero horizontal overflow**, where before exactly one page overflowed at one width. Everything wide now scrolls inside its own box (tab strip 447 in 194, the stepper, the prose, the subscription table wrap), and the only clipped text left is the sticky-tab identity, which has an ellipsis by design.
- **`newipo.finwave.co` IS A WHITE-LABEL PARTNER DOMAIN NOW, NOT A RETIRED ONE** (operator, 2026-10-15). It served the main site until the move to investoyard.com and was briefly treated as a retiring host of ours; it is FINWAVE's domain as of that afternoon, replacing `demo.finwave.co`. **Three places had it listed as ours and each would have broken the partner site differently**: `OUR_HOSTS` in `admin.service.ts` refused it as a `customDomain` (the operator hit this as a **400 on `PATCH /admin/tenants/finwave`**), `OWN_HOSTS` in `apps/web/lib/site.ts` would have served **OUR About/Terms/Privacy/Disclaimer and footer identity to THEIR clients**, and the `canonical-host` 301 in `web.config` would have redirected their whole domain to ours. **A host stops being ours the moment something else is pointed at it — a "retiring" host and a reassigned one are not the same thing, and only the second is urgent.**
  - **THE CANONICAL-HOST REDIRECT MATCHES BY EXACT HOSTNAME, NEVER "anything that is not the apex".** Every white-label partner is served by the SAME IIS site off the SAME `web.config`, so a catch-all redirect there takes every partner domain off the air at once and sends their clients to us. It matches `^www\.investoyard\.com$` and the plain-HTTP upgrade, nothing else.
  - **A PARTNER ORIGIN IS ALLOWED FROM THE DATABASE AND MUST NOT BE HARDCODED IN `DEFAULT_ORIGINS` OR `CORS_ORIGIN`.** An origin in source outlives the tenant row, so a domain withdrawn in admin would go on being trusted. Proved live rather than assumed: with the hardcoded list holding only our own two origins and nothing in `.env`, the newly-set `newipo.finwave.co` was allowed over BOTH schemes while the just-removed `demo.finwave.co` was **rejected** — which only the `Tenant.customDomain` lookup can produce.
- **"CHECK DOMAIN" REPORTED "DNS not pointing here" FOR A DOMAIN WHOSE DNS WAS CORRECT WORLDWIDE, AND IT WOULD HAVE DONE SO FOR EVERY PARTNER FOREVER** (found + fixed 2026-10-15). `checkTenantDomain` fetched the PUBLIC hostname from this server. **This box is behind NAT and its public address is not bound locally, so that connection hairpins and times out** — the abort surfaced as `(20)`, which is `DOMException.ABORT_ERR`, i.e. **the 6-second timeout and not a DNS error at all**. It then reported the timeout as the partner's DNS being absent. dnschecker showed the A record resolving correctly from four continents at the same moment.
  - **DNS AND REACHABILITY ARE TWO QUESTIONS AND THE CHECK NOW ASKS THEM SEPARATELY.** It resolves the A record through a public resolver and compares it with **our own canonical host's** A record — so "points at us" needs no hardcoded IP and survives a move — then probes OUR OWN server over its LAN address with the `Host` header and the TLS **SNI** name set. Neither question needs the request to leave the building. New state **`other-dns`** (resolves, but somewhere else) split out of `no-dns` (resolves nowhere); the admin label map carries both.
  - **`dns.resolve4()` RETURNS ECONNREFUSED FOR EVERYTHING ON THIS BOX.** The configured resolver is **127.0.0.1** and nothing listens there, while `dns.lookup` — the OS resolver, which is what `fetch` uses — answers perfectly. So a diagnostic built on `resolve4` without setting servers reports "no DNS" for every name in existence. **Set explicit public resolvers.**
  - **`ok` ON A PROBE MEANS THE REQUEST COMPLETED, NOT THAT THE SITE SERVED IT**, and the first cut of the fix read it as the latter: the HTTPS probe of an unbound host returned a **404 generated by http.sys**, which is neither the IIS default page nor our markup, so it was classified `other-site` when the truth was `no-binding`. **Judge on what came back across BOTH schemes** — an unbound host gives the IIS default page on 80 (there is a catch-all site there) and an http.sys 404 on 443 (SNI must match a binding, and there is nothing to fall through to). Verified against all four real cases before deploy: partner host → `no-binding`, apex and www → `live`, `demo.finwave.co` → `other-dns`.
- **`demo.finwave.co` — the ONLY tenant with a `customDomain` — SERVES A DIFFERENT APPLICATION ENTIRELY** (found 2026-10-14). It answers `/` with 20 KB of *"ArbTool — Arbitrage Trade Consolidator"* and **404s `/about/`, `/contact/` and `/contact/index.html`**. `/about/` has existed for months, so this is not new and is not an Investoyard regression: **FINWAVE's `customDomain` points at a host that is not this site, so no white-label is actually live today.** The partner page's **Check domain** button reports exactly this (`other-site`). Either point it at the right place or clear the field — a tenant with a domain set looks configured when it is not. Until then, verify the partner branch with the `?brand=<slug>` dev override, which drives the identical renderers.

Set to match, because **43,33,437 is a STATED SHARE COUNT and a stated count does not move with the price**: it works out to ₹699.85 Cr at the discounted cap and ₹663.02 Cr at the discounted floor, round at neither, so it was typed off the RHP rather than derived from a rupee figure.
      - **It was never customer-visible, and that is the useful part.** Every reader prefers `sharesUpper` (the four-readers rule below), so the built page already carried 43,33,437 and never the bad figure — **no rebuild was needed**. A `sharesLower` typo only surfaces in the admin form's Lower column and as a fallback if `sharesUpper` is ever blank. Worth knowing before spending a build on one.
    - **AUGMONT has no `extra.issueSizeCr` at all** — only the ₹3,152 Cr column — so nothing derives from it until that is entered too.
  - ~~**ARMEE's split of 25 / 15 / 7.5 / 52.5 is illegal**~~ **NOT A FAULT — this note was WRONG, corrected 2026-09-30.** It claimed 6(1) sets a QIB **minimum** of 50%. It does not: under ICDR 6(1) QIB is a **CEILING** — "not more than 50%" — with NII ≥15% and Retail ≥35%. Our own rule pack has said `qib: { max: 50 }` all along, and sir's mainboard spec §7.3 says the same, so this file was the lone outlier. ARMEE's 25 / 22.5 / 52.5 satisfies all three bounds and `computeIssue` raises **nothing**.
    - **Corroborated externally too**, so the percentages are not a typo either: a public source gives total 8,000,000 with **QIB 11.69% + anchor 13.31% = 25.00%**, NII 22.5%, Retail 52.5% — our stored split to the digit. (That implies an anchor of ~10,64,800 shares, 53.2% of QIB; NOT entered, and a figure back-derived from a rounded percentage is too soft to feed a published Book Size.)
    - **The lesson is about this file, not the record**: a rule written down here as a floor when it is a ceiling turned a legal issue into a standing action item. When a note and the rule pack disagree, the pack is under test and the note is not.
  - ~~**17 RECORDS' RESERVATION TABLES DO NOT SUM TO THEIR ISSUE SIZE**~~ **TWELVE REPAIRED 2026-10-09, 5 LEFT — and all twelve were ONE fault, not a pile of typos.**
    - **THE QIB ROW HELD THE EX-ANCHOR QIB.** The reservation table is the OFFERED reservation: QIB's row is the whole quota and the anchor is a SUB-ALLOCATION of it, deducted downstream. On twelve records the row carried QIB *net of* the anchor, so the table was short by exactly the anchor — and **that is why the shortfall came in two tiers, −28% and −11%. It is ONE fault**: a 60% anchor off a 50% QIB loses 30% of the issue, off a 20% QIB it loses 12%. The note that called the nine a cluster needing "one RHP" was right that it was systematic and wrong that it needed a document.
    - **`scripts/fix-qib-ex-anchor.js` repaired the NINE carrying `extra.anchorShares`** — AXIOMGAS · ROBOKIDZ · SAIURJA · BMISL · HIMALAYAN · ACMEUNIV · ACMERAIL · TNA · SHREETNB. Dry-run by default; backup in `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\backups\qib-ex-anchor-*.json`.
    - **FOUR INDEPENDENT WITNESSES per record, and the guards are the whole point.** (1) the table is short by EXACTLY `anchorShares` · (2) adding it back reconciles to the issue size exactly · (3) **the reconstructed QIB is LOT-ALIGNED to the share** · (4) the anchor is then 59.93–59.98% of it, just under B09's 60% cap. All nine satisfied all four, and the reconstruction lands at a legal ICDR share of the net offer (~49.9% on seven, **20.0% on HIMALAYAN and AXIOMGAS** — SME QIB is a CEILING, not a floor). **The guards refused three look-alikes** short by 65 · 28 · 1 shares (PERNIASPOP · KANOHAR · AONESTEELS): rounding noise, not an anchor.
    - **LOT ALIGNMENT IS HOW YOU SETTLE A DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN TWO STORED FIGURES.** ROBOKIDZ held a stale `sharesLower` of 13,66,400 against a reconstruction of 13,81,200. 13,66,400 is **1138.67 lots**; the reconstruction is **1151 exactly**. That decides it with no document. SAIURJA is the mirror image — its `sharesLower` ALREADY held the exact full quota, so there the operator had put the full figure in one band and the published *QIB (Ex. Anchor)* figure in the other.
    - **`source` STAYS `operator`, deliberately.** A `derived` row is one `refill-derived-reservation.js` may overwrite from the percentages, and these records have no percentages at all — restamping would licence a later refill to zero the figure the repair just restored.
    - **`--infer-anchor` REPAIRED THE THREE THAT STORE NO ANCHOR** — BLACKOPAL · EIMS · VANS — by taking the shortfall itself as the anchor (operator's call). That loses witness (1), and worse, **`shortIsAnchor` becomes TRUE BY CONSTRUCTION and therefore proves nothing**, so three further guards carry the decision:
      - **all four of qib/hni/hni2/retail must ALREADY carry a count** — the table complete apart from QIB. This is what tells *"QIB is net of the anchor"* apart from *"the table was never finished"*, and it is the guard that stops **PRIORITY**, where only the QIB row was ever entered: inferring there would double QIB and declare an incomplete table done.
      - **the implied anchor must be 25–60% of the reconstructed QIB.** Sixteen records are short with no anchor figure and **fourteen are short by 1–679 shares** — rounding, not an anchor.
      - **the QIB ceiling is checked against 75%, ICDR 6(2)'s limit, not 50%.** MVELECTRO is a legitimate 75% record, so a 50% ceiling would be a rule about one regulation basis dressed up as a rule about legality. PRIORITY's implied QIB is 100% of the net offer and fails either.
    - **ANNU IS THE RECORD THAT GUARD SET EXISTS FOR, and it is why lot alignment is not optional.** Its implied QIB is a plausible-looking **20.00% of the net offer** and would have passed on percentage alone; it is **23,422.82 lots**, and nothing else catches it. Measured catalogue-wide BEFORE the flag was written: exactly three fire, nineteen refused with the reason named.
    - **`anchorShares` IS NOT WRITTEN, not even under `--infer-anchor`.** The QIB count is FORCED by reconciliation and exact lot alignment; the anchor is the INFERRED part, and storing it would make the page print *"Net (post anchor) · actual"* — a label reserved for an allotment read off the intimation letter, which a reader can check against the exchange. These three go on saying **"Gross"**, which is the honest statement of what is known. Verified after: VANS's legend reads Employee 1.67 · sHNI 4.67 · Market maker 5.08 · bHNI 9.33 · Retail 32.67 · QIB 46.58 = **100.00%**, and it is the one record carrying BOTH an employee and a market-maker quota, i.e. the case `--c-mm` was ordered before the employee branch for.
    - **LOANFRONT IS THE THIRTEENTH, AND THE FIRST CAUGHT BEFORE IT OPENED** (2026-10-10, three days ahead of its 13 Oct open). Spotted from an operator screenshot asking why the table would not reach 100%: QIB **14,94,000** where the full quota is **36,94,000**, short by the **22,00,000** sitting in *Anchor shares allotted*. All four witnesses held — lot-aligned at **1847 lots exactly**, 49.88% of the net offer, anchor 59.56% of QIB. **W-ANCHOR now catches this shape at entry**, which is the actual fix; the script is the cleanup.
    - **The remaining 4, all needing a document**: PRIORITY (only the QIB row ever entered) · ANNU (Σ 90%) · SJP and SOLLFEGE (+94.97%, an `OTHER` row equal to sHNI + retail). **RAVITA repaired itself** — the operator re-saved it on 2026-10-10 and its rows now divide the NET offer, summing to 100%.
    - **A TOOL CALL REPORTED AS PERMISSION-DENIED HAD IN FACT EXECUTED.** The first `--infer-anchor --apply` came back as a refusal with no script output; the records were already repaired, and the tells were a **second backup file** on disk and a retry that found **nothing to do**. This file already insists that a script reporting SUCCESS has only proved it issued the write; the mirror holds too — **a report of failure is not proof the write did not land. Check the data, never the command's own account of itself.** The guarded, idempotent, backup-first shape is what made the accidental double-run harmless.
    - **SJP and SOLLFEGE are +94.97% OVER**, each carrying an `OTHER` row **exactly equal to sHNI + retail** — double-counted.
    - **PRIORITY −50.00%** (only the QIB row was ever entered — the long-standing B01 failure) · **RAVITA +5.04%** (its split divides the GROSS where it should divide the net — the 2026-09-25 class, and separate from its carve-out basis fault above) · **NITYAS +0.69%** (the AUGMONT-style overshoot).
  - **RAVITA'S DATES ARE A DAY EARLY AT BOTH ENDS, AND THE COST FALLS ON ITS LAST BIDDING DAY** (found 2026-10-13 from a `catwise failed` on a record with a COMPLETE reservation table, so not the W-SUB blocker). **NSE's own ipomaster says 14 Oct → 16 Oct; we stored 13 → 15.** The probe that settles it is cheap and worth repeating: of the six records open or upcoming that BOTH we and the NSE master carry, **RAVITA is the only one that disagrees** — HDFIRE · LOANFRONT · FUSIONCX · SMT · TONBO all match to the day.
    - **The customer-facing half is the lesser half.** Publishing an issue as *open* a day before the exchange opens it is wrong but loses nothing — there are no bids to lose. **The expensive half is the CLOSE date**: the poller's window is gated on it, so it would stop polling after the 15th while NSE still takes bids on the **16th — the final day, normally the heaviest** — and that day is the W-SUB class of permanent loss.
    - **A FAILED `catwise` ON A RECORD WITH A GOOD RESERVATION TABLE MEANS THE DATES, NOT THE DATA.** The exchange refuses a symbol that is not open yet, which looks identical to a feed fault. **Check our dates against `getIpoMaster()` before suspecting the adapter.**
    - **`scripts/probe-nse-ipomaster.js` is the tool** (read-only) — it diffs every master entry against our dates, names an EARLY close date as the expensive case, and lists EQUITY symbols the exchange knows and we do not. **Worth running whenever a batch of issues is entered**, since it is one command and the fault it catches is irreversible.
    - Operator is fixing the dates (2026-10-13). **`BONFIG` (EQUITY, 15→19 Oct) is on the NSE master and NOT in our catalogue at all** — a genuine gap; the RIGHTS and DEBT entries there we legitimately do not carry.
    - **Normalise the format before comparing a date to NSE's.** `ipomaster` ships **`DD-MM-YYYY`**, so a string compare against our `YYYY-MM-DD` is garbage and silently produced a nonsense "open today" list in the first pass of this very check.
  - **ELEVATE's QIB row was hand-restored** after the poller wiped it — `nseShares: 3,225,142`, `timesSubscribed: 0.07`. Both corroborated (our own pre-loss build and yashasvifinvest). Note the times is `demand/GROSS`; a first attempt wrote the DISPLAYED `0.19` and had to be corrected.

- **W01's tolerance is 0.005 pp, not 0.001** (widened 2026-09-30, operator asked for the impact first). W01 exists to flag an **unusual** NII split — the issuer's business, hence a warning — and at 0.001 it could not tell that apart from an operator writing two decimals. **A 2:1 quota only lands on 2 dp when the NII total divides by 3**, so SKYWAYS 10.03/5.01, VNL 19.33/9.67 and ANNU 26.67/13.33 each missed by **exactly 0.00333 pp — the same figure every time**, which is the tell that it was arithmetic and not data. The only way to silence it was to type `10.0267`.
  - **0.005 is still three orders of magnitude tighter than anything meaningful**: a genuinely off-ratio 20/16 misses by 4 pp, and even a 0.01 pp slip misses by 0.0057 and still warns. **The TRANSPOSITION case is untouched** — Big < Small is B02, a blocker, on its own path, so widening this cannot weaken it.
  - **The general shape, and it cost two rules today**: a check whose tolerance is tighter than the precision of the field it reads will fire on correct data forever. B18 measured a floored count against a percentage; W01 demanded exactness from a 2-dp input. **Ask what the input can actually express before choosing an epsilon.**
  - **Anchor: 22 records carry an ACTUAL allotment, 2 carry only a percentage estimate.** The rest of the 1,260 publish a GROSS QIB Book Size and say so.

Filling it therefore has no published consequence and needs no rebuild. It still decides `primary` *inside the form*: SPECTRAA's ₹118 equals its band cap, which is why setting it moved nothing (primary ₹118, netQibOffered and QIB unchanged, no issues either way). - **`investoyard.com`'s SPF AUTHORISES MICROSOFT 365 AND NOTHING ELSE, WHILE THE PLATFORM SENDS THROUGH SENDCLEAN — so every platform email currently fails SPF on a HARD fail** (measured 2026-10-15, right after the domain move). The records: `v=spf1 include:spf.protection.outlook.com -all`, MX on `investoyard-com.mail.protection.outlook.com`, DMARC present at `p=none`, and **no DKIM selector for sendclean** under `_domainkey` (checked sc · sendclean · s1 · default · k1). The configured sender is `noreply@investoyard.com` over `us1-mta1.sendclean.net:587`. `-all` is an explicit instruction to reject, and with no DKIM there is nothing to align on instead, so Gmail and Outlook will spam-folder or refuse — which reaches the **partner activation link** and the **sponsor-bank unblock request**, i.e. both of the paths that only work if the mail arrives.
  - **THIS IS A FINDING ABOUT AUTHENTICATION, NOT ABOUT WHETHER MAIL SENDS — IT DOES.** Corrected 2026-10-22 after the operator pushed back: **14 messages were accepted between 2026-09-07 and 2026-10-16** (gmail.com, acml.in, sihl.in, nvwealth.in, finwave.co, mysafal.com) and **11 of 12 operator accounts set a password from an emailed link**, so the activation path demonstrably works. **No evidence of a single undelivered mail exists.** What is true is narrower and still worth knowing: the mail is UNAUTHENTICATED for this domain, so it is being delivered on SendClean’s reputation rather than on investoyard.com’s permission.
  - **Two ways out, and the cheaper one needs no DNS at all: send through Microsoft 365**, which this domain's SPF, DKIM and DMARC already authorise. The alternative is adding sendclean's `include:` to the SPF and publishing their DKIM CNAMEs. **Operator's choice — do not change a DNS record or a mail provider without asking.**
- ~~**SMTP IS THE LAST BLOCKER ON REQUIREMENT 1**~~ **IT NEVER WAS — corrected 2026-10-22.** The `email` provider has been configured since 2026-09-28 (`us1-mta1.sendclean.net:587`, from `noreply@investoyard.com`) and has been **sending successfully since 2026-09-07**. **`MessageLog` was the evidence all along and nobody looked** — one query answers it, and instead the claim was carried in this file for three weeks and repeated to the operator as a live blocker. **Before calling something unproven, check whether the system already records the proof.**
  - **WHAT IS ACTUALLY OPEN IS DELIVERABILITY, AND THE OPERATOR DECIDED TO LEAVE IT (2026-10-22).** Measured that day: we send as `noreply@investoyard.com` through `us1-mta1.sendclean.net` = **52.5.155.215**, while the domain publishes `v=spf1 include:spf.protection.outlook.com -all` — which authorises 40.92/15, 40.107/16, 52.100/15, 52.102/16, 52.103/17 and 104.47/17, **none of which contains that address** (checked arithmetically, not by eye). **No DKIM selector exists** (14 tried). DMARC is `p=none` with `rua=dmark@investoyard.com`, and the MX points at Microsoft 365 — so the domain RECEIVES via M365 and SENDS via SendClean.
  - **WHY IT STILL ARRIVES, and the distinction that matters: SPF validates the ENVELOPE sender, not the From header.** If SendClean uses its own bounce domain then SPF passes for THEIR domain and only DMARC alignment fails, which `p=none` makes harmless today. **That is unresolved** — it needs the `Return-Path` and `Authentication-Results` of one delivered message, or one DMARC aggregate report from the mailbox already collecting them. **`sent` in `MessageLog` means SendClean returned 250, nothing more**, so delivery is not something this platform can currently observe.
  - Known costs of leaving it, none of them urgent: **SPF does not survive forwarding and DKIM does**, so a forwarded activation link fails; raising DMARC above `p=none` would stop every mail the same day; and the two sends where a silent failure is expensive are the **partner activation link** and the **sponsor-bank unblock request**, because nobody reports an email they did not receive. **Do not re-open this on a hunch — read a DMARC report first.**
- **BEFORE HANDING THE MOBILE APP TO TEST USERS — audited 2026-10-09, and these are what a tester WILL hit**, in order of how certain they are to be reported as bugs:
  1. **PUSH NOTIFICATIONS DO NOT DELIVER.** FCM/APNs is a `TODO(prod)` stub in `notifications.service.ts`: it logs, then marks `sentAt` as if it had sent, and returns `delivered: devices.length`. **6 device tokens registered, 1 notification ever created.** Every reminder bell and lifecycle alert will look broken.
  2. **NO IPO IS OPEN** (as of 9 Oct; next HDFIRE 13 Oct, FUSIONCX 14 Oct) **and `startBid` is true on only 2 records, both closed.** So the Apply CTA will not appear even when HDFIRE opens unless the operator turns Start Bid on. **Testing apply requires that toggle.**
  3. **An application never moves past `submitted`** — nothing reaches an exchange without NSE UAT creds, so a tester who applies sees no status change, ever.
  4. ~~**FOUR LANGUAGES ARE OFFERED AND UNTRANSLATED**~~ **HALF-CLOSED 2026-10-23.** The four without a dictionary are now **disabled and marked**, so none of them silently renders English, and **the choice persists** (SecureStore — AsyncStorage was never added). **What remains is the translation itself**, which needs a translator: `en` and `hi` carry **157 keys each** (not the 120 this line used to claim) against an app that has grown well past them.
  5. **Hindi covers 120 keys** against an app that has grown far past that: the demat panel, the UPI change, the unblock request and the Application-wise toggle are all English-only.
  6. **`apps/mobile/.env` is GITIGNORED.** It holds `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=https://newipoapi.finwave.co/api`, so Expo Go from this machine is fine — but an **EAS build from a clean checkout loses it** and falls back to the dev machine's LAN IP or `localhost:3000`.
  7. **Family apply loops one applicant at a time**, so a mid-batch failure leaves the earlier applications created. The bulk endpoint exists for exactly this and nothing calls it from either UI.
- **Untested**: registrar upload end-to-end (ALTREJ.DBF / MOLBIO XLSB + one archive→restore cycle), Excel catalog import end-to-end, **partner self-onboarding end-to-end** (apply → approve → activate), **GMP entry**, **message log + template test send** (the OTP path is the one that matters — it bypasses `MessagingService`), **the SME single-exchange fix**, MANIPAL blank on a live IPO, WhatsApp channel URL not yet configured, reminders/bells, first news post, mobile device walk-through. **Partner API surfaces are exercised** (2026-09-16): a partner-issued key has been printing real forms, the Print report + View modal work end-to-end, the docs Test button runs a dry-run through the real engine.
- ~~**SMTP is not configured yet**~~ **STALE — it HAS been configured since 2026-09-28** (re-checked 2026-10-01): the `email` provider is enabled with host `us1-mta1.sendclean.net:587`, username `smtp63388571`, from `noreply@investoyard.com`, secret set. **Configured is not the same as proven** — true as written, and it HAS since been proven: see the corrected note above.  A real approval email has since been sent and received; what remains untested is deliverability under a stricter DMARC policy, not the send itself.
- **The `chatbot` provider secret is the ONE that predates the vault swap and may not decrypt** (flagged 2026-09-28, still unactioned). Every other provider was re-saved on or after 31 Aug — `gmpfeed` 31 Aug · `sms` 1 Sep · `email` and `ai` 28 Sep — but `chatbot` has not been touched since **2026-07-22**, so its secret was sealed under whatever key was live that day, which is not necessarily the Aug 29 original now in `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\pii-vault.key`. **`updatedAt` is the cheap test for this class of fault**: any encrypted-at-rest row older than the last key change is suspect. Re-saving the key in admin → System Settings → Provider Keys fixes it in one action; nothing reads it until the WhatsApp bot runs, which is why it has gone unnoticed.
- **Before publishing the 2,267 historical rows**: `GET /ipos` needs `from`/`to` date params so `/ipos/archive` can page server-side — it filters client-side today, which is fine for a few hundred rows but not the full catalog.
- EAS Android build (needs an Expo account — **the icon, adaptive icon and splash landed 2026-11-04**, so that half is no longer a blocker); native Hindi pass on the newer mobile screens.
- Human actions: counsel sign-offs (Terms/Disclaimer drafts, consent wording, the raw-PAN allotment store), NSE UAT creds (**the single thing blocking the whole bidding surface** — see the rail section), native Hindi review.

## IPO entry (sir's spec — NARROWER fix shipped; full rebuild NOT started)
- Docs: `docs/ipo-entry-spec.md` (authoritative) · `…-sequence-and-public-contract.md` · `…-implementation-brief.md` (8-phase plan) · **`docs/ipo-entry-phase0-survey.md`** (survey — read first).
- **SCOPE — say this out loud before anyone reviews the screen.** The operator chose a *narrower structural fix* over the 8-phase rebuild. Sir reviewed against the full spec and found it incomplete — correctly. Anyone measuring this screen against the docs must be told which scope is in force.
- **Shipped**: `computeIssue()` + `issueRules.ts` in `packages/shared-types` (pure, 29 tests, reproduces the spec's §9 MVELECTRO fixture exactly: 20,069 / 717 / 287) · all three derivation copies converged on it via one `issueInputsFor()` mapper · Share Count / Category Remark / Require for 1X read-only · inline validation · exchanges · carve-outs · ISIN checksum · Fresh/OFS · mechanism + regulation basis · tabs in spec order (Pricing BEFORE Offer & Reservation) · NCD columns, Shares Size grid, Remarks 2, maxAmtRetail dropped · `noOfApp` → `applicationsReceived`.
- **`derived.issues[]` carries the spec’s rule codes** — `{ code, severity, message, field }`, returned by `computeIssue()` itself, NOT assembled at each call site. **B01 · B02 · W01 · B09 · B10** live there; the form only renders them (its old `B-QIB`/`B-RET`/`B-NII` copies are gone). **FOUR stay in the form** (W-SUB 2026-10-06, W-ANCHOR 2026-10-10), as post-conditions on data the engine cannot see: **B04**, which should be unfalsifiable since Step 3 absorbs the residual, and **E-RESV** (2026-09-26), which fires when a record holds an employee OR shareholder quota in the reservation table with that carve-out empty — the engine is handed the table already stripped of both rows, so by the time it runs there is nothing left to complain about.
- **B04 WAS A FALSE BLOCKER ON EVERY COUNT-ONLY RECORD, and it took an operator screenshot to notice** (fixed 2026-10-10). It is a post-condition on the ENGINE's own split, and the engine maps PERCENTAGES — so a record stating its reservation as COUNTS gives it no categories, this summed them to **0**, and the screen blocked with *"Fix before publishing: Category shares total 0 but the net offer is 74,06,000"* on a record where nothing was wrong with that. **24 records, all SME**, with nothing the operator could do about it — which is how a blocking banner is trained into background noise, the same failure as a false OVERWRITE badge on the card parser. It now guards on `categories.length > 0`, exactly as **B01 counts the percentages before complaining about their sum. An empty set is not a violation.** The table's own reconciliation is not lost: it is the `Total (issue size)` row.
- **W-POLL — the per-issue poll window will not do what it says** (2026-10-21). Fires on an inverted pair, which the poller discards whole in favour of the global window, and on an hour outside the sweep's **07:00–22:00** envelope, which is accepted here and then never acted on. Both fail SILENTLY without it: the operator sets a window, sees no error, and loses the hours they were buying — and an unpolled bidding hour cannot be recovered. The message comes from `pollWindowProblem` in shared-types, beside the constants `windowFor` enforces, so the screen cannot explain a rule the API does not apply. A WARNING, because a bad window costs the default and that is not worth refusing an otherwise good record over.
- **W-ANCHOR — the QIB row looks like it holds the POST-ANCHOR figure.** Fires when the table is short by EXACTLY `extra.anchorShares`, names what QIB should read, and is a WARNING because the engine cannot prove which figure was meant. **The test being the shortfall to the share is what keeps it quiet on an ordinary incomplete table — a record missing a row is short by THAT ROW, not by the anchor.** It exists because twelve records were repaired on 2026-10-09 and a THIRTEENTH was caught being entered the same way the next morning: **thirteen is a systematic trap, not thirteen slips**, and the number in front of the operator is the *QIB (Ex. Anchor)* line every information site publishes.
- **W-SUB — the form warns when an issue that has NOT CLOSED YET has no reservation split** (operator ask, 2026-10-06, after ten SME issues closed with nothing). It is the most expensive omission on the screen and **the only one whose cost is irreversible**: with no percentages the poller has no denominator, so it fetches the exchange's demand and throws it away on every tick, and a bidding day that passes cannot be recovered afterwards — the usual rescue, `nseShares ÷ timesSubscribed`, needs a row that was written. See the poller section for the mechanism.
  - **A WARNING, not a blocker, deliberately**: an issue is often entered before its RHP is out and the percentages genuinely are not known. Blocking the save would mean the record is not entered at all.
  - **It needs a CLOSE DATE to fire**, which keeps it quiet on a half-typed new record and on the ~1,200 closed historical rows where the data is already gone and the warning would be pure noise.
  - It is a FORM rule, not an engine one — the third alongside **B04** and **E-RESV** — because "has this issue closed yet?" is a question about today, which `computeIssue()` is never asked.
- **W04 · W05 — two rules added 2026-09-30, both WARNINGS, both calibrated against the catalogue BEFORE they were written.**
  - **W04, the minimum application VALUE.** A mainboard bid lot belongs between **₹10,000 and ₹15,000** at the primary price, which makes it the cheapest guard there is against a mis-keyed lot size — the class of error that let SPECTRAA publish ₹1 Cr. **470 of the 471 mainboard records already sit inside the band**, and the ONE exception is an **FPO** (Adani Enterprises, lot 4 × ₹1,638 = ₹6,552), which is priced against a listed market price and not under the IPO minimum-bid rule. So the check is scoped to `instrument: 'ipo'` — a new `IssueInputs` field — and **without that gate its only firing would have been a false positive**. It is **silent on SME**, where the individual bid is two lots and no rupee band expresses it; `lotValueRange` is simply absent from those packs, the same way B11/B12 go quiet outside a known holiday year. Fires on **0 of 986** derivable records.
  - **W05, the shareholder reservation cap** — ICDR's 10% of the issue size. **No record in the catalogue carries a shareholder carve-out**, so this fires on nothing and is a guard for the historical import, not a repair. A warning rather than a blocker on purpose: the cap is well attested, its BASIS (issue size vs post-issue capital) is worth the operator confirming, and the message names the basis so that judgement can be made from the screen. **The EMPLOYEE cap deliberately has no rule** — ICDR measures it against POST-ISSUE CAPITAL, which the engine is never given, and a check against the wrong denominator is worse than none.
  - **Its own test caught the round-vs-floor trap, again.** W05 first divided by `primary.totalOfferShares` — the ALLOCATION total, floored to lot — so a reservation of *exactly* the cap read as **10.00001%** and fired. `ScenarioResult` now carries **`totalOfferOffered` / `netOfferOffered`** beside the allocation pair, as `netQibOffered` already did. Even then the tolerance is **one LOT, not a float epsilon**, because `totalOfferOffered` still inherits a floor when the offer is described by LEGS alone with no stated count and no ₹ total. **Before comparing any two figures here, check which basis each one is on.**
- **B02 enforces the NII split as a RATIO, not as `10` and `5`.** `RulePack.niiSplit` used to be `{big:10, small:5}` and was never read by anything; it is now `{big:2, small:1}` and drives the check. Absolute per-row bounds would be wrong: ICDR sets a *floor* on NII under 6(1) and a *ceiling* under 6(2), not an exact figure, so a 6(1) issuer may legally offer 24/12 — which a hard `max: 10` would reject. `bounds` is now `Record<string, CategoryBound>` keyed by category, plus the pseudo-key **`nii`** for the combined quota (a 20% NII split correctly 2:1 looks fine row by row and is caught only in aggregate). **Big < Small blocks** (the transposition, a data-entry defect that published wrong figures on three records); an off-ratio split that is not transposed is **W01, a warning** — an unusual split is the issuer's business. B01 *has* to: Step 3 absorbs the floor-to-lot residual into one category, so a table summing to 99.9% still satisfies `Σ shares == net_offer_shares` and every post-condition passes — the shortfall is silently handed to the residual holder. Checked only in the admin form (as it was), the Excel importer and the API derive a clean-looking wrong answer. **B09/B10 don't stack**: a fixed-price pack caps the anchor at 0, so reporting both would tell the operator to *reduce* a percentage they need to *remove*.
- **Anchor % of QIB is now an input** (`extra.anchorPct`, Offer & Reservation tab) with anchor shares / net QIB derived beside it. The net-QIB readout is `anchor.netQibOffered` (published basis) while the MF sub-splits stay on `netQibShares` (allocation basis) — see the two-figures rule under Subscription surface. Before this the rule pack's `anchorMaxPctOfQib: 60` existed with nothing to compare against — B09 was unreachable from every surface.
- **Derived values are no longer persisted.** `shareResv[k]` stores `{on, pct}` only — `count` / `req1x` / `remark` are stripped on save AND on load, so a legacy record sheds them the first time it is opened. `extra.retailCutOff` is no longer written. `admin/catalog/view` was the last reader of all four and now derives from the same engine; it also stopped reading `extra.sharesSize`, which the form had **stopped writing** when that grid was dropped (a dangling read that rendered stale rows on old records and nothing on new ones).
- **Working days come from a REAL NSE calendar** — `packages/shared-types/src/holidays.ts`, the CM (equity) segment of `nseindia.com/api/holiday-master?type=trading`, each date carrying the reason it is closed. **Regenerate every January**: NSE only ever serves the current year, and `HOLIDAY_YEARS` is what **B11/B12 check before they dare to BLOCK** — outside those years they fall back to counting weekends and say so in the message, because a blocking rule that is guessing stops an operator entering a valid issue. Why it was worth doing: close Mon 30 Mar 2026 → list Mon 6 Apr is **seven calendar days and exactly T+3**, because Mahavir Jayanti (31st) and Good Friday (3rd) both fall in between. Counting weekdays called that T+5 and flagged a correct issue. (NSE's homepage 403s a server-side fetch but the API itself answers 200.)
- **What the entry screen can and cannot take** (audited 2026-09-30 when the operator asked whether the team can now add any type of IPO). **Can**: mainboard and SME · book-built and fixed-price · ICDR 6(1) and 6(2) · Fresh and OFS legs in ₹ OR shares · employee / shareholder / policyholder / market-maker carve-outs · anchor as an estimate or an actual allotment · **FPO** (4 in the catalogue; W04 exempts them from the minimum-bid band). **Cannot**, and both are worth knowing before someone tries:
  - **REIT and InvIT are in the `Instrument` enum but have NO rule pack.** `packFor` falls back to `mainboard/book_built/icdr_6_1`, whose bounds (QIB ≤50 · NII ≥15 · Retail ≥35) are not how a REIT allocates — so a REIT would validate against IPO rules and look fine. Latent only because the **IPO Category master holds just two rows** (Mainboard, SME), so the picker cannot reach them. **Add the pack before adding the category, not after.**
  - **Buyback and standalone OFS are not modelled at all** — the Excel importer counts and parks those sheets. Still the pending decision below.
- **Start Bid / Start Print / Is Active stay INDEPENDENT toggles** (operator decision, 2026-08-29). The status machine is not coming for them; Review & Publish shows every failing check beside the toggles but never overrides the operator. Do not re-propose gating them.
- **Deliberately NOT done, and why**: the status machine (replacing Start Bid / Start Printing / Is Active — **18/16 consumer files incl. the partner Print API**) and the entity tables (per-category bid windows — **dates are read in 15 API files** — typed intermediaries, structured financials/objects). Both are far cheaper BEFORE the real catalog is entered.
- **Regulatory rupee thresholds live ONLY in `issueRules.ts`**, as three named exports: **`RETAIL_MAX_AMOUNT`** (₹2 L — retail ceiling, cut-off eligibility, shareholder cap) · **`SNII_MAX_AMOUNT`** (₹10 L — above it is B-HNI) · **`UPI_MANDATE_MAX`** (₹5 L — UPI cap, a PAYMENT rule) · **`ASBA_SYNDICATE_ABOVE`** (₹5 L — the resident-vs-syndicate ASBA form split, a FORM-TYPE rule). An audit on 2026-08-27 found them retyped at **ten** sites; all ten now import. **`apps/api` gained a `@investoyard/shared-types` dependency** to do it — it had none before; both packages are CommonJS/ES2021 and the root workspace symlink resolves under iisnode (proven by boot test, and `@investoyard/rail-adapters` already worked the same way).
- **Grep with underscores AND without.** `200_000` evades a `200000` search — that is how two of the sites stayed hidden through the first pass, and how the sweep later turned up four MORE than the audit had catalogued (`ASBA_RETAIL_LIMIT` in the API, plus `upiCap` fallbacks in `web/lib/tenants.ts`, `mobile/lib/api.ts` and `mobile/app/apply/[symbol].tsx`).
- **`DEFAULT_BID_RULES` must stay numerically identical** across any change here — it drives live apply validation. Assert it (`{retailCap:200000, bhniAbove:1000000, syndicateAbove:500000, upiCap:500000}`), don't eyeball it.
- **Still NOT what the brief asks for**: it wants **seed config** — a versioned table with `effective_from`, admin-editable. `issueRules.ts` is *source*, so a SEBI amendment remains a deploy, not a config edit. That is Phase 1 and is not built. The percentage bounds (`75`/`15`/`10`/`50`/`35`) also still live in the `RULE_PACKS` literal.
- **Three of the 7 records with a reservation table now fail validation** (2026-08-27, all invisible until the rules existed). **ARDEE** raises both of its faults by itself now — retail 15% against the ICDR 6(1) floor of 35%, *and* Big 15 / Small 20 transposed — which is exactly why `fix-hni-swap.js` refused to touch it. It still needs the RHP, but the screen states the diagnosis. MVELECTRO and HORIZON, the two that script did repair, come back clean.
- **Two live records fail validation, and as of 2026-09-26 they fail on DIFFERENT rules** — look for the right one. **AUGMONT** raises **E-RESV**, not B01: its 0.691% employee row is now excluded from the split, so Σ reads a clean 100.000% and B01 passes. The fault is unchanged — a 0.691% **employee quota sits in the reservation table when it belongs in the carve-out** (spec §2.5: carve-outs come off the top *before* the percentage split) — but the diagnosis moved from "the percentages overshoot" to "this quota is read nowhere", which is the more honest statement. AUGMONT is one of the 3 fully-derivable records, so it published figures with that overshoot absorbed into QIB for as long as the row was counted. **PRIORITY** still fails **B01** at Σ = 50% — only the QIB row was ever entered, so QIB absorbs the whole net offer and every derived number on it is wrong. Both need the operator, not a script.
- `floor_to_lot` everywhere with residual absorption; `ceil` for applications-to-reach-1× and `floor` for allotment capacity — two different numbers the old code conflated. `Math.round` under-reported the first.
- **HNI Big/Small**: `hni` = Big, `hni2` = Small; SEBI gives Big the larger two-thirds of NII. Three records were transposed — MVELECTRO + HORIZON repaired by `scripts/fix-hni-swap.js` (**dry-run by default**, only touches a known-safe signature); ARDEE flagged, not rewritten (its split also breaks the retail floor — needs the RHP). The form now warns when Small > Big.
- **Two figures used to be invented and printed as fact**: Fresh/OFS was `issueSize × 0.85 / 0.15`, and the API wrote `['NSE','BSE']` for every mainboard issue regardless. Both are real inputs now; the exchange guess survives only as a fallback for the Excel importer.
- `Ipo.status` is effectively dead (all rows `upcoming`; status derived at read time by `effectiveStatus()` / `stageOf()`), and `Ipo.reservations` (quota NAME list) is NOT `extra.shareResv` (the percentage table). `Ipo.issueSize` is RUPEES, `issueSizeCr` is Cr — mixing them is a 10⁷ error.

## Messaging: delivery log + test sends
- **Every send is logged** — SMS, email, WhatsApp — from the **LEAF adapters** (`SmsService.send` · `EmailService.send` · `WhatsappService.graphSend`), never from their callers. OTP calls `SmsService` DIRECTLY and bypasses `MessagingService`, so logging at the caller would miss the messages most worth tracing. `MessageLogService.record()` never throws.
- Recipients are stored **masked** (`98XXXX1234`) with `recipientLast4` as the search key — a support tool, not a contact list. Changing that is one line in `maskRecipient()` but should be a deliberate DPDP call.
- Admin → System Settings → **Message Log**; **Test** button per template shows the rendered body beside the provider's reply and the DLT template id (a DLT rejection is nearly always a text mismatch).
- Nothing is backfilled — the log starts at the deploy.
- **A ROLE IS IDENTIFIED BY `(tenantId, name)`, NEVER BY NAME ALONE — and three places resolved it by name** (operator report + fix, 2026-10-16: *"you always create Roles - Admin for partner's... How I identify which admin belong to which partner?"*). Every partner's admin role is called `Admin`, so the Roles screen showed one indistinguishable `Admin` row per partner: `listRoles()` returned every role on the instance and **dropped `tenantId` from the response**, so the identifier was half-missing before the UI ever saw it. The screen now carries an **Organisation** column and the API scopes the list to the caller — **15 roles → 2** for every partner admin, measured.
  - **THE ESCALATION WAS IN `addMember`, AND `createOperator` IS WHERE TO READ THE CORRECT SHAPE.** `POST /admin/members/:slug` (guarded `users.manage`, which every partner Admin holds) resolved its role with a bare `findFirst({ where: { name } })` — **no tenant filter, no `isSuperAdminRole` check and no `callerScope` at all**, where `createOperator` had carried all of them for months. `'Staff'` therefore resolved to the **PLATFORM Staff role, `scope: 'all'`**, and `callerScope` returns `{superadmin: true}` for ANY role with scope `all`. So a partner admin could attach platform-wide authority — every partner's reports, clients and bids — to any mobile number on their own tenant. **Measured before the fix: all 6 partners, `scope=all 18p superadmin=YES`; after: all 6 resolve to a local role, `scope=own 6p`, 0 partners exposed.**
