The first instinct was the stale-asset trap (`out/` keeps old hashed chunks) and **checking whether the LIVE PAGE REFERENCED that chunk is what ruled it out** - it did. **Grep the pattern you just fixed, then grep what actually shipped.**
- **KNOWN AND NOT FIXED: AT +1 DAY THE PAGE STILL THROWS, AND THE CAUSE IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN ANOTHER LABEL.** Rendering with the clock pinned a day forward **swaps whole IPOs in and out** - HGSINDIALT out, VPFL and ANTALA in - so the content **SELECTION** is day-derived, not only the text. A label fix cannot reach that. **The pragmatic answer is a daily rebuild**, which is the next section. An attempt to attribute it to `compareForList` did NOT reproduce (identical top-16 both days), so more is involved - likely the `isRecent` six-month window and the banner slide choice.
- **A FOURTH LABEL OF THE SAME CLASS REMAINS**: `HeroBanner.tsx:833` renders closes-today / closes-tomorrow / closes-in-N-days from `daysUntil(ipo.closeDate)` during SSR, 4 occurrences baked into the live page.

## The nightly rebuild, and the guard that stops it publishing WIP (2026-11-03)
- **`scripts/daily-rebuild.js` + the `Investoyard-DailyWebRebuild` scheduled task, daily 04:30.** Two measured reasons, and the second is the one that forced it:
  - **DATA GOES STALE.** The export bakes subscription figures, GMP, stage chips and reservation tables for every one of ~2,643 pages, and the deploy cadence is every 2-3 days. A visitor with JavaScript sees the figure corrected a tick later by `useLiveIpo` / `IpoExplorer2`; **a crawler does not re-fetch and reads yesterday book.**
  - **HYDRATION BREAKS THE DAY AFTER ANY BUILD** - the structural fault in the section above. Rebuilding is the only thing that reaches it.
- **04:30 AND NOT 04:00**: both IIS pools recycle on a fixed 04:00 schedule, and the first request after a recycle has been measured at **10.6 s**. 04:30 is after that settles and 5.5 hours clear of the 10:00 poll window.
- **IT REFUSES TO BUILD AGAINST AN API IT CANNOT REACH**, and starts the forwarder itself when it is down - the forwarder runs detached and does not survive a reboot, so an unattended job that dies every time the box restarts would be a job nobody notices has gone. `safeGet` swallows a dead API and that build **exits 0 with a perfect-looking log**, which is how a deploy once baked 6 ipo pages against 2,639.
- **THE CLEAN-TREE GUARD: A DEPLOY READS THE FILESYSTEM, NOT HEAD.** Neither this script nor `publish-web.mjs` holds a single git reference, verified rather than assumed - so WIP left under `apps/web` went live at 04:30 with nobody watching, and **the publish gate catches a build that BROKE, never one that is merely unfinished.**
  - **WATCHED PATHS are what the web build actually READS**: `apps/web` plus the two packages it imports (`shared-types` compiles to dist, `i18n` supplies the dictionaries). **`apps/api` is deliberately absent** - it cannot reach the export.
  - **THE CRLF PHANTOM IS WHY THIS IS NOT ONE `git status` CALL.** `core.autocrlf` is true, so git can report a file MODIFIED whose content diff is **EMPTY** - this file records exactly that on itself. Guarding on status alone would block every rebuild from then on with no diff to show for it, and **a guard that can only fail is worse than no guard**. So an unstaged tracked modification is CONFIRMED against `git diff --numstat` and a phantom is reported and ignored; anything untracked or staged is real whatever the diff says. **A git failure is not a clean tree either** - it refuses, because the whole point is knowing what is about to publish.
  - **PROVED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, because a guard that refuses everyone looks identical to a working one.** Clean tree passes; an untracked file under `apps/web` and a real edit in `shared-types` each refuse as `abort-dirty-tree` naming the file; a phantom built by **adding 49 CRs to `apps/web/lib/site.ts`** (status `M`, diff clean) is ignored by name and let through; `--allow-dirty` passes over a real edit; an unreadable git refuses as `abort-git-unreadable`.
  - **A FIRST ATTEMPT TO BUILD THAT PHANTOM PROVED NOTHING AND WAS LABELLED AS IF IT HAD.** The file already held **zero CR bytes**, so nothing was stripped and status and diff were both simply empty. **The repo stores LF, so a phantom needs CRs ADDED, not removed.**
  - **`--allow-dirty` IS FOR A RUN BY HAND and the task passes no arguments**, verified on the registered task rather than assumed.
  - It runs FIRST, ahead of the forwarder and of anything with a side effect: if the answer is no, nothing should have been started.
- **IT CLEARS `fetch-cache` ONLY and leaves the ~360 MB sibling `webpack` folder alone** - measured intact after a run. It also removes stray `.html` / `.rsc` DIRECTORIES across the **whole** `.next/server/app` tree, not the one path a note happens to name, and clears `out-staging` itself so the Next clear step cannot race. **ONE retry**, because both the `next/font` flake and the EISDIR race have succeeded on the first retry.
- Measured end to end: **174 s, all six gate checks, 2,643 pages, live byte-identical to disk** (sha256), webpack cache intact, one `ok` row appended to `C:\ProgramData\Investoyard\daily-rebuild-health.csv`.
  - **THE FIRST REQUEST AFTER A PUBLISH MEASURED 15.19 s AND THE NEXT 0.087 s** - the documented cold path, not an outage. Do not read one slow response after a deploy as a problem.
  - **THE FIGURES DRIFT WITHIN MINUTES, which is the honest limit of a nightly rebuild.** Minutes after a build, baked against API: HGSINDIALT 165.84 / 166.5 · MAXENGS 501.94 / 510.07 · VECTRAS 2.56 / 2.69 · JOHNSONCAB 19.43 / 21.68. The rebuild bounds staleness to one night and `useLiveIpo()` corrects the rest in the browser; **the crawler is who the nightly run is for.**
- **STILL OPEN AND NEEDING ELEVATION: the task runs as `Interactive`, so it only fires while the operator is logged in.** Fix it with `Set-ScheduledTask` and a `New-ScheduledTaskPrincipal` of `-LogonType S4U -RunLevel Limited`. **The registration failure PRINTED ITS OWN SUCCESS LINE** when first attempted: PowerShell reported the `0x80070005` as a NON-TERMINATING error, so the `catch` was skipped and a *registered as the current user* line appeared directly under the exception. **Verify with `Get-ScheduledTask`, never from the command own account of itself.**

## sync-mobile reported success for a push it did not make (2026-11-03)
- **THREE DEFECTS IN ONE BLOCK, ALL THE SAME SHAPE: it trusted its OWN BYTE COUNT instead of asking git, and reported success for things that had not happened.** The pre-push hook runs this, so each one reaches a monorepo push.
  - **ONE, which blocked a real push.** It gated the commit on `stats.copied || stats.updated || stats.deleted` - its own byte comparison - while **git normalises line endings** (`core.autocrlf` is true here AND in the mirror). The two legitimately disagree on a line-ending-only copy, and **`git commit` exits 1 on an empty commit**, which `execSync` turns into a throw, which exits the script 1, which the hook turns into a **BLOCKED monorepo push with no real change to find.** *Nothing to commit is a no-op, never a failure.* It asks git now, with `git diff --cached --name-only`, after the add.
  - **TWO. It pushed only from INSIDE the something-changed branch**, so a commit left behind by an earlier run whose push failed **stayed unpushed for ever while the hook reported the mobile repo in step.** It now pushes whenever the mirror is AHEAD, whether or not that run committed.
  - **THREE, found while testing and not previously named: the inverse of one.** With the byte count all zero but a **real staged change** in the mirror - a leftover from an interrupted run - the old code took the nothing-changed branch and **left it uncommitted.** Asking git fixes it by the same stroke.
- **THE AHEAD CHECK READS THE LOCAL `origin/main` REF rather than asking the remote**, which is accurate because this script is the only thing that pushes the mirror, and it **keeps the nothing-changed path free of any network call** - so a GitHub blip on the MOBILE remote cannot block an unrelated monorepo push. If the ref cannot be read it pushes anyway: the push is idempotent and a failure there is a genuine out-of-step worth blocking on.
- **WHAT TRIGGERED IT WAS MY OWN TEST, AND THAT IS THE LESSON.** A `git checkout --` reverting a test edit to `packages/shared-types/src/ipoClock.ts` restored it with **CRLF**, because autocrlf writes CRLF on checkout. **The monorepo git called the tree clean - it normalises - so the tree WAS clean from the git point of view, while the file on disk had changed and `sync-mobile` copies BYTES.** So the clean-tree guard built the same afternoon and the thing that blocked the push are **the same phantom approached from two directions: git normalises and a byte copy does not.**
- **PROVED AGAINST A THROWAWAY SOURCE AND A THROWAWAY BARE REMOTE in the scratchpad, so the real mobile repo was never touched** - verified afterwards at `cab33bc`, zero dirty, zero ahead. `TARGET` derives from `SOURCE`, so pinning both in a mechanically-produced copy exercises the real logic with no network: **the same harness shape as the clean-tree guard, and the way to test any commit-and-push path here.**
  - Six branches: nothing changed gives no commit and no push · a **line-ending-only copy explains itself and exits 0 where it used to throw, reproduced at a scale of 137 files** · a real change commits as *Sync from monorepo* with the probe arriving in the pushed blob · a mirror ahead by one pushes its backlog to zero · byte-count-zero with a staged change commits · and **a genuine commit failure still throws, so the hook still blocks when it should.**
  - **A THROWAWAY CLONE HAS NO `user.name` / `user.email`**, so the first real-change test failed on identity and read exactly like a defect in the fix. The real mirror has both. **Check the harness before believing it found something.**
- **TODAY ADDED MORE BADLY-SHAPED CHECKS to the family this file tracks**, and the pattern is identical every time: a check that cannot see what it is looking for reads as a clean result. A grep for the deploy-failed-twice line **returned 0 on an intact file** because the real line is a **template literal with a backtick** while the grep used a single quote · a sha256 comparison of the live page against disk **never ran at all**, because `/tmp` in Git Bash is not the `D:\tmp` that node reads, and the summary around it had already been written as though it had · and one patch script **failed to parse** because a line of its prose quoted a single-quoted literal. **Use the right quote character, grep the compiled output, and re-run a check before reporting what it said.**

## Phase 0 of the Claude Design handoff (2026-11-04)

- **THE OPERATOR SENT A DESIGN PROJECT AND ITS OWN UX QC REPORT, 23 FINDINGS. PHASE 0 IS THE SUBSET THAT DOES NOT WAIT ON A DEVICE OR A DECISION** - six commits, each measured rather than eyeballed, because **React Native cannot be rendered on this box at all** and nothing in this phase was ever seen.
- **THREE OF THE FINDINGS I LISTED AS PHASE 0 WERE NOT OUR WORK AT ALL, and reading the report properly is what settled it.** D1 is *"Orkla India tells three different stories"* across three MOCKUP screens, with a lot size borrowed from a different IPO; D2 asks for the MOCKUP dates to be generated with the T+1/T+2/T+3 logic `lib/ipoStage.ts` already applies; L2 says outright *"fix the mock so review screenshots stay honest"*. **Our app has had no sample data since the mock-free pass**, so there is no fixture here to unify. **A finding about a reviewer's own artefact is feedback to send back, not work to absorb** - and I had three of them on my list before reading past the headline.

### The file's own mobile line was two SDK majors stale
- **IT SAID EXPO SDK 54. The project declares and resolves expo 58.0.19, with react-native 0.88.0 and react 19.3.0.** `scripts/start-mobile.ps1` has been right the whole time **because it READS the range out of package.json and prints it**; the prose was written down once and rotted. **The script measures, the sentence remembered** - this file's own standing rule about counts, applied to a version number.
- **SO THE 2026-10-23 EXPO GO NARRATIVE IN THAT LINE NEEDS RE-CHECKING RATHER THAN FOLLOWING.** It records the store moving to SDK 57 while the project sat on 54 and prescribes installing the SDK 54 build with auto-update off. The project is now on **58, newer than the 57 in that story**, so the advice may be inverted. **Only the PROJECT version was measured here** - nothing was checked about what the Play Store carries today, and that is the question to answer before anyone acts on those instructions.

### C2 - a GMP projection wore the colours of a realised gain
- **THE REPORT UNDERSTATED IT.** In `IpoListCard` the **same `styles.market` block, the same `greenTint` and the same green label** served both the **LISTING RESULT** - a realised outcome - and the **GREY MARKET PREMIUM** - a projection. Same card, same slot, byte-identical, in a block whose own comment calls it *the loudest element on the card*. Green on a projection reads as a promised return, which is SEBI-sensitive and against this project's own **Distribute + inform, don't advise** rule.
- **THE FIX IS GRADUATED, NOT BLANKET, BECAUSE THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE POINT.** Realised figures KEEP green and red: the card's listing result, the listing gain in applications and on Home, the detail listing-performance card, the avg listing gain on the performance screen. **Four indicative sites take neutral ink** - the card's premium band, the GMP screen's pill, the detail panel's rupee and percent rows, and the GMP trend line - and each keeps a SIGN or an ARROW, so direction survives without colour, which is also finding A6.
- **THE NEUTRAL TREATMENT IS ALSO THE MORE LEGIBLE ONE, measured rather than assumed**: slate on slateTint 5.17:1, body 8.65, title 14.82, against the 4.84 and 5.72 of the green and red pairs they replace.
- **MY OWN ENUMERATION MISSED A FOURTH SITE AND THE SWEEP CAUGHT IT** - the GMP trend line in `app/ipo/[symbol].tsx`, found only by grepping for the pattern after fixing three. The broad sweep then classified **every** green and red use in the app as realised, indicative or a status cue, rather than only the lines with *gmp* in them, which is how the fourth should have been found first.
- **TWO VOCABULARY DRIFTS FOUND IN THE SAME BLOCK.** `IpoListCard` hardcoded *GREY MARKET PREMIUM* where `LABEL.gmp` is **Exp. Premium**, and the Insights tile said *GMP Trends* while the screen it opens is titled *Exp. Premium Trends* - **a tile disagreeing with its own destination**. Both read the locked term now. The `GmpDialog` uses of the long name are correct and untouched: naming the thing in the disclaimer is `gmpLong`'s documented job.
- `s.pos` / `s.neg` are RETIRED rather than left unused - GMP was their only caller, and a green/red pair sitting beside an indicative figure is an invitation to re-apply it.

### A1 - muted was the colour of every date, sub-line and disclaimer
- **MEASURED RATHER THAN TAKEN FROM THE REPORT: `#767CA3` is 4.05:1 on white and 3.73:1 on the canvas, against a 4.5:1 bar. `#5C6187` is 5.97 and 5.50.**
- **NOT A BLIND REPLACE, because muted has a real job it passes at.** Of 113 StyleSheet colour uses, **111 are TEXT and move to slate**; the **16 icon props and 18 placeholder colours are untouched**, where the bar is 3:1; and **two stay muted** because they are genuinely DISABLED state - `langTxtOff` / `langSoon`, the row for a language with no dictionary.
- **THE RULE IS NOW WRITTEN BESIDE THE TOKEN IN `theme.ts`, with the measured figures**, because a convention recorded only in a commit message is a convention that drifts back. `mutedTxt` is renamed `subtleTxt`: **a style named after a colour it no longer has is the same class of lie as a comment describing a fix that was only half applied.**

### The icon assets, and Expo cannot read SVG
- **THE APP HAD NO ICON AT ALL.** Measured from the EVALUATED config rather than read off app.json: `icon`, `android.adaptiveIcon` and `web.favicon` were all **undefined**, so every build had shipped Expo's default. That was the long-standing EAS blocker, and it is now on the critical path because a development build is what pins the SDK and makes push notifications testable.
- **EXPO CANNOT READ SVG, and that is from the installed packages rather than from memory**: `@expo/image-utils` is built on **jimp-compact + parse-png**, both raster-only, and `withAndroidIcons` reads `foregroundImage` / `backgroundImage` / `backgroundColor` / `monochromeImage`. The handoff README asks for exactly what that code asks for, which is a good sign both were read rather than assumed.
- **THERE IS NO SHARP AND NO RESVG ON THIS BOX**, so the rasteriser drives headless Chrome over CDP - the pattern this repo already uses to measure built pages - with **`Emulation.setDefaultBackgroundColorOverride`** for the transparent targets. **A plain screenshot composites onto opaque white and silently destroys the alpha the Android foreground depends on**, which is the one failure here that looks fine until it ships as a white square.
- **VERIFIED BY MEASURING PIXELS, because a rasteriser reporting success has only proved it wrote a file.** `icon.png` is **0 clear and 0 partial** - it matters because iOS composites a transparent app icon onto BLACK. `adaptive-icon.png` is **95.2% clear**, so the alpha survived; its art measures **346x410 centred exactly at (512,512)**, worst corner **r=268px against the 313px circular mask** and the 341px guaranteed-visible square, i.e. inside both Android safe zones with 45px to spare. All three brand colours are present in the raster, **which is what proves the SVG painted at all** rather than rendering an empty box.
- **MY OWN ARITHMETIC FOR THAT SAFE ZONE WAS WRONG AND THE MEASUREMENT IS WHY IT DID NOT MATTER.** I estimated a 534px corner radius from the art's own centre-to-corner diagonal; **the circular mask is centred on the CANVAS, not on the art**, and the art was narrower than I guessed. **Derive the figure and you get an answer that is 2x out and still plausible.**
- **THE `splash` KEY IN app.json WAS DEAD AND HAD BEEN FOR SOME TIME.** On SDK 58 `expo-splash-screen` takes its config from **PLUGIN PROPS**; the top-level key is legacy, the plugin was not in the `plugins` array, and **it is not auto-applied either** - absent from prebuild-config's default and unversioned lists. So `resizeMode` and `backgroundColor` were read by nothing and there was no image anyway.
- **THE SPLASH IMAGE IS TIGHT-CROPPED SO `imageWidth` MEANS WHAT IT SAYS.** The plugin scales the whole IMAGE, so on a full 1024 canvas `imageWidth: 200` renders the mark at **68dp** - not what anyone adjusting that number expects. Cropped to 512 with the art filling 79% wide and 93% tall, the same 200 gives a **157dp** mark. **The crop is a viewBox rewrite, not a re-encode**, which is only clean because the art turned out to be exactly centred.
- **THEN PROVED EXPO'S OWN PIPELINE READS THEM, not merely that parse-png can**: every file through `getPngInfo` and `generateImageAsync` at the real Android density buckets and the iOS 1024 - **11 resizes, all exact**. **`expo config` only resolves PATHS and never opens an image**, so a correct config over an unreadable PNG looks perfectly fine.
- **`assets/investoyard-logo.svg` WAS DELETED: dead and misleading.** Referenced by nothing in apps/mobile - the only repo-wide hit is an API comment about the WEB copy - and **monochrome white**, so anyone who did reach for it would get a white logo on a white ground. The real wordmark is `components/logo-xml.ts`, genuinely two-tone with a light variant that keeps the gold.
  - **WHICH CORRECTS SOMETHING I TOLD THE OPERATOR: that our mobile logo had lost its gold. It had not.** I read the dead asset file and reported it as the app's logo **without checking who imported it**. The compiler cannot see an unused ASSET the way it sees an unused function, which is exactly why a dead one is worth deleting.
- **NOT DONE deliberately**: `monochromeImage` for Android 13 themed icons is not in the handoff and is **not invented here** - flattening the mark to a silhouette discards the gold, which carries meaning, so which parts survive is a design decision rather than a derivation. Without it, themed mode falls back to the adaptive icon, which is correct and not broken. The handoff's pre-expanded logo SVGs are also not adopted over `Logo.tsx`'s runtime class-stripping: it works, and **swapping a logo is exactly the change that needs eyes on a device.**
- **GIT STORED ALL FOUR PNGs BYTE-IDENTICALLY and reports them binary** (`- -` in numstat), checked against the working files, because `core.autocrlf` is true here and there is no root `.gitattributes`. **The mirror carries them intact too**, verified by hash in `D:/Investoyard-Mobile` - `sync-mobile` compares with `Buffer.equals` and copies with `copyFileSync`, so binary is safe by construction.

### The status chips were no longer a legend
- **THREE STATUSES DID NOT HAVE THEIR OWN COLOUR, against this file's standing rule that status colour is a COLOUR CODE with `STAGE_TONE` as the single map.** **Awaiting Allotment was `neutral` GREY** - web moved it to teal on 2026-09-02 for a stated reason and mobile never followed - and **Pre Apply and Upcoming BOTH sat on `brand`**, one swatch for two statuses, which is the single thing the legend exists to prevent.
- **AND THE DOCBLOCK HAD BEEN ASSERTING BOTH AS CORRECT** - *"brand Upcoming/Pre Apply"*, *"neutral Awaiting Allotment"* - so anyone checking the drift by reading the comment would have found it documented as the design. Its `gold` note was worse than stale: *"act TODAY - Closing Today and Allotment Out"* is a **fossil of the five-tone semantic grouping that was REJECTED** after it put two different statuses on the same swatch side by side.
- **NOT WEB'S PRE-APPLY HEX, AND THE REASON IS A MEASUREMENT.** Web's own ink `#7565BD` on `#F3F1FB` is **4.32:1 and FAILS WCAG AA**; this chip is 11px bold, which is not *large text*, so the 4.5 bar applies. **`#6356AB` is 5.45:1 with margin and still dE 16.7 from Upcoming's indigo** - web's intent, made legible. Awaiting takes web's own `#E2F2F4` / `#1C6570` at 5.80:1, **dE >= 39.7 from every other status ink**.
  - **WEB HAS THE SAME DEFECT ON ITS OWN `.status` CHIP** (12px/600, `--tone-ink` on `--tone-soft`, so Pre Apply renders at 4.32:1). **Not fixed here** - it is a web change of its own and the operator has it as an open item.
- **MY FIRST SEPARABILITY MEASUREMENT USED THE WRONG INSTRUMENT, and that is the lesson worth more than the hexes.** I compared the two indigos with the **WCAG contrast ratio**, which is luminance-only and says Live green and Closing red differ by **1.21:1** - obviously false. For *"can a reader tell two swatches apart"* the instrument is a perceptual difference in **Lab (dE)**; the same pair is **101.9** there. **A contrast ratio answers legibility of text on a ground, never discrimination between two hues.**
- **THE CHECK THEN FOUND A FOURTH FAULT NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR, and it is A1 again.** The CLOSE family - Closing Today, Closed, Withdrawn - rendered `#D8412A` on `#FCEBE8` at **3.86:1**. That is the **exact figure this file records for web's `--neg` on `--neg-soft`**, the pair web fixed by promoting `--neg-ink`, because mobile had copied web's pair verbatim **and its comment said so**. Mobile took the failing pair and not the fix. It is `#B3372E` now, 5.19:1, dE 17.2 away so it reads as the same red. **Deliberately not `ui.red` `#B3261E`**, which also passes but is a different red - the close family tracks web on purpose. **Every status swatch now clears AA; gold is the thinnest at 4.94.**
- **WRITING A CHECK THAT MEASURES THE WHOLE LEGEND IS WHAT FOUND IT.** It reads the SHIPPED tables rather than restating them, compares the **PARTITION** of `STAGE_TONE` against the mobile engine's - which statuses share a colour must match, and the tone NAMES legitimately differ between a CSS class and a palette key - then measures contrast per swatch and dE between every distinct pair. Two collisions are allowed and **asserted as allowed**: Open Today IS Live on day one, and Closing Today / Closed / Withdrawn are one event caught at different moments.
- **A DEAD `STATUS_TONE` MAP WAS REMOVED from `app/ipo/[symbol].tsx`.** The chip on that screen reads `stageOf(ipo).tone`, so the map had no caller - and it keyed off the COARSE `ipo.status`, which knows nothing of Pre Apply or Awaiting, so it was **a second legend already drifted from the first and waiting for someone to start using it.** The comment above it described a yellow Upcoming that has not existed for months.

### A3 - an 11sp type floor, and the three that cannot have it
- **42 SITES RENDERED TEXT BELOW 11sp**: 2 at 9, 6 at 9.5, 12 at 10, 22 at 10.5 - dates, sub-lines, table headers, stepper labels, a disclaimer and a notification count, on a financial app read by retail investors.
- **NOTHING HERE IS A BLIND BUMP, because a font size change is a REFLOW risk** - the exact trap that wrapped the web IPO card's chip row when the webfont landed five days earlier, which **a page-overflow sweep could not see**, because a chip wrapping inside a flex row is not page overflow.
- **SO EVERY SITE WAS MEASURED WITH `fontkit` AGAINST THE REAL INTER TTFs THE APP LOADS.** It reads the font's own hmtx table, which is the same data RN's text engine shapes with, **so an advance width computed here IS the advance width on the device.** Container widths come from the real styles rather than a guess, at 360, 375 and 412.
- **A FIRST ATTEMPT MEASURED IN HEADLESS CHROME AND ITS `@font-face` NEVER LOADED.** The numbers were plausible and were the fallback font's. It was caught only because the harness compared Inter against serif and sans before reporting anything - **and that check was itself weak**, since headless Chrome can map every generic family to one default. **fontkit removes the whole class of doubt: there is no font to fail to load.**
- **TWO KINDS OF FIX, AND THE FIRST IS THE BETTER ONE.** 15 sites spread `microLabel` and then override its fontSize **DOWNWARD** - and microLabel is already **11.5**, above the floor. So the fix there is to **DELETE the override** and let the shared token apply, not to pick a new number. The remaining 24 are standalone and go to exactly **11**: the floor, and the smallest change that clears it.
- **THREE ARE LEFT BELOW THE FLOOR, each with its measurement written AT the site so the next person does not finish the job and ship the clip:**
  - ~~**`tabBarLabelStyle` stays 10.**~~ **IT IS 11 AS OF 2026-11-04, because the bar was cut to four and the rename to "Applied" took the widest label from 66.6px to 40.9px** — 38.3px of spare in an 83px tab. The reasoning below is why it could not be done at five: The pill is inset left:14 right:14, so a tab is **(screen-28)/5 = 66.4px at 360**, and *Applications* at 11px measures **66.6px**. It fits at 375 and up and not on a narrow Android. **THE HANDOFF CUTS THIS BAR FROM 5 TABS TO 4**, which makes a tab 83px with 16px to spare - so this one is gated on that nav decision, which is the operator's, and not on type at all.
  - **`stepLbl` stays 10.** Four STEPS across a card is 74.0px per column at 360 and *Refund/Credit* at 11px is **74.7px**, with `numberOfLines={1}` - so the floor would ellipsize the word that says what the step IS.
  - **`lotK` stays 10.5**, the one microLabel override left in place. The lotGrid cell is 91px at 360 and **MAX / 130 LOTS ALREADY measures 90.3px at the current size**, with no numberOfLines, so it wraps. **The cell is too narrow for the string it holds: a layout fault, not a type one**, and raising it would deepen a wrap that already happens.
- **THE NOTIFICATION BADGE NEEDED ITS CIRCLE GROWN TOO, which is the kind of thing a text-only pass misses.** The count was the smallest text in the app at 9px, white on red. At 11px Inter wants a **13.3px line box**, which left 2.7px of leading in a 16px circle, so the circle is **18** with a matching radius. Two copies, `index.tsx` and `HeaderIcons.tsx`, **kept in step by hand** like the split UPI input. Measured in passing: **99+ has ALWAYS overflowed 16px** (18.1px at 9px), so that badge has been a pill rather than a circle all along.
- **CHECKED AND FOUND HARMLESS RATHER THAN ASSUMED**: the day-wise panel LOOKED like a six-column table and is a **two-column wrapping grid** (`width: '50%'`), so its headers have 140px each; the allocation bar's segments **already clip by design** (overflow hidden, numberOfLines 1), so raising their label only narrows the band of segment sizes that can show one; and the real day-wise labels are QIB, bHNI, sHNI, Retail, Total, all short.
- **MY OWN FIRST TWO CONTAINER FIGURES WERE WRONG AND THE CHECK IS WHY IT DID NOT MATTER.** I measured a tab as `screen/5`, **missing the pill's 14px inset**, which turned a 0.2px overflow into 8px of slack and would have shipped a clipped tab label; and I assumed the badge circle was a fixed 18 when it is `minWidth: 16`, which GROWS. **Both surfaced only because the next step was to read the real style.**

### A7 - a fixed height around text clips when the reader raises the text scale
- **17 BOXES WRAPPED A FIXED `height` AROUND TEXT.** `minHeight` changes nothing at the default scale and lets the box grow rather than cut the text off - **one of the few accessibility fixes with no visual cost and no reflow risk**, which is why this one is done in full while A4 is not.
- **19 CONVERSIONS, NOT 17, AND THE EXTRA TWO ARE THE WHOLE LESSON. A MODIFIER MUST MOVE WITH ITS BASE.** `Button` renders `[styles.base, small && styles.small]`, so converting base to `minHeight: 48` while `small` kept `height: 40` would have left RN applying BOTH - **a 40px box with a 48px floor is a 48px box** - and every small button on the platform would have silently grown by 8px.
  - **THE CHECK THEN FOUND A SECOND ONE I HAD NOT THOUGHT OF**: `BidControls` layers `tabCompact` (height 26) over `tab` (now minHeight 30), so compact tabs would have become 30. **Found by scanning every style ARRAY in the app** for a converted style mixed with one that still pins a height - not by reading the 17 sites again, which is how the first one was nearly missed too.
- **SQUARE BOXES ARE DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED**: an equal width and height is an icon, a dot or an avatar, where the fixed height is correct and a minHeight would let a stray child stretch it. 21 of those, plus 21 boxes that do not centre their content.
- **THE STRETCH QUESTION WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN WAVED AWAY, because it is the one way this could change layout**: in a `flexDirection: 'row'` the cross axis is height, the flex default is `alignItems: 'stretch'`, and **an explicit height is currently the only thing stopping a chip from growing to match a taller sibling.** Every one of these parents maps over HOMOGENEOUS children, so the row's height is the chips' height either way and the swap is a no-op at the default scale. The five whose parent could not be resolved were read by hand: each holds exactly one `Text`.
- **A `height` INSIDE `shadowOffset` IS NOT A LAYOUT HEIGHT, and the checker flagged one until it was told so** - `tabOn` carries `shadowOffset: { width: 0, height: 2 }`. **One real conflict and one false positive out of two reports**, which is the right ratio to investigate rather than to act on.
- **THE LEFTOVER CHECK WAS ITSELF SELF-TESTED by feeding it four styles that DO still pin a height**, and it flagged all four. **Its all-clear is therefore a real one rather than a broken search returning nothing** - this file's standing rule, applied to a check written the same hour.

### L1 - the tab bar scrolled by a magic number
- **OURS WAS ALREADY A HORIZONTAL ScrollView AND ALREADY SCROLLED THE ACTIVE TAB INTO VIEW** - the report's *"last tab clipped by 55px"* is the static mockup, not the app. **What was actually wrong is HOW it scrolled**: `idx * 92`, a hardcoded stride, while every chip is sized by its own label.
- **MEASURED**: the chips run **70px (Trends) to 111px (Exp. Premium)**, nothing like a uniform 92, and the content is **915px against a 360-412px viewport**. The stride is short by up to **78px**, and always short, so the chip lands further right than the -60px of left context intends.
- **AND IT IS A WRONG CONSTANT RATHER THAN A LIVE BUG, which is worth stating precisely because the two deserve different urgency.** Checked across all three section orders, all 8 subsets the `sections` filter can produce, three widths and every chip - **540 cases, and in NOT ONE is the selected chip actually outside the viewport.** The -60px nudge and the clamp absorb the error. **It becomes a real bug the moment a label grows or a tenth section appears**, which is the argument for fixing it now rather than for having fixed it sooner.
- The fix measures each chip's x with `onLayout`, **the same idiom this file already uses for each section's y**, so the two read alike. The old stride survives as the fallback for the first paint, before onLayout has fired - refusing to scroll at all would be a regression.
- **NOT DONE: the right-edge fade L1 also asks for.** It is purely visual, **its colour has to match whatever sits behind each row - and the six horizontal rows in this app sit on the canvas, on white AND on an indigo card**, so one fade colour cannot serve them. That one wants the dev build.

### A4 - a 44dp target on the axis each control leaves free
- **29 SITES, AND THE BOUND MATTERS MORE THAN THE SLOP.** `hitSlop` costs no layout, which is exactly what makes it look like the easy answer - and **it extends the target OUTSIDE the view, so an expanded target that reaches a SIBLING fires the wrong control**, because RN gives the overlap to whichever view is on top. **A mis-tap is strictly worse than the small target it was added to fix.** So the safe slop per axis is the distance to the nearest INTERACTIVE neighbour on that axis, measured per site rather than chosen once: **4 symmetric** (the only tappable in its container, so neither axis has a competitor and both reach 44) · **18 vertical-only** (a chip among row siblings - horizontal would cross the row's gap, vertical has nothing above or below) · **7 capped at `floor(rowGap / 2)`** because the row WRAPS, so vertical slop meets the wrapped line - reaching 32 to 42 rather than 44, short but strictly better than the box and **incapable of mis-firing** · **1 untouched on purpose**: `IpoListCard`'s topic chips sit inside `<Card onPress>`, which renders `PressableScale`, so ANY slop there steals taps meant for the card.
- **THAT SPLIT IS COUNTED FROM WHAT LANDED, AND MY FIRST ATTEMPT AT IT WAS WRONG.** I wrote **19 vertical and 6 capped** - from the PLAN, where the stacked `sug` was added by a later patch and filed under the vertical SHAPE. **The shape is not the classification**: `{{ top: 3, bottom: 3 }}` on a 32px box reaches 38, so it is CAPPED whatever its form. Measured against the code: **4 / 18 / 7 / 1**, with the capped seven running 32 to 42dp and nothing else short of 44. **A count taken from the plan instead of from the result is the same defect as every rotted figure in this file** - and the commit message carries the wrong pair, because it was written before anything counted it.
- **THREE OF MY OWN DETECTORS WERE WRONG BEFORE ONE WAS RIGHT, and it is the pattern this file keeps recording: a check that cannot see what it is looking for returns a clean answer.** The first scanned upward for an opening tag and **never checked whether that tag had CLOSED** - 7 false "inside a pressable card". The second counted opens against closes and reported the same 7, now as `Button > Button > Button`, because its attribute pattern `\{[^{}]*\}` **cannot match the nested braces** in `style={({ pressed }) => [s.x, pressed && { opacity: 0.8 }]}` - so the element never matched as self-closing and the tag never popped. **REGEXING JSX ATTRIBUTES IS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT.** The third walks by INDENTATION, which this codebase formats consistently, and answers 2 - of which `Stack.Screen` on calendar's view toggle is **still** a false positive, because that control lives in `options.headerRight`, a render function. **The real answer is ONE, and I had already confirmed it by READING line 60 of the file rather than by running anything.**
- **THE VERIFIER FOUND A SITE THE PLAN HAD MISSED, by reading a DIFFERENT Pressable than the patch had touched and reporting NO SLOP.** `s.sug` serves **two** Pressables - the two branches of a ternary, so only one ever renders - in genuinely different containers: one inside `<View style={{ flex: 1, minWidth: 210, gap: 6 }}>`, which **states no `flexDirection` and therefore defaults to COLUMN**, so those suggestion rows STACK 6px apart; the other in a wrapping row with gap 8. The stacked one needed its own cap of 3, by the same mechanism as a wrap gap - a sibling that distance away on that axis. **It was never in the 29**: its container was one of the 19 the classifier could not resolve, and reading the code resolved it. **TWO IDENTICAL-LOOKING CHIPS, ONE WITH SLOP AND ONE WITHOUT, IS WORSE THAN NEITHER**, which is why that find mattered more than the site count.
- **THE CHECK THAT EARNS ITS KEEP IS THE OVERLAP ONE, never the count**: can any two EXPANDED targets now reach each other, across a row gap, a wrap gap, or a pressable ancestor? **None can** - and that is the only property here a measurement can actually settle.
- **THE SECOND SWEEP'S "UNDER 44" LIST IS NOT A FINDING LIST, and saying so is half the value of running it.** It enumerates every tappable in the app rather than my own table - **93 tappables, 56 now reach 44dp, 46 carry a hitSlop** - but its box resolution takes the first style with a height in a 12-line window, **and that can be a CHILD's style rather than the button's.** All four of the smallest entries were read and **all four are artefacts**: `PickerField`'s `grabber` is a decorative `<View />` (the Pressable there is the full-screen backdrop), `BidControls`' `shaCheck` is the 20px box inside a text-driven row, and `HeaderIcons`' `badge` and `ipos`' `seg` are a child and a container picked up instead of the button. **Checking them is what stopped four non-faults being reported as four faults.**

### What Phase 0 leaves open
- **A4's REMAINING 18 SITES ARE "CONTAINER NOT RESOLVED", WHICH IS NOT THE SAME AS "STACKED ROW"** - labelling them that way in the first pass overstated what was known. One of the original 19 was resolved simply by reading it (the second `s.sug`, above); the rest need per-site reading or a thumb on a device. **Two earlier "findings" there were my own arithmetic**: the `performance.tsx` and `gmp.tsx` list rows were estimated at 38dp on a one-line assumption and actually measure about **57dp**, so they were never faults, and padding them would have been damage.
- **THE WRAP-CAPPED CHIPS IN `BidControls` WANT A THUMB FIRST.** They reach 32 to 42dp rather than 44, and that is the one group where measuring cannot tell you whether it is enough.
- ~~**THE TAB BAR'S 5-TO-4 CUT IS AN OPERATOR DECISION**~~ **DONE 2026-11-04 on the operator's instruction, and the 11sp floor on the tab label came with it.** See the tab-bar section below.
- **WEB'S PRE APPLY CHIP IS FIXED** (4.32:1 → 6.72:1, and both dark themes with it) — see the QC section below. What it left behind is a question rather than a defect: **`--brand-500` now has a dark value equal to `--brand-700`**, because the dark scale is compressed and 600 sits too close to Upcoming. Two scale tokens sharing a hex in one theme is deliberate here and would be worth revisiting if the dark palette is ever filled in properly.
- **TOOLING, all paid for today**: `getPngInfo` takes a **PATH and is ASYNC** - passing a Buffer and not awaiting it printed `undefined` rather than failing, which is the quieter of the two wrong answers · **POSIX ERE has no lookahead**, so a `grep -E` with `(?![0-9.])` printed nothing and read as an all-clear until a `perl` run answered properly · and **the shell ate a backslash in a heredoc twice more** (`\\s` collapsing to `\s`, which in a JS string is just `s`), each time caught only by a count guard that refused. **The Write tool plus `node script.js` remains the only shape that does not do this.**

## The rest of the QC report — C1, U4, U5, A5/A6/A8 (2026-11-04)

> **READ THIS BEFORE THE FOUR SUB-SECTIONS: THE WEB HALF OF U4, U5 AND A6/A8 WAS REVERTED ON THE OPERATOR'S INSTRUCTION, SO EVERY BULLET BELOW THAT DESCRIBES A WEB FIX AS DONE IS DESCRIBING SOMETHING THAT NO LONGER EXISTS.** The task was the MOBILE design handoff; I took its findings, went looking for the same shapes on web, and changed 16 web files nobody had asked about. **17 files are back at `3b021e5` byte-identical.** The reasoning stays recorded because the defects are real and will be found again — but **the code is not there.** See *The web revert* at the end of this file for what came back and the two changes that were kept.

**SEVEN OF THE 23 FINDINGS ARE ABOUT THE DESIGNER'S OWN MOCKUP CANVAS AND ARE NOT WORK HERE** — D1 (one company telling three stories across three mockup screens), D2 (generate the mockup's dates from the T+3 rule), L2 ("fix the mock so review screenshots stay honest"), U2 (no iOS screens drawn), U3 (no first-run journey drawn), U6 ("mark Turn 1 superseded"), U7 (no design for clashing Moments). **A finding about a reviewer's artefact is feedback to send back**, and three of them were on my own to-do list before I read past the headline.

### C1 — the GMP consent flag gated nothing
- **IT WAS READ IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE: `GmpDialog`'s own mount check.** So consent **DISMISSED A MODAL** rather than **UNLOCKING** anything, and all eight grey-market figure renders were unconditional. Three ways that showed GMP to someone never given the disclaimer, plus a fourth found while reading the dialog:
  - **the dialog was mounted on Home ALONE** while the figures are on FOUR screens — the card premium band, the Trends screen, the detail panel and the detail sparkline;
  - **A DEEP LINK BYPASSED IT PERMANENTLY.** `app.json` sets `scheme: investoyard`, so expo-router's automatic linking makes `investoyard://gmp` mount the Trends screen **without Home ever mounting** — the dialog never runs, and because acceptance was what dismissed it rather than what revealed the numbers, such a reader was never shown it **at all**;
  - **a pre-consent paint**: `useState(false)` plus an async SecureStore read means the first frame painted the premium band on every card with no modal over it. Same class as the white-label legal-operator flash the web fixed with a pre-paint script;
  - **THE ANDROID BACK BUTTON GRANTED CONSENT.** `onRequestClose` was wired to `accept`, so hardware-back stored the flag **and recorded a server-side `gmp_disclaimer`** against a reader who had agreed to nothing.
- **THE GATE IS WHAT MADE THE BACK-BUTTON FIX POSSIBLE, which is the interlock worth remembering**: before it, a non-accepting close would have left the figures on screen anyway, so back-means-accept was the only non-absurd behaviour. The dialog also gained a visible **"Not now"** — a disclaimer whose only visible action is the one that accepts it is a formality.
- **`ok` IS THREE-STATE AND CALLERS WITHHOLD ON `null`.** Revealing a figure and retracting it is worse than one arriving a tick late — the reasoning that makes the web footer wait for the host before naming a legal operator. **A read failure counts as NOT CONSENTED**: the safe answer to "we cannot tell" is to keep the figures behind the disclaimer.
- **A ROOT CONTEXT, matching `AuthProvider`, not a module store** — so every screen under the stack shares one answer and accepting on one reveals the rest with no remount, and the dialog is mounted ONCE where any screen can reach it. **HOME STILL OFFERS IT UNPROMPTED on a first visit**: the gate is ADDITIVE, so nothing the operator approved changed and the common path is identical. It could now be dropped for just-in-time — this project's stated DPDP posture — but that is the operator's call and not a side effect of a bug fix.
- The chip reads **"See Exp. Premium"**, not the report's "See GMP": `LABEL.gmp` is the customer-facing term and the vocabulary rule keeps GMP for the URL, the SEO copy and the disclaimer that has to name the thing.
- **`gmpEnabled` HAS NINE READERS ON WEB AND ZERO IN `apps/mobile`**, while `registerTenant` gives a white-label tenant `gmpEnabled: false` **AND `locked: true`**. Latent only because mobile is single-brand — it fetches `/tenants` for `upiCap` alone, off **`list[0]`**, which is itself fragile: a different ordering would silently adopt another tenant's cap. **Live the day a partner-branded build ships.**
- **The verifier resolves LOCAL COMPONENT INDIRECTION**, because a textual enclosure test called `Row` in `gmp.tsx` unguarded — it is defined above the gate and rendered inside it. Waving that through by hand is how a site hides.

### U4 — claims with no visible methodology
- **HALF THE FINDING DOES NOT APPLY: there is no probability claim anywhere in our code.** The report's "1 in 3" and "80% chance" are mockup copy; a grep for that shape hits only comments. **Mobile's performance screen carries three headline statistics and web's hub carries NONE** — per-IPO rows only.
- ~~**BOTH SURFACES ARE LATENT TODAY**~~ **FALSE, AND CORRECTED THE SAME DAY — BOTH RENDER, AND THE PERFORMANCE CARD IS PUBLISHING "+22.4% AVG LISTING GAIN · 100% LISTED IN PROFIT" OFF A SAMPLE OF ONE.** I wrote that of 1,172 visible listed IPOs zero hold a listing price, so nothing renders and "these claims go live the moment the operator enters a listing price". One was already entered. **TWO MEASUREMENT FAULTS, BOTH OF THEM SHAPES THIS FILE ALREADY DOCUMENTS:**
  - **I FILTERED ON `Ipo.status`, AND THE SURFACES DO NOT READ IT.** Both `enrich()` functions set `status: effectiveStatus(ipo)`, which DERIVES from the dates — so the column said 1,172 listed while the enriched answer is **1,292**, and MVELECTRO, which listed on 6 Aug with a 22.35% gain, sits in the column as **`upcoming`**. This file says outright that `Ipo.status` is effectively dead and derived at read time; I queried it anyway. **Query what the SCREEN reads, which here means applying `effectiveStatus` before counting.**
  - **`extra->>'nseListingPrice' IS NOT NULL` COUNTED 124 AND 123 OF THEM ARE THE EMPTY STRING.** Only MVELECTRO holds a real value (₹520). That is the trailing-`""`-reads-as-PRESENT trap `mergeContentPatch` exists to avoid, met from the SQL side: **a NULL test cannot see an empty string, and a blanked operator field is stored as one.**
- **SO THE REAL STATE IS WORSE THAN LATENT AND THE BASIS LINE IS WORTH MORE THAN I CLAIMED.** `listingInfo()` returns non-null for **exactly 1** record of 1,292, and the stats card therefore reads **avg +22.4% · 100% in profit · 1 listing averaged**. A 100% success rate over one issue is the single strongest case for stating the sample, and it is what the card now says: *"Simple average across the 1 listing we hold a listing price for, 2026 — not every IPO ever issued, and not a forecast. One unusual debut moves it."* **The web accuracy table likewise renders ONE row** (MVELECTRO, GMP +25.6% against a listing +22.4%) with its new methodology footnote visible on the live home page.
  - **The old denominator bug is invisible at n=1** — `listed.length` and `gains.length` are both 1 — which is exactly why it had to be reasoned about rather than observed. It becomes visible the moment a record carries a listing price and no price band.
- **THE DISPLAYED DENOMINATOR WAS NOT THE AVERAGE'S.** `listed` admits a record with a listing price **OR** a gain; `gains` needs a gain. So an issue with a price and no price band counted as a "listing tracked" while contributing nothing to either percentage beside it. And **"listings tracked" reads as a set somebody curated** when it is whatever that API response carried. Now `gains.length` under **"listings averaged"** — the first wording, "listings in this average", measures **118.6px in a 108.7px column at 360px** and wraps (fontkit against the real Inter TTFs; RN cannot be rendered here). This one is 90.7px.
- **THE BASIS LINE TAKES ITS SIZE AND RANGE FROM THE SAMPLE**, not a fixed window: the catalogue reaches back to 2016 and an average over ten years is not the same claim as one over a season. It says it is a simple mean and that one unusual debut moves it. Same house rule as `Total (net offer)` and `QIB (Ex. Anchor)` — a figure states its own basis.
- **"on target" IS A JUDGEMENT RENDERED AS A LABEL and this site publishes no ratings**, so `GmpAccuracy`'s thresholds are **named constants the footnote INTERPOLATES** — the sentence cannot drift from the branches. It also states what the rows are a selection **of**: a sample presented without its selection rule reads as a track record over everything.
- **THREE COPIES OF ONE UNITS BUG, found by grepping for the shape after fixing the first.** `gmpPct ?? gmp` prints **RUPEES PER SHARE with a `%` sign**, because the API derives `gmpPct` as `gmp / upper band` (`ipo.service.ts`) and leaves it undefined on a record with no band:
  - `GmpAccuracy` diffed it against a real listing-gain percentage and handed the result to `verdict()`. A GMP with no band HAS no percentage, so the issue is left out.
  - **`GmpHub` printed it in the pill while the column immediately to its LEFT already prints that same figure as ₹** — one row would have read `₹45` and `+45%`. Its `?? 0` was the second half: an issue with no GMP showed `+0%`, a real measurement of no premium, beside a dash.
  - **`IpoCard`'s v1 chip, the most-rendered of the three.** It falls back to the figure in its OWN unit rather than a bare label, because that chip is the only place a v1 card shows GMP.
  - **All three are LATENT**: all 24 IPOs holding a GMP row have a price band, checked across every status and not just listed.
- **MY OWN FIX TO THE `IpoCard` CHIP HAD A SIGN BUG** and I caught it rereading: `Math.abs` under a prefix that only supplies `+` would have printed a negative GMP as `₹45`. It uses the explicit `+`/`−` the mobile card already uses.
- **KNOWN, NOT FIXED: the listing-gain derivation exists in THREE copies** — mobile's `listingInfo()`, `GmpAccuracy`'s `listedGainPct()` and `PerformanceHub`'s inline pair. They agree today, and that is the shape this codebase has paid for repeatedly.

### U5 — an applicant the server will refuse must not look selectable
- **OUR CONSENT MODEL IS NOT THE REPORT'S.** `Consent` is per USER per TYPE with a `withdrawnAt`, not per family member, so there is no "revoked member" to show. **And the API is already right**: withdrawal is prospective and re-ticking the apply box mints a fresh grant, which is what DPDP wants — nobody can be permanently barred from consenting again — and the box is `useState(false)`, so the re-grant is explicit rather than silent.
- **WHAT IT MAPS TO IS THE PICKER, AND WEB WAS AHEAD OF MOBILE RATHER THAN BEHIND.** Mobile's only filter was `!!p.upiId && relationship !== 'child'`, so it would offer an applicant who had **already applied** for this issue (refused server-side on `panHash`), **two applicants sharing a PAN** (refused per batch), or one with **no PAN or no demat** (never checked).
- **THE SEVERITY COMES FROM THE LOOP.** `onPlace` creates applications ONE AT A TIME, so a batch containing any of the three creates the earlier applications and **then** errors: the investor is shown a failure and has live bids. A chip that can only fail is worse than no chip.
- **THE DEMAT TEST MUST BE DEPOSITORY-AWARE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, and my first version was only half of it.** I read the `demats` list with `clientId` as a fallback — CDSL-safe, and **WRONG FOR NSDL**, where a bid genuinely needs the DP ID: it would have passed the picker and been **refused by the rail builder**, which is strictly worse than being told at the picker. Web's expression was already right, so mobile uses that one rather than a second reading of the rule.
- **`myApps` ARRIVES LATE**, which needed one guard: an applicant selected before it lands can turn out to have applied already and would otherwise render as **selected AND blocked at once**. A missed check costs the OLD behaviour and never a wrong allow — `listApplications` returns `[]` on failure and the server still refuses. **Keyed on `ipoId` rather than the symbol**, and draft/failed/rejected bids do not count, matching the server's own filter: a failed attempt must not lock someone out of retrying.
- **`ApplicationView` NOW DECLARES `investorProfileId`.** `toView` has always sent it and the interface never declared it, so web read it through a local type. **A field the API populates but the type does not declare is the same hazard as one the type declares and nothing populates**: the next surface reaches for `any`.
- **THREE DEAD THINGS FOUND READING THESE RULES:**
  - **`profileReady` in `store.ts`: ZERO callers, and it tested `dpId && clientId`** — the CDSL fault, which would have reported every CDSL applicant as not ready. **No longer hypothetical: there are 4 CDSL profiles now** where this file recorded "all 230 are NSDL". **Dead code carrying a known-bad pattern is the version waiting for a caller.**
  - **`Profile.consent`**: set `true` by both mappers and read only as `&& p.consent`. **A field named `consent` that is always true is worse than no field on a DPDP surface** — it reads as a per-applicant gate and no such thing is tracked.
  - **`PrintFlow`'s OWN `mapApiProfile`**, which the shared one's docblock says does not exist ("a copy in each is how two readings of one API row drift apart"). **THEY HAD ALREADY DRIFTED: the copy omitted `demats`**, precisely the field that docblock singles out. Harmless only because the print flow has no demat picker.
- **REACHABILITY, MEASURED — AND MY FIRST ANSWER WAS WRONG.** I was about to record that neither state can be reached because every application is a partner print "with no investor profile". **2,886 of the 4,214 DO carry one**, across 1,291 profiles, with **2,313 distinct profile/IPO pairs** holding an application — so **already-applied is reachable**. **Same-PAN is NOT**: no user holds two profiles sharing a PAN. Nothing has been through this flow either way — all 4,214 are `applyMethod: 'pdf'`, zero native. **Counting it is what corrected it.**

### A5 / A6 / A8 — and the dark mode one of them exposed
- **MOST OF A5 DOES NOT APPLY, AND MEASURING IS WHAT SHOWED THAT. None of the six colours the report names exists in our code** — they are mockup values, which also settles **A2** (its `#8C93AD` on `#F3F4F8` is their Turn-1 canvas). Measured against OUR 18 designed pairs instead: **the amber warning pair is 4.93:1, BETTER than the 4.88 the report recommends** and clear of the 4.38 it flags; **the active chip is 4.82:1** (theirs was 4.44 because `#7A6CC0` is lighter than our `#7565BD`); and **their "make it `#3C2E7E`" would collapse Pre Apply into Upcoming**, the one thing the status colour code forbids.
- **THE ONE REAL MOBILE MISS WAS `tabBarInactiveTintColor`, AND THE A1 SWEEP HAD CLASSIFIED IT AS AN ICON PROP.** It colours the tab **LABEL** too, so the 4.5:1 text bar applies rather than the 3:1 graphic one — **4.05:1 on the white pill**. And that label is **the smallest text in the app at 10px**, the one place the A3 floor could not be applied, so contrast is all there is to give it. 5.97:1 now.
- **RE-CLASSIFYING THE SURVIVING `muted` USES CORRECTED THIS FILE'S OWN A1 ARITHMETIC** (16 icons + 18 placeholders + 2 disabled does not reach the 40 uses that are there): **17 icons · 18 placeholders · 1 tab tint · 2 disabled-state text · 1 background · 1 icon fallback.** The two text uses are the greyed language rows, which **WCAG 1.4.3 explicitly exempts as an inactive component**. **The 18 placeholders stay**: at `slate` a placeholder would be indistinguishable from an entered value.
- **WEB'S Pre Apply CHIP TOOK `--brand-500`, WHICH WAS ALREADY IN THE SCALE** — `--brand-400` on `--brand-50` is 4.32:1 and fails AA for the 12px/600 `.status` chip; `#5645a6` is 6.72:1. A token promotion, the move `--pos-ink` and `--neg-ink` already made. **`--tone` stays `--brand-400`**, so the rail, dot and swatch are untouched and only the text darkens. **Mobile's chip takes the identical hex** — a semantic colour is one of the few things the two surfaces may not differ on.
- **AND THAT CHANGE WOULD HAVE BROKEN DARK MODE, WHICH A SLOPPY REGEX IN MY OWN CHECK IS WHAT CAUGHT.** It resolved the brand tokens with one global match and took the **LAST** declaration, which is the dark block's, and reported 2.11:1 — a bug in the check that named a real one: **`--brand-500` HAD NO DARK VALUE**, because nothing used it until now, so the LIGHT ink would have landed on a dark ground at **2.28:1** where `--brand-400` (which does have dark values) was 4.57. Both dark blocks now declare it, promoting their own `--brand-700` hex: **8.18:1 consumer, 7.47:1 admin**. **A pair must be resolved WITHIN the theme that declares it, and every theme has to be asked.**
- **MEASURING THAT FOUND TWO PRE-EXISTING DARK FAULTS NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR.** `--brand-400`'s dark value is only **dE 8.0** from Upcoming's dark ink, so **PRE APPLY AND UPCOMING WERE ALREADY INDISTINGUISHABLE IN DARK MODE** — a legend collision, the exact thing the colour code exists to prevent — and in admin dark that ink was **3.71:1**, already failing. Both fixed by the same change; neither introduced by it.
- **A SEPARABILITY QUESTION NEEDS dE, NOT A CONTRAST RATIO** — the ratio is luminance-only and says Live green and Closing red differ by 1.21:1. Every candidate was measured both ways, which is why `--brand-600` was rejected in dark (7.00:1, dE 8.3).
- **`--brand-500`'s ONLY OTHER CONSUMER IS DEAD CSS** — `.sh-track i`'s gradient, referenced nowhere outside `globals.css` — so the dark change reaches `.t-preapply` alone. **Check who else reads a token before giving it a new value in a new theme.**
- **A6 — THE STICKY TAB BAR'S STATUS WAS ABSENT FROM ASSISTIVE TECH ALTOGETHER.** The dot had only a `title`, which many screen readers never announce and no touch device shows — **and it sits inside an element whose `aria-label` OVERRIDES its contents**, so whatever the dot held never reached a reader. Named in the link now, dot hidden. The other three dot sites were already fine (two sit beside their own label, one already carried `aria-hidden`). **Mobile's reservation bar already has a swatch legend** with category names and share counts, so "add a legend to the chart" was already satisfied; its verified tick sits beside a full sentence; its only glyph-labelled control already has an `accessibilityLabel`.
- **A8 — one emoji in a result title, `Approved 🎉`, removed.** The `ok` tone does the celebrating; an emoji renders differently on every OS and a screen reader says "party popper" where the word Approved already says it. **And the sweep for it found 18 decorative `.emoji` illustrations with ZERO `aria-hidden` between them**, so a screen reader announced "open lock", "card index dividers", "magnifying glass tilted left" immediately before the heading that says the same thing in words. **The codebase already did this correctly in ONE place** — `SignupForm`'s tick — so this applies an existing pattern. `GmpDialog`'s `⚠️` is deliberately untouched: operator-approved wording, and a warning glyph on a warning heading carries its own meaning.
- **THE EMOJI SWEEP FIRST RETURNED NOTHING AND THE ALL-CLEAR WAS FALSE: `perl` without `-CSD` reads BYTES**, so `\x{26A0}` can never match a UTF-8 file — and the bytes for the one emoji I already knew about were sitting right there (`342 232 240`). **Never read an empty result from a search you have not confirmed can see the data**; the control run against a known emoji is what made the real sweep trustworthy.

### Deployed, and what the deploy needed
- **`node scripts/api-forward.js --check` IS THE ONE COMMAND THAT SEPARATES A HEALTHY BUILD PATH FROM A PLAUSIBLE-LOOKING ONE**, and it earned it: port 3000 was held by the unrelated app again (302 to `/login`), then free, then ours. **Run it before every web deploy** — `safeGet` swallows a 302 exactly as it swallows a dead API, and that build exits 0 with a perfect-looking log.
- **CLEAR `.next/cache/fetch-cache` AND `out-staging` BY HAND** before the build: the cache was 16 MB against a **356 MB** webpack sibling that must be left alone, and clearing staging yourself removes Next's own clear step from the equation — the `ENOTEMPTY` / `ENOENT` race that has now bitten three times.
- **VERIFIED BY CONTENT, NOT ROUTE COUNT**: live index **10,079,809 bytes, byte-identical to disk**, 2,643 ipo pages, all three `--brand-500` values present in the **large** referenced stylesheet (284 KB — the 12 KB one is the decoy), the old failing pairing absent, `"aria-label":"".concat(c).concat(r&&d?" — "...` in the chunk, and **zero chunks carrying an unhidden `className:"emoji"`**.
- **The first request measured 14.5 s and the next 0.077 s** — the documented cold path, not an outage. Do not read one slow response after a publish as a problem.
- **TOOLING, paid for again**: a `{/* … */}` comment is **JSX syntax and invalid in a JS expression position**, so placing one inside `{title && (` is a parse error; and a patch script's own prose backticks closed its template literal **for the second time in this file's history** — put long replacement blocks in `.txt` files and read them in.

## The tab bar is four, and the 11sp floor came with it (2026-11-04)

- **Home · Applied · Insights · Account**, the handoff's own four and its order. **Its README asserts "this matches `app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, which was cut from 5 tabs to 4" — a claim about OUR file that was not true**, and its route map is what said where the fifth goes: *"Family members | `app/(tabs)/profiles.tsx` (move under Account)"*.
- **PROFILES MOVED OUT OF THE GROUP RATHER THAN TAKING `href: null`.** That API is real and was verified in `node_modules` rather than recalled — **`TabsClient.js:91` turns `href === null` into `hidden: true`** — but **A HIDDEN TAB IS STILL A TAB**: the bar stays on screen with no active item and there is no back button, so the reader lands somewhere they can only leave by picking another tab. As a stack screen it gets the native back header every other secondary screen here has. **Account ALREADY pushed `/profiles`**, so the route in existed and the handoff's "move under Account" was half done; a path-based push resolves to the new file unchanged.
- **THE MOVE NEEDED NO IMPORT EDITS, which is worth knowing before anyone fears it**: `app/(tabs)/profiles.tsx` and `app/profiles/index.tsx` are both two levels below `app/`, so every `../../` still lands where it did.
- **TWO THINGS TRAVELLED WITH IT.** Its `paddingBottom: 110 /* clear the floating tab bar */` is 110px of dead space under a screen that no longer has one. And it printed `profiles.title` as its own `h1`, which the native header now carries — **NO OTHER STACK SCREEN HERE CARRIES ITS OWN HEADING** (checked: consents, notifications, calendar, glossary are all zero), so keeping it would have printed the same words twice.
- **THE RENAME TO "Applied" IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT COSMETIC** — it is what makes the A3 11sp floor reachable, and this file recorded that floor as blocked on this very decision. Measured with fontkit against the real Inter TTFs, because React Native cannot be rendered on this box: at **5 tabs** a tab is 66.4px at 360 and *Applications* is **66.6px — it clips by 0.2px**; at **4 tabs** a tab is 83.0px and the widest label, *Account*, is **44.7px, 38.3px spare**, still fitting at a **1.3× font scale** (58.1px), which the column does not grow for. *Applications* was 66.6px; **"Applied" is 40.9px.**
  - **The figure this supersedes was right for its own assumption and is now wrong**: the A3 note said 4 tabs would give "83px with 16px to spare", which is true if the label stays *Applications*. With the rename it is 38.3px.
- **Applied sits SECOND, ahead of Insights** — the handoff's order and the better one: an investor opens this app to check a bid far more often than to browse.
- Swept for anything else pointing at the old route: **`/profiles` is pushed from Account alone**, `/profiles/new` from apply, print and the list (all stack routes, untouched), the `lib/api.ts` hits are API paths rather than app routes, and no server-side notification deep-links it. **`UsersIcon` / `UsersIconFill` were left imported and unused by the cut** — `tsc` does not flag that without `noUnusedLocals`, which this package does not set, so it has to be grepped for.
- **THE PRE-PUSH HOOK BLOCKED THE MONOREPO PUSH AND WAS RIGHT TO.** GitHub was in a **"Minor Service Outage"** and the mobile remote had rejected with `Internal Server Error`, so the mirror held an unpushed commit — exactly the out-of-step state the hook exists to catch. **A retry pushed the mirror, and a later retry pushed the monorepo; nothing was wrong with the commit.** Read a `remote: Internal Server Error` as GitHub rather than as the change, check `githubstatus.com/api/v2/status.json`, and stop retrying rather than hammering it.

## The web revert, and the scope that caused it (2026-11-04)

- **THE TASK WAS THE MOBILE DESIGN HANDOFF AND I CHANGED 16 WEB FILES.** The QC report's findings are about mobile screens; I fixed them on mobile, then went looking for the **same shapes** on web and fixed those too — the units bug, the decorative emoji, the sticky-tab status, the dead readiness code. Every one is a real defect. **None of them was asked for**, and the operator's question was the right one: *"I have ask for only mobile design then what u change in web."* **17 files are back at `3b021e5` byte-identical.**
- **THE LESSON IS NOT "THE FIXES WERE WRONG", IT IS THAT A FOUND DEFECT IS NOT A MANDATE.** Grepping for the shape after fixing one instance is a rule this file states and it is a good one — but it finds work on surfaces the request never named, and the right move there is to REPORT it, not to ship it. A deploy nobody asked for is the part that makes it expensive: it reached the live site before anyone had agreed to it.
- **TWO CHANGES WERE KEPT, AND THE TEST FOR KEEPING THEM WAS WHETHER THE MOBILE WORK FORCED THEM:**
  - **`globals.css`'s `.t-preapply --tone-ink: var(--brand-500)`** plus its two dark values. Mobile's chip now uses **`#5645A6`**, and this file's own rule is that **SEMANTIC COLOURS MAY NOT DIFFER BETWEEN THE TWO SURFACES** — so reverting would put web back to 4.32:1, *failing AA*, **and** make the two disagree. Undoing it means also reverting mobile's chip, or accepting the divergence.
  - **`ApplicationView.investorProfileId`** in shared-types, which mobile's apply picker reads. Additive; web is unaffected either way.
- **WHAT CAME BACK, STATED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE DISCOVERED** — because a revert that hides its own cost is worse than no revert:
  - **`gmpPct ?? gmp` on three web surfaces**, printing RUPEES PER SHARE with a `%` sign. **LATENT**: all 24 records holding a GMP row also hold a price band, so it cannot fire today. `GmpHub` is the one to watch — the column to its LEFT prints that same figure as ₹, so one row would read `₹45` and `+45%`.
  - **18 decorative `.emoji` with no `aria-hidden`**, so a screen reader again announces "open lock" and "card index dividers" before the heading that says it in words.
  - **`profileReady`** with its `dpId && clientId` CDSL fault — 0 callers, and **there are 4 CDSL profiles now** where this file once recorded "all 230 are NSDL".
  - **`Profile.consent`**, always `true` and read only as `&& p.consent`, and **`PrintFlow`'s duplicate `mapApiProfile`** which had already drifted (it omits `demats`).
  - **THE ONE VISIBLE LOSS: `GmpAccuracy`'s methodology footnote.** That section renders ONE row on the live home page, so the footnote was being read — which is also the thing that corrected the latency claim above. Everything else here is invisible or latent.
- **THE REVERT WAS VERIFIED AGAINST THE BASE COMMIT'S BLOBS, not by eye.** A revert that leaves one hunk behind is the worst outcome: the file then reads as original and behaves as neither. `git diff --name-only <base> --` must come back empty for every file meant to go back.
- **AND THE VERIFIER REPORTED THE REVERT HAD FAILED WHEN IT HAD NOT — THREE TIMES OVER, IN THREE DIFFERENT WAYS.** All three are the same root error, which is worth more than the revert itself:
  - **IT READ `git show HEAD:…` WHILE THE REVERT WAS STAGED AND UNCOMMITTED**, so it was reading the PRE-revert content and three checks failed. **`HEAD` is not the working tree, and during a staged change it is the thing you are moving away from.**
  - **A FOURTH CHECK PASSED FALSELY, because its pattern matched the COMMENT I had written ABOUT the bug rather than the bug.** `/gmpPct \?\? ipo\.gmp/` hits the explanatory comment as readily as the code. **Exclude comments when asking whether a fix landed** — the same fault as the `take: 50000` grep matching its own docblock and the `.sh-track` grep matching this file.
  - **THE BYTE COMPARISON COMPARED UTF-16 STRING LENGTH AGAINST A BYTE COUNT** and reported the live page as differing from disk. This page is full of `₹`, `—` and `·`, so the two can never agree. **Compare `Buffer`s.**
  - **THE TELL EACH TIME WAS A RESULT THAT CONTRADICTED A MEASUREMENT ALREADY TRUSTED** — the file list said 17 of 17 reverted while three content checks said otherwise. **When two checks disagree, suspect the newer one.**

## The second mobile parity pass — subscription (2026-11-04)

**THE OPERATOR REPORTED THAT MOBILE'S SUBSCRIPTION SCREENS DID NOT MATCH WEB AND NAMED THREE PANELS. The audit's first useful answer was that NO DATA WAS MISSING ANYWHERE** — both surfaces already import `reservation`, `lotLadder` and `subscriptionTable` from shared-types, so every figure was identical and every gap was a field that never reached the screen. **Say that first in any parity audit: a missing number and an unrendered number need different fixes.**

- **Reservation was already matched** and needed nothing — same shared resolver, same stacked bar, same legend. Worth recording because it was one of the three named, and because the "Anchor portion / QIB (Ex. Anchor)" sub-rows that look like a gap are in **`IpoForm.tsx`, the ADMIN form**, not the public page. I nearly reported them as missing.

### What was actually missing, and the fix
- **THE AS-OF STAMP WAS A RAW ISO DATE.** `subscriptionAsOf.slice(0, 10)` printed *"as of 2026-11-04"* where web prints *"Updated 04-Nov-2026 3:45 PM"*. **Two faults in one expression**: the wording differed, and vocabulary is one of the few things the two surfaces may NOT differ on; and the value was unformatted. **It is the same field web was burned by** when `asOf` arrived formatted at one call site and raw at another — the lesson recorded then was that **FORMATTING BELONGS WHERE THE VALUE IS RENDERED**, which the shared `fmtDateTime` now does.
- **`Reserved` ON THE LOT PANEL — AND SCOPED TO SME, WHICH CORRECTS MY OWN AUDIT.** I told the operator *"web's lot ladder has a Reserved column mobile lacks"* with no qualifier. **Web's MAINBOARD ladder is `Category | Lot & Share | Amount` and has no such column**, and on mainboard mobile already shows MORE than web (min and max broken out). **Adding it everywhere would have been a difference FROM web dressed up as parity.** It takes the ENTRY POINT cell, which on a `single` SME card restated the MIN amount from the cell beside it.
- **THE DETAIL PAGE HAD NO HEADLINE AT ALL.** Web opens with a big times figure, a meter ticked `0×/1×/2×/3×+`, the tone word and three tiles — Book · Subscribed · **Applications**. Mobile went straight to the table, so **the one number a reader opens the page for was only reachable by reading a column.** `SubscriptionHero` is mounted on the DETAIL page only, which is where web mounts it: the same panel renders inside the home list card, where a 100px headline would push the table off the first screen.
  - **THREE SIGNAL COLOURS WHERE WEB HAS FOUR, deliberately.** Web ramps `pos → gold-600 → warn → neg`; mobile's palette carries green, amber and red, and a fourth for this one figure would be a fifth signal colour on a surface whose palette is already the status legend's. **`warm` and `cool` share amber and THE WORD carries the distinction** — which is the A6 rule anyway.
- **THE HUB CARD WAS A BAR LIST** (category · bar · `N×`) where web renders four tables. It now renders **`SubscriptionPanel`, which already IS web's Share-wise, Application-wise and ₹-crore tables behind one toggle**, so the hub gained Book Size, the share counts, Req 1×, the application counts and the anchor note. **NO NEW COMPONENTS AND NO NEW ARITHMETIC** — every part already existed for the detail page; the fix was to mount what was there.
  - **The lot ladder and day-wise go behind `ExpandTile`.** A phone list of open issues has to stay scannable, and **web affords four unfolded panels per card by being WIDE**, which a phone is not. The bar list survives as the fallback for the 11 records with no reservation table, where the panel renders empty.
- **QIB'S INSTITUTIONAL SPLIT APPEARED NOWHERE IN `apps/mobile`.** The poller writes `extra.qibSplit` and web breaks QIB into FIIs / DIIs / Mutual funds / Others. It is a **PARTITION** of the QIB row — the exchange sends the sub-rows with no parent and their sum equals the stored figure to the share — so they are **indented UNDER QIB and never counted beside it**, the same reason the two HNI bands sit under NII. **₹-crore view only, as web**: in Share-wise a sub-row has no reserved quota to be a multiple against, and in Application-wise QIB is not a row at all.

### Three things moved into shared-types rather than copied
- **`subTone.ts` — the tone ladder and the two money formatters, and IT HAD ALREADY DRIFTED INSIDE WEB.** At JPL's 3.00× the hub said *Oversubscribed* in green while the detail page said *Heavily subscribed*, and **between 2× and 3× the two disagreed about the COLOUR as well** — hot green on one page, warm amber on the other, for one issue. 11 tests. Mobile had no tone word at all, so there was nothing to drift until the hero added one — **which is exactly when a second copy starts.**
- **`QibSplit` · `QIB_SUB_ROWS` · `qibSubRows()` in `shareWise.ts`.** Five exchange codes print as four lines because `FI` and `IC` are both DIIs; **a second copy of that list is how a category goes missing**, which this project has paid for four times.
- **`subDisclaimer.ts` — the operator-locked subscription disclaimer.** See the next section.

### What this pass taught
- **`export { x as y } from '…'` RE-EXPORTS WITHOUT CREATING A LOCAL BINDING.** It broke `SubscriptionHub` in four places that use `tone` itself, and again for the `QibSplit` type. **Aliasing through a local import is the form that gives both** — `import { subTone } …; export const tone = subTone;`.
- **A GREP FOR A FIX'S OWN PATTERN MATCHES THE COMMENT EXPLAINING IT**, so every check in this pass runs on comment-stripped source. That had already produced one false pass earlier the same day.
- **A verifier should assert the REFERENCE too, not only the thing being changed.** The point-1 check asserts that web's own filter is still open-only, so if the reference moves it says so rather than silently measuring mobile against a target that changed.

## The subscription disclaimer is one string now (2026-11-04)

- **MOBILE'S `/subscription` CARRIED NO DISCLAIMER AT ALL**, and listed CLOSED issues below the open ones under a *"Closed — final subscription"* heading. Web lists `status === 'open'` with subscription data and nothing else.
- **THE CLOSED SECTION IS GONE, and the reason is not symmetry for its own sake: A HUB OF LIVE FIGURES THAT ALSO SHOWS SETTLED ONES INVITES A READER TO ACT ON A NUMBER THAT CAN NO LONGER MOVE.** A closed issue keeps its own detail page, which is where its final subscription belongs. **`sevenDaysAgoIso` went with it** — it was correct (both ends UTC midnight of a plain date string, so the subtraction could not drift across a timezone boundary) and **dead code is dead however careful it is.**
- **THE TEXT MOVED TO `packages/shared-types/src/subDisclaimer.ts`.** The web component's own docblock already gave the reason — *"same text on both pages so we render it from one place; a copy edit lands on both surfaces at once"* — and **that argument reaches across SURFACES exactly as it reached across routes.** A legal notice is the last thing that should exist in two copies: **one edited and one not is worse than one merely out of date, because nothing on either screen says which is which.**
- **PROVED BYTE-IDENTICAL BEFORE THE MOVE, which is the check that mattered.** The wording is operator-locked (2026-09-18), so relocating it must not change one character. **Comparing by eye would not do**: the JSX spells the curly quotes as HTML entities and wraps at arbitrary columns, so the two differ in SOURCE while being identical in OUTPUT. Normalising the entities and the JSX whitespace collapse, **both come to 1,259 characters and compare equal.** `scratchpad`-style one-off, but the technique is the point.
- **THE SPEC ASSERTS THE EXACT LENGTH, NOT A FLOOR.** *Locked* should mean any edit fails a test and forces the re-sign-off the docblock asks for; **a `>` threshold waves through a rewrite of the same size**, which is the edit most likely to be made by accident. **My first guard was `> 1400` — a guessed number against a 1,259-character text**, so it failed on the real thing, which is how the length came to be measured at all. **A guessed threshold that happens to pass is the worse outcome.**
- The disclaimer renders **UNDER THE LIST and never on the empty state**: it exists to sit beneath live figures, and under *"No subscription data yet"* it would be a page of legal text about nothing. 11px and `slate`, not `muted` — **a disclaimer is the last thing to set in an ink that fails the contrast bar.**

## Phase 2 of the handoff, without the hero (2026-11-05)

- ~~**THE OPERATOR SAID "START PHASE 2 BUT KEEP HOME CARDS-FIRST FOR NOW"**~~ **THEY ASKED FOR THE HERO LATER THE SAME DAY AND IT IS BUILT** — see *The Moments hero, built*. The reasoning below is why the engine shipped two commits before anything rendered it, which is also why the component that finally drew it is small: **Home still keeps its cards, the hero sits ABOVE them.** The original note read: the handoff’s primary design is a **Moments home**, mounting it reverses a decision recorded here, **so the hero, U7’s clash card and U9’s freshness line were NOT built** — all three hang off a hero nothing was mounting, and a component with no caller is the dead code this repo has deleted three times (`STATUS_TONE`, `profileReady`, `assets/investoyard-logo.svg`).
- **WHAT IS LEFT OF PHASE 2 IS THE ENGINE AND THE WATCHLIST, AND THE WATCHLIST IS THE ENGINE'S FIRST REAL CONSUMER** rather than a stub for one. That ordering matters: a pure module with tests and no caller is only marginally better than the dead code above, and the watchlist genuinely needs the engine's answer to group its rows.

### `packages/shared-types/src/moment.ts` — the engine, 33 tests
