- **IN SHARED-TYPES AND NOT `apps/mobile/lib/moment.ts`, WHERE THE HANDOFF PUTS IT.** Mobile has no test runner, so a derivation living there cannot be exercised at all — the same reason `offeredFromIpo`, `parseAnchorRows`, `offerTotals` and the rich-text sanitiser were moved out of the files that hid them. It is also a derivation web could want.
- **IT EXPECTS THE ENRICHED RECORD, AND THE DOCBLOCK SAYS SO.** `stageOf` reads `ipo.status`, and that column is dead — every row says `upcoming` until `enrich()` overwrites it with `effectiveStatus(ipo)`. **Handing the engine a row straight out of SQL reports the entire catalogue as quiet**, which is the measurement mistake already made once in this file, against the U4 claim, and wrong there by 120 records.
- **ONLY ONE PAIR OF THE PRIORITY ORDER COMES FROM THE DESIGN.** It states exactly one clash — *"listing morning and closing day … the higher priority (listing) takes the hero"* — so results lead actions; the rest is extrapolated from that same principle and the whole order is **one named constant**, `MOMENT_PRIORITY`. **It is not an obvious decision and the source says so**: a closing deadline, once missed, cannot be recovered, where a result can be read at any time. What makes putting results first safe is `also`.
- **`also` IS NOT `all[1]`, AND THAT IS WHAT U7 IS ACTUALLY FOR.** Taking the second-highest by rank would, on a day holding a listing, an allotment and a closing, put the **allotment** in the clash card and leave the only deadline off the screen — two results stacked while the thing with an hour attached went missing. It prefers an **action** (`ACTION_MOMENTS` = closing · open) and falls back to any differing kind.
- **A RESULT MOMENT NEEDS THE FAMILY IN IT.** *"The hero becomes the family's results"* cannot be said to someone who never applied, and saying it anyway is the same class of fault as the demo store that minted three fake applicants into every browser: a screen asserting a stake the reader does not have. **An APPLICATION is enough, not an allotment** — a family that applied and missed is the one most curious on listing morning, and gating on an allotment would hide the Moment from exactly them.
- **A RESULT MOMENT IS TODAY ONLY.** It is a *Moment*: allotment evening is the evening the registrar publishes, and the results stay readable on the Applied tab for as long as anyone wants them. Without that bound every issue the family ever applied to queues up behind today's.
- **AN ALLOTMENT GETS NO CLOCK.** Registrars publish when they publish, there is no convention and no stored hour, so a countdown there is invented precision on the Moment people refresh hardest. `at: null` renders as a state, not a stopped timer — the rule `CountdownDial` already follows. Closing and open take `closeAt`, which honours the operator's own hour; listing counts from the 10:00 market open.
- **SYMBOLS ARE FOLDED TO UPPER CASE HERE, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE ALLOTMENT SEARCH'S RULE, and the difference is in the data.** A symbol is canonically upper case; **47,92,920 stored application numbers contain a lower-case letter by design.** Folding is safe for one and destroys the other.
- **RUN OVER THE REAL CATALOGUE, AND 2026-11-05 WAS BY CHANCE THE EXACT THREE-WAY CASE `ACTION_MOMENTS` WAS WRITTEN FOR**: hero **listing BCSSL**, `all[1]` **allotment VPFL**, and `also` correctly promoted to **closing ANTALA** — the only issue on the whole platform with a deadline that day. Without that rule it falls off the screen. Two result Moments were withheld from a non-applicant and the one action shown to everyone, which is the gating rule holding on live data rather than on a fixture. **Run the engine against `/api/ipos` through the forwarder before trusting a change to it**; the fixtures prove the rules and only this proves it does something sensible.

### `apps/mobile/app/watchlist.tsx` — the screen the bell never had
- **THE BELL HAS EXISTED FOR MONTHS WITH NOWHERE TO SEE WHAT IT COLLECTED.** `RemindBell` toggles a server row that drives the hourly alert sweep, and the only way to learn whether an issue was on the list was to navigate to it and look at the bell.
- **WHAT A BELL DOES IS SHOWN, NOT OFFERED AS A CHOICE.** The design wants a reminder chip per event and **`WatchlistItem` is `(userId, ipoId)` with no preference column**, so a chip row would be four controls that change nothing. The sweep fires all four (`ipo_open` · `ipo_closing` · `ipo_allotment` · `ipo_listing`) to every watcher, so the row states those four with their dates and dims the ones past. **Per-event opt-out needs a schema column, which is a handshake.**
- **NOTHING IS REMOVED BEHIND THE INVESTOR'S BACK.** The design says an issue *"moves to your Home Moment and leaves the watchlist"* when it opens; dropping the server row would **cancel the allotment and listing alerts, which are the ones people set the bell for**. A live issue is grouped under a heading saying where to find it and keeps its reminders.
- **AN UNREACHABLE API SHOWS OFFLINE, NEVER AN EMPTY WATCHLIST.** It read `getIpos()` and inferred this from *"a non-empty watchlist against an empty catalogue"* until 2026-11-05; it reads **`getIposResult()`** and **`listApplicationsResult()`** now, so the screen is told which happened instead of guessing. An empty state over a list we simply could not fetch is the fault this app removed its mock catalogue to avoid.
- **A WATCHED ID THE CATALOGUE NO LONGER CARRIES IS SHOWN, NOT DROPPED** — an invisible row is one nobody can get rid of.
- **NO SWIPE-TO-REMOVE, and the reason is not only that `react-native-gesture-handler` is absent.** A swipe has no screen-reader equivalent, which is A6's own complaint about meaning carried by one modality. An explicit Remove button is also the 44dp target A4 asks for.
- **EN-LITERAL, matching `subscription.tsx`, `gmp.tsx` and `performance.tsx`, which carry ZERO `t()` calls between them.** A new screen in English literals matches the surrounding practice; U8's Hindi pass covers all four together and needs a translator.

### Two existing rules given one home, because the screen forced it
- **`apps/mobile/lib/watchlist.ts` — ONE ANSWER TO "IS THIS IPO WATCHED".** The cache was module state inside `RemindBell`, which was fine while the bell was the only thing that could change it (`toggle` mutates the cached Set on its way through). **It stops being fine the moment a second surface can remove a row**: the screen would drop it server-side and every bell already mounted would go on showing a lit reminder, because nothing had told them. It lives in `lib` rather than beside the bell because a SCREEN importing state out of a component is backwards and the next consumer would copy it.
- **`isLiveApplication` / `DEAD_APPLICATION_STATUSES`, LIFTED VERBATIM out of the apply screen's inline `DEAD` array.** It decides whether an applicant has already applied for an issue, which is exactly what the engine needs before showing a result — and a second reading of it is how two screens come to disagree about whether a family is in an issue. **`cancelled` stays absent because that is what the apply screen has always done**: this moved the rule, it did not rewrite it. Worth settling separately — a cancelled application arguably should not block re-applying, but adding it changes who the apply picker lets through.

### What this leaves
- **THE CATALOGUE HOLDS ZERO ISSUES WITH A FUTURE OPEN DATE** (measured 2026-11-05 against 1,319 rows; ANTALA was the only one open and it closed that day). **So a quiet-day hero would have nothing to look ahead to** — `nextOpen` is null, correctly. That is operator data, not code, and it is the same family as the W-SUB and readiness-card gaps.
- ~~**STILL NOT BUILT, and all three wait on the hero being wanted**~~ **ALL THREE BUILT 2026-11-05** — the Moment hero, U7’s clash card and U9’s freshness line and skeleton. **U9 was as cheap as predicted**: pull-to-refresh already existed on every screen and `Skeleton.tsx` was already there, so only the "Updated N min ago" line and the hero skeleton were new.
- **THREE OF THE FIVE HERO STATES STILL LACK THE DATA THE DESIGN DRAWS, and the hero now DEGRADES rather than waits for it**: **5b** "one button applies for every eligible member" still has no caller for `createBulkApplication`, so the CTA sends the family to `/apply` · **4c** listing morning wants a live opening price and **1 record of 1,148** holds one, so that state shows the issue price and the premium and **invents nothing** · **4b** allotment evening wants a shareable image and mobile’s only `Share.share` is TEXT. Only **4a** closing day was ready, because `appliedIds` / `panClash` / `blockedWhy` were built for U5.
- **A SERVER-SIDE A8 INSTANCE WAS FOUND AND LEFT ALONE**: `alerts.service.ts` puts 🔔 ⏳ 📋 🎉 in its notification titles, and 🎉 is the exact emoji A8 names. Reported rather than fixed — it is server code, push does not deliver, and the client half of A8 is done.
- Verified: 33 engine tests, 679 in the package, 7/7 turbo tasks, three clean typechecks, and **18 static properties of the screen measured from source and the theme** — the 11sp floor, no fixed `height` around text, no `hitSlop` inside the pressable Card (the documented tap-stealing hazard), and the three new colour pairs at 9.50 / 5.17 / 5.50:1. **None of it was seen; React Native cannot be rendered on this box.**

## Phase 3, U1 — a failed read no longer renders as "you have none" (2026-11-05)

- **THE WORST CASE WAS LIVE AND IT WAS THE APPLIED TAB.** `listApplications` returned `[]` on a dead network, a 500 **and an expired session** alike, and that screen renders `[]` as *"Your first application will show up here — pick an open IPO and go for it."* So an investor with money blocked, on a day the API was down or their session had quietly lapsed, was told they had **never applied for anything and invited to apply again**. The same shape told the apply screen *"no applicant — add one"* on an issue closing that afternoon, which is U1's own *"these failures hit on the busiest days"*.
- **THE COMMENT THOSE FUNCTIONS CARRIED WAS NOT WRONG, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED.** *"Empty, not a stale local store — an investor must never be shown an application list that is missing bids they really placed"* is true, and it is still there. It simply never addressed the other half: **`[]` IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM "NONE".** A true comment that answers a different question is the quietest way for a fault to keep its cover.
- **AUDITED BEFORE DESIGNING, AND THE AUDIT IS MOST OF THE VALUE: 26 of the 41 exported API readers swallow a failure, and MOST OF THEM ARE CORRECT.** The auth pass already gave `verifyOtp` / `passwordLogin` / `requestOtpResult` a discriminated result carrying `reason: 'unreachable'`; `checkSession` is deliberately three-state; a failed **write** returning `false` is a different question entirely. **The dangerous shape is much narrower — a LIST whose `[]` is RENDERED as a statement about the investor** — and there are four: applications, profiles, consents, notifications. **Counting the whole set and then classifying it is what kept this from becoming a 26-function rewrite.**
- **`Fetched<T>` IS `IposResult` GENERALISED, NOT A NEW IDEA.** That type was built on 2026-10-26 after two test devices showed an empty home screen and the only way to tell was the IIS access log, and its docblock already states the rule: *a 502 is not an empty catalogue*. **ONE `authedGet`**, because four copies of a try/catch/status check is how two of them end up disagreeing about what a 503 means.
- **401 IS ITS OWN REASON, AND THE RECOVERY IS WHAT SEPARATES THEM FROM A DEAD NETWORK.** Mobile checks the session **ONCE**, during hydration in `components/auth.tsx`, so a session that expires while the app is open turns every authed read into `[]` with nothing signing the user out until the next launch. A dead network wants a Retry; **an expired session fails identically however many times it is tried, so it is offered a sign-in and NO RETRY** — a button that can only fail is worse than no button, the same rule that keeps the UPI-change action off a confirmed bid. **That rule lives in ONE component** (`components/ui/LoadFailure.tsx`) rather than in each of the four screens.
- **TWO SHAPES, BECAUSE A FAILURE MEANS DIFFERENT THINGS DEPENDING ON WHETHER ANYTHING IS ALREADY ON SCREEN** — and they are two of the four states U1 asks for. A first load that failed hands over to **`LoadFailure`**; a **REFRESH** that failed **KEEPS THE ROWS** and adds **`LoadFailureBar`**, because an investor pulling to refresh must not watch their bids disappear. The bar names what is wrong and what to do, never only that something went wrong.
- **THE BRANCH ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING AND IS ASSERTED BY A CHECK**: the failure test must come BEFORE the `=== null` skeleton, or a first load that failed sits under skeletons for ever with no way out. Verified by index comparison on all three screens rather than by reading them.
- **THE PROVIDER WAS THE ONE WITH SIX CONSUMERS, AND I FIRST WROTE FOUR, FROM THE ONES I HAD LOOKED AT.** `ProfilesProvider` set `[]` on a failed fetch, so apply, print, the profiles screen, the profile FORM, Account's count and `DematPanel` all read *"this investor has no family"*. **A verifier that COUNTED `useProfiles()` call sites rather than trusting my list is what caught it**, and the two it added were not filler. **`print/[symbol].tsx` carried the IDENTICAL "no applicant - add one" branch to apply**, on the path the operator's brokers actually use. **`profiles/new.tsx` was worse and quieter**: `editing = profiles.find(p => p.id === id)` resolves to undefined over an empty list, so **EDITING AN EXISTING PROFILE SILENTLY BECOMES ADDING A NEW ONE**, while `taken` goes empty and the form offers a relationship already in use. Both produce a DUPLICATE PROFILE, which is the damage PAN-key churn already caused once, so the form now refuses to render at all in that state. It carries a `failure` reason now; **the context memo depends on it, or no consumer would ever see it change**; **signed out is the HONEST EMPTY rather than a failure**; and a failed refresh keeps the rows so a family cannot vanish mid-apply. **Count the call sites - a list of the consumers you examined is not a count of the consumers.**
- **PROVED AGAINST REAL ENDPOINTS, AND NOT AGAINST A REPLICA — this is the reusable technique.** `authedGet` depends on nothing but `fetch`, `API_BASE` and `__DEV__`, so **its own source text is lifted out of `api.ts` and evaluated** with those three injected. **A replica would only prove I can write the same thing twice.** Six cases: 200 → ok · 401 with no token and with a junk token → signedout · a real 404 → unreachable · a dead port → unreachable · a bad host → unreachable. **The harness first confirms `/applications` really answers 401**, so the signedout cases cannot pass on a different status — the same reason the emoji sweep needed a control run.
- **DELIBERATELY NOT CONVERTED, AND THE VERIFIER ASSERTS EACH ONE** so the next reader does not take it for an oversight: **`createBulkApplication`** is a MUTATION (and uncalled) and **`getPosts` / `getBanners`** are news. **THE TWO FORM PICKERS I NAMED HERE TURNED OUT TO HAVE DIFFERENT ANSWERS** — `getRelationships` was already correct and `getUpiHandles` was a DEAD END; see the next subsection.
- **A GUARD TOO BROAD AND THEN A WINDOW TOO WIDE, in one patch script, and both refusals were useful.** A check for the swallow PATTERN (`if (res.ok) return res.json();`) refused and **was right to**: the other instance is `createBulkApplication`, a mutation, which is a different case — so the guard taught me something rather than merely blocking me. Narrowing it to *"these four must no longer fetch for themselves"* then used a **400-character window** from each function's start, which ran past the short new bodies **into the next function**, which does fetch, and reported a false positive. Slicing to the closing brace at column 0 fixed it. **A guard that fires is cheap; the two shapes to avoid are a window measured in characters and a pattern that matches a legitimate sibling.**
- **A SCRIPT THAT THREW PART-WAY HAD ALREADY WRITTEN TWO OF ITS THREE STEPS**, so the third had to be re-run on its own rather than by re-running the script, whose earlier anchors were by then gone. **Checked with `git status` rather than reasoning from the script** — this file's standing rule, paid for again.
- Verified: **6 live cases + 33 static properties**, three clean typechecks, 7/7 turbo tasks. The static set covers the branch order, the provider's four behaviours, no Retry on an expired session, the 11sp floor, `minHeight` rather than `height`, no `hitSlop` reaching into the bar's own row, and both bar palettes at **15.68 / 14.60 / 4.93 / 9.50:1**. **No screen was seen; React Native cannot be rendered on this box.**

### U1's named states, finished — and two of my own claims corrected (2026-11-05)
- **THE UPI HANDLE PICKER WAS A DEAD END, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT BLOCKED A FLOW.** The handle can only be chosen from chips, so a failed `getUpiHandles` left *"Handle list unavailable — try again in a moment."* and **NO WAY TO ENTER A UPI ID AT ALL**, on the one screen an applicant cannot avoid. **It degrades to free text now, which is the rule the bank, branch, city and state pickers ON THAT SAME SCREEN already follow** — one picker out of five not following the screen's own rule.
  - **FREE TEXT IS SAFE HERE AND THE CHECK IS NOT BEING DROPPED.** `upiHandleOk` **already** skips when the list is empty (`!upiHandles.length ||`), and **`ProfilesService.setUpi` is the authority** — it refuses a handle that is not on the master whatever the client sent. So this hands the decision to the server instead of to a dead end.
  - **LOADING AND FAILED ARE TOLD APART**, or free text would flash for the half-second before the list lands — the `loaded`-flag distinction `/subscription/v2` already draws between *"still fetching"* and *"not in the catalogue"*. A retry IS offered, because unlike an expired session this is a case where one can work.
- ~~**THE REGISTRAR-DOWN CASE IS NOT COVERED**~~ **PARTLY COVERED ALREADY, AND THE FINDING OVERSTATES IT.** The registrar-**NOT-YET-PUBLISHED** case was correct all along: `!found` with `!allotmentOut` renders *"yet — registrar data arrives on allotment day"*, which is the honest answer and the common one. **WHAT WAS NOT COVERED IS THE NETWORK**: `checkAllotment` threw and the screen rendered `String(e.message)`, so a dead connection showed the investor **React Native's own "Network request failed"**, in red, with no way forward, on the night they refresh hardest.
  - **A REFUSAL KEEPS THE SERVER'S OWN MESSAGE, and that was proved rather than assumed** — a malformed PAN comes back ***"Enter a valid 10-character PAN."***, which is the useful one. Only the no-status case gets our wording, and **only that case gets a Retry, because only that case can succeed on one.**
  - **NO THROWING VERSION IS KEPT.** The `getIpos` / `getIposResult` precedent exists to spare callers that never asked for a reason; this function has exactly **ONE** caller, so a second contract would be dead code.
- ~~**THE WATCHLIST SCREEN STILL DISTINGUISHES OFFLINE BY HEURISTIC**~~ **FIXED, and it is the inconsistency this file flagged the same morning.** **THE TWO READS FAIL DIFFERENTLY, WHICH IS WHY THEY ARE NOT TREATED ALIKE**: without the CATALOGUE there is nothing to render, so that takes over the screen; without the APPLICATIONS the rows still render and only the grouping loses precision (`appliedIpoIds` decides whether a result Moment is this family's and whether *"You applied"* shows), so that gets the bar — the same split the other four screens draw.
- **`getRelationships` NEEDED NOTHING, AND I HAD LISTED IT AS OPEN.** It already carries `DEFAULT_OPTIONS` and replaces them only `if (r?.length)`, which is exactly the degradation rule. **The verifier asserts that**, so nobody "fixes" a thing that was right. One of the two form pickers I flagged was fine and the other was a dead end — **a pair named in one breath is not a pair with one answer.**
- ~~**A FILE-WIDE GUARD REFUSED THIS PATCH AND WAS RIGHT TO — the other four `throw new Error(msg)` are MUTATIONS, STILL OPEN**~~ **CLOSED 2026-11-05, later the same day** — `changeApplicationUpi` · `requestUnblock` · `createProfile` · `updateProfile` share one `sendJson` helper now; see *A segment label that cut off its own last letter*. **The refusal was still the useful kind**: it is what established that the other instance was a mutation and a different question.
- **A SOFT-PASS CHECK IS WORSE THAN NO CHECK, AND I WROTE ONE.** The first version of that record tested for `} catch` **anywhere** in the body and reported *"re-check it deliberately"* on all four — but **all four carry `try { await res.json() } catch {}` INSIDE the error branch**, which guards the JSON parse and nothing else. The question is whether the **FETCH** is wrapped, so it slices before `await fetch(` and tests that; the four now fail loudly instead of passing vaguely. **A check with an escape hatch will take it.**
- Verified: **5 live cases** through the function's own source text (a real lookup, a malformed PAN, a dead socket, plus the screen's retry gating) and **29 static properties** — the dead end gone, `loadHandles` called on mount and on retry, the typed handle normalised, the watchlist's fatal/non-fatal split, and both new actions at `minHeight` with no `hitSlop` reaching into neighbouring content. Three typechecks clean, 7/7 turbo tasks. **Nothing was seen.**

### What U1 still owes
- ~~**U1 IS NOW DOWN TO THE FOUR LEAKING MUTATIONS**~~ **U1 IS CLOSED, 2026-11-05.** All four mutations share `sendJson`, and the 401 through them now carries the shared expired-session sentence and a typed reason instead of the server’s *"Missing token"* — see *The 401, and one reason declared once*. **The one thing deliberately NOT done is clearing the token on a 401**, which is a change to how the whole app behaves rather than to a message, and is the operator’s call.
- **U3** (first run) is partly blocked: the member's own consent request arrives by push or WhatsApp and **push does not deliver**. **U2** needs a Mac, **U8** a translator, **L1** a device, and **C1's** server half needs the FCM credentials.

## A segment label that cut off its own last letter (2026-11-05)

- **THE OPERATOR REPORTED THE BOARD SEGMENT READING "Al / Mainboar / SM" — the last letter gone from all three.** It is worth following the diagnosis rather than the fix, because every instinct about it was wrong.
- **IT IS NOT A WIDTH PROBLEM, AND MEASURING IS WHAT PROVES IT.** At 375 each button is **112.3px** and the widest label, *Mainboard*, needs **65.7px** in Inter SemiBold 12.5 — **46.7px of spare**, and it fits at every phone size from **320** up (28.3px spare at the narrowest). **A container with that much room cannot clip a glyph.**
- **NOR IS IT THE FONT SCALE.** At a 2× scale *All* is 32.2px of 112, so it still fits — and **ALL THREE BEING CUT is the constraint that kills every width story**, because a width fault takes *Mainboard* alone. **When a symptom hits every item in a set equally, stop looking for a per-item cause.**
- **WHAT CAN DO IT IS THE TEXT SIZING ITSELF.** `segBtn` is a **COLUMN** container — no `flexDirection`, so the default — with `alignItems: 'center'`, which **shrink-wraps the child to its own intrinsic measurement**. If RN measures the label a glyph short of what it then draws, **the Text's own box is short and the tail is cut while the parent's 46px goes unused.**
- **AND THE APP COULD REACH THAT STATE BY DESIGN.** `app/_layout.tsx` rendered after a **3-SECOND font timeout** with every `fontFamily: 'Inter_600SemiBold'` still applied, and on Android a family name the platform cannot resolve is exactly where measurement and drawing come apart. **The old comment called that fallback "acceptable"; "Al / Mainboar / SM" is what acceptable turned out to mean.** Five font files over a LAN dev server on a cold Metro cache can take longer than 3s, which is why **a dev-server restart is a plausible trigger** — and the operator had just restarted. **NOT REPRODUCED HERE**, because React Native cannot be rendered on this box; it is the best-supported explanation, not a watched event.
- **THE LAYOUT FIX IS THE ONE THAT HOLDS WHATEVER THE FONT DOES**: `alignSelf: 'stretch'` plus `textAlign: 'center'` on the label, so it takes the button's full width and any measurement error lands in the slack instead of in a glyph. **This is web's `width: 0; min-width: 100%` idiom, which this file already records three times for grid tracks sized to max-content: DO NOT LET AN INTRINSIC MEASUREMENT DECIDE THE BOX.**
- **APPLIED TO ALL SIX SEGMENTED CONTROLS, not only the one reported** — home (`index.tsx`), all-IPOs, login's OTP/password toggle, day-wise, the demat picker and the subscription view. Same control, same shape, same latent bug. **Icon-only buttons with a fixed width are deliberately untouched: there is no text to clip.**
- **THE FONT HATCH OPENS AT 12s RATHER THAN 3, AND IS KEPT, because its instinct was right** — a splash screen that never leaves because one asset 404'd is worse than a wrong typeface. In a production build these are local assets that resolve in milliseconds, so a generous hatch costs real users nothing and only stops a dev session rendering in the wrong face. **AND IT NOW WARNS IN DEV ON BOTH FALLBACK PATHS**: the old one was silent, which is why *"why is the text cut off?"* was not answerable from the device.
- **NOT FIXED, AND THE REASON IS MECHANICAL**: dropping the Inter names when they did not load would be the complete answer, and it **cannot be done by mutating `fonts`** — `StyleSheet.create` is an identity function in modern RN, so every screen captures `fonts.semibold` when its module evaluates, which under expo-router's eager route requires happens **before** `_layout` decides. That needs a different mechanism.
- Verified: **30 static properties**, including the two measurements above re-derived from the font's own `hmtx` table rather than quoted. **Nothing was seen.**

### The four leaking mutations, closed
- **ALL FOUR POSTED WITH THEIR `fetch` UNWRAPPED**, so a dead connection threw React Native's own ***"Network request failed"*** straight past them and their callers showed it to the investor — **while saving a profile, changing a UPI ID, or asking a sponsor bank to release blocked funds.** The `} catch {}` each one carried guards `res.json()` and nothing else, **which is what made a grep for "has a catch" report all four as fine** — see the check note below.
- **ONE HELPER, `sendJson`, AND FOUR THIN CALLERS — AND THEY HAD ALREADY DRIFTED**: three said *"Could not save"*, one *"Could not send"*. The `verb` threads through both sentences, so a caller reads *"Could not save"* and *"…and save again"* rather than a generic apology. Same argument as `authedGet`: four copies of a status-check-and-throw is how two of them come to disagree about what a 503 means.
- **THE THROWING CONTRACT IS KEPT.** These are mutations whose callers already catch and surface `e.message`; turning them into Results would change every call site for no gain. **What changes is that the message is OURS when there was no response and the SERVER'S when there was.**
- **PROVED ON THE HELPER'S OWN SOURCE TEXT against real endpoints** — a 401 keeps the server's words (*"Missing token"*), a dead socket and an unresolvable host both get ours with the right verb, a 404 stays usable, and **no case leaks a transport string**. **Nothing was written**: every case is refused before it reaches a handler.
- ~~**KNOWN AND NOT FIXED: a 401 through these four shows the server’s terse *"Missing token"***~~ **FIXED the same day** — `sendJson` maps 401/403 to `signedout` and throws an `ApiError` carrying that reason, so these now agree with `authedGet` about what a dead session is.

### The 401, and one reason declared once — U1 closed (2026-11-05)
- **A 401 THROUGH THE FOUR MUTATIONS SHOWED THE INVESTOR THE SERVER'S OWN *"Missing token"*** — true, and useless to someone mid-form saving a profile or asking a sponsor bank to release blocked funds. `authedGet` had mapped that to `signedout` on the READ side since the day before; **this is the write side of the same state.**
- **THE REASON IS TYPED, NOT MATCHED ON WORDING, AND THE PRECEDENT IS ALREADY IN THIS REPO.** Web's client carries `status` and `code` on its `ApiError` for exactly this — an INTERFACE plus a cast there, where mobile uses a real CLASS so `instanceof` works and `isSignedOut` needs no cast; **the PRINCIPLE is the precedent, not the mechanism** — with the reasoning written down: **matching on the message breaks the moment the message is edited or translated — and these ARE translated.** So `sendJson` throws an `ApiError` carrying the same `'unreachable' | 'signedout'` vocabulary `authedGet` already returns, plus `'refused'` and the status, and **`isSignedOut()` lets a caller recognise it without reading text.**
- **ONE DECLARATION OF THAT UNION, AND IT WAS A DRIFT OF MINE FROM TWO COMMITS EARLIER.** It had been written twice — inline in `Fetched<T>` and again as `FailureReason` in `components/ui/LoadFailure.tsx`. **`lib/api.ts` owns it now, because that is where failures are produced**, and the component **RE-EXPORTS** it so the screens' imports did not all have to move. A type declared twice is a type that drifts from the thing it describes.
- **ONE SENTENCE FOR AN EXPIRED SESSION.** `SESSION_EXPIRED` is shared by the 401 throw and by the signed-out gate `LoadFailure` renders, so **a failed SAVE and a failed READ cannot tell the investor two different stories about the same thing.** The stale-data BAR keeps its own shorter line on purpose: it sits over real content, where the full sentence would be a paragraph.
- **NOT DONE, AND IT IS THE OPERATOR'S CALL RATHER THAN SCOPE TAKEN QUIETLY: clearing the token on a 401** so every screen's existing `if (!token) return <LoginGate />` fires. It is arguably right — **web does exactly that**, and a 401 from our own API with a Bearer token means the token is genuinely dead, since `JwtAuthGuard` verifies the signature and only then resolves the subject — but it is a behavioural change to the **whole app** rather than to a message, and it would make `LoadFailure`'s `signedout` branch nearly unreachable.
- Proved on the helper's own source text against the live API: the 401 says exactly the shared sentence, carries `reason: 'signedout'` and the status, is an `ApiError`, and **no longer leaks "Missing token"**; a real status stays `refused` with the server's words; a dead socket is `unreachable` with the right verb on both wordings. **Nothing was written** — every case is refused before a handler.

### A FIFTH species: a check that accepts anything that is not the bad thing
- **THE SOFT PASS IS THE MOST DANGEROUS OF THE FAMILY, because it reports OK.** `prove-mutations.js` asserted only that the thrown message was **not** a transport string. When `ApiError` fell out of the evaluated scope, the throw became `"ApiError is not defined"` — and the check **PASSED on a ReferenceError**, twice, because a ReferenceError is not a transport string.
- **THE RULE IS THE ONE THE RICH-TEXT SANITISER ALREADY FOLLOWS: ALLOW-LIST, NEVER A DENY-LIST.** `cleanRich` tests link protocols against what is permitted rather than against a list of what is not, because `javascript:` has more spellings than anyone enumerates. A check is the same shape: **name the message you expect, never the one you fear.** Every case in the replacement asserts an exact string.
- **AND HAND-STRIPPING TYPESCRIPT WAS THE WRONG TOOL.** The source-text technique needs the types removed, and a handful of regexes did it until `: number,` matched inside `?: number,`, left `status?,` and threw a SyntaxError. **`ts.transpileModule` is already a dependency** and handles the parameter-property shorthand in `ApiError`'s constructor, which no regex of mine was going to. **Reach for the compiler, not a regex, when the input is a language.**
- **THE THIRD FAILURE OF THE ROUND WAS AN ASSERTION GOING STALE BY DESIGN**, which is what an assertion is for: *"exactly one `throw new Error(msg)` left"* is now **zero**, because the helper throws `ApiError` on all three paths. Updated with the reason recorded beside it rather than loosened.

### A fourth species of badly-shaped check: one that sees its OWN output
- **FOUR GUARDS IN ONE SESSION REFUSED A CORRECT PATCH BY MATCHING THE TEXT THEY HAD JUST WRITTEN**, which is the inverse of the family this file already tracks at four places above: those could not see the data, these see too much.
  - A check for the swallow pattern `if (res.ok) return res.json();` matched **`createBulkApplication`**, a legitimate sibling — that one was *useful*, because it taught me the other instance was a mutation.
  - A narrowed version then used a **400-character window** from each function's start, which ran past the short new bodies **into the next function**, which does fetch.
  - A CLAUDE.md guard for *"does the stale claim survive"* matched **the new section's own `~~…~~` citation** of the heading it was retiring.
  - A style guard re-tested its own pattern for *"the old style survives"* — and the NEW style is still `  segTxt: { … },`, so it matched.
- **THE RULE: A GUARD MUST ASSERT THE DIFFERENCE, NOT THE SHAPE.** Test for the thing that is now TRUE (`alignSelf` present, `sendJson` called, `~~` beside the citation), never for the absence of a pattern the replacement also matches. **And slice to a real boundary** — a closing brace at column 0 — rather than to a character count.

## The Moments hero, built (2026-11-05)

- **THE HANDOFF'S CENTREPIECE IS ON SCREEN** — five states (quiet day · open day · closing day · allotment evening · listing morning), decided by `momentPlan` in shared-types, which is pure and already carried 33 tests. **The component only DRAWS the answer**, which is why it is small: the thinking was done when the engine landed, two commits before anything rendered.
- **HOME KEEPS ITS CARDS.** The hero is the first child of the existing ScrollView, so the cards-first list is untouched below it — **this ADDS a hero, it does not replace the layout**, which is the shape the operator asked for when they said yes.
- **U7 AND U9 CAME WITH IT**, because both hang off a hero and had been waiting for one: a clashing day's second Moment renders as a compact action card **directly under** the hero, and the hero carries *"Updated N min ago"* plus a skeleton.
- **FOUR RULES FROM THIS WEEK ARE LOAD-BEARING IN IT, and the verifier asserts each one**, because breaking any reintroduces a fault already paid for: **the premium tile sits inside `GmpReveal`** (the home Moments are the exact surface C1 said GMP leaked from) · **no success colour appears anywhere in the hero and the premium keeps an explicit sign** (C2) · **the CTA falls back to "View details" when `startBid` is off**, since `/apply` would say *"Bidding hasn't started yet"* and a button that can only fail is worse than none · **the CTA label fills its button and centres**, which is the clipped-segment lesson from the same morning.
- **NO INVENTED LISTING PRICE.** The design's listing morning shows a live opening price against the issue price; **we hold one on ONE record of 1,148 and there is no live feed**, so that state shows the issue price, the premium and the family's applications and leaves the rest to the performance screen.

### Rendering a React Native component on a box that cannot run React Native
- **THE PREVIEW IS GENERATED FROM THE COMPONENT'S OWN STYLESHEET, NOT HAND-DRAWN** — every colour, size, radius, weight and gap **parsed out of `MomentHero.tsx` and `theme.ts`**, Inter loaded from the TTFs the app bundles so the metrics are the device's, and the figures pulled from the live catalogue. This exists because the hazard of a hand-copied harness is already recorded here: it *"verifies the CSS while exercising none of the data path"*. **It still exercises no data path — it proves appearance and nothing about behaviour** — but the styles cannot drift from the component.
- **AND IT FOUND A REAL BUG, WHICH IS THE ARGUMENT FOR DOING IT.** The first render drew **`+₹0`** for the premium, and the cause was in the COMPONENT: it read **`extra.gmp`** when **GMP IS A TOP-LEVEL FIELD** (`ipo.gmp`, which `enrich()` maps). `extra` carries `gmpLog` and `gmpSourceId`, which is what misled me. **That tile could never have rendered**, and no typecheck would have said so — the read was through `as any`.
- **TWO FAULTS IN THE PREVIEW ITSELF, and both are the hazard of the technique rather than of the app**:
  - **IT NEVER EMITTED `display:flex`, so the tile ROW stacked.** **React Native is flex-by-default AND column-by-default; CSS is neither.** Any RN-to-CSS translation must add `display:flex` to every container that sets a flex property, and `flex-direction: column` when the component states none. The component was never wrong; the translation was.
  - **EVERY STATE SHOWED THE SAME COMPANY**, because the filters for listed, allotment and upcoming matched nothing on the day and all fell through to one fallback. **A PREVIEW THAT REPEATS ONE ROW TEACHES NOTHING ABOUT HOW THE STATES DIFFER.** Each now takes a distinct record and the caption says whether that state is **live today** or **illustrated**.
- **28 checks**: the five states, U7, U9, the gate, the sign, the `startBid` fallback, the 11sp floor, 44dp by the button's own box, `minHeight` on everything but two 7px dots, mounted exactly once above the cards with the filters and segment still present, and four contrast pairs on the gradient — **10.53 · 14.42 · 9.61 · 6.34:1**.
- **ONE CHECK OF MINE REPORTED A PASS AS A FAILURE**: it counted fixed heights with a NEWLINE-ANCHORED `\n\s*height:`, and the two dots are written INLINE (`dot: { width: 7, height: 7, … }`), so it counted zero. `(?<!min)height:` is the test that means something. **Anchor on the token, not on the formatting.**

### The row under it: breadth, not a second copy of the hero
- **THE OPERATOR SAID THE TODAY LINE "NOT LOOKS GOOD", AND THE ANSWER WAS NOT TO RESTYLE IT.** `TODAY  1 closing` sat 8px under a hero whose eyebrow already read *"CLOSING TODAY · 3h 12m left"* above the issue's own name — **the same fact, twice, with the quieter copy second.** This file has booked that shape before on the featured panel (*"an Opens in 4 days chip beside an Opens tile and a Closes tile"*). **Restyling it would have made the repetition prettier.**
- **THREE MORE FAULTS AS AN ELEMENT, worth naming because none is about colour**: it **was not a thing** — bare grey text on the canvas where everything else on the page is a card or a chip; it **was dead** — not tappable, sitting directly above filter chips that are; and it was the **fourth row of chrome** before the first card.
- **ITS ONE REAL JOB IS BREADTH: the hero is ONE issue, this counts EVERY event in the catalogue today.** That is worth having on a morning with three listings and two allotments and worth nothing on a morning when the hero already IS the only event. **So it shows only what the hero and the clash card are not already showing, and renders NOTHING when that leaves nothing** — which is the common case, and is what removes the repetition rather than dressing it.
- **IT MOVED INTO `MomentHero`, BECAUSE THAT IS WHERE THE PLAN IS.** Home cannot subtract what the hero covers without re-deriving the plan, **and two derivations of "what is today" is how they come to disagree.** One component now answers that question in three descending registers — the hero, the clash card, and this.
- **IT IS ACTIONABLE, WHICH IT NEVER WAS.** Each pill goes where that event is DEALT WITH — allotments to the checker, listings to performance — **a destination the filter chips do not offer**, checked rather than assumed: they are All / Open / Upcoming / Closed.
- **IT BORROWS THE STATUS LEGEND RATHER THAN INVENTING COLOUR.** The pills render `Chip` with the `closing` / `gold` / `listed` tones those statuses carry everywhere else, so **a colour cannot drift from the legend here** — and the verifier asserts no literal hex appears in the new styles.
- **MEASURED, BECAUSE A LABEL CLIPPED THE SAME MORNING**: the label plus TWO pills is **273px of 343**, which is the common case once the hero has taken one of the three kinds; **three pills reach 353 and wrap**, which `flexWrap` handles and is only reachable when the hero is on `open` or `quiet` (neither covers any of the three counts).
- **A CHECK OF MINE WAS BROKEN BY THE REMOVAL ITSELF, which is a shape worth recognising.** The hero verifier placed the hero by comparing `indexOf('<MomentHero ')` against `indexOf('{todayTotal > 0 ? (')` — and `todayTotal` had just been deleted, so the second returned **−1** and the comparison **could never be true again**. It reads as a regression and is a dead check. **WHEN YOU DELETE A LANDMARK, GREP FOR CHECKS THAT NAVIGATE BY IT**; `indexOf` returning −1 fails silently in exactly the direction that looks like a fault.

## The broker website — per-tenant site templates (2026-11-06)

**WHAT IT IS.** A white-label partner picks a LAYOUT in admin and their public site renders in it; with nothing picked they get ours. Four layouts — `default` (ours) · `broker` (Sir's Broker Website v2) · `dashboard` (2a) · `detail` (2b) · `landing` (2c) — plus a typeface, three optional blocks and the hero copy. Built from Sir's `Investoyard Broker Templates.dc.html` and `Broker Website v2.dc.html`.

**THE COST-DECIDING FINDING, before any of the detail: the designs RE-SKIN OUR OWN INFORMATION ARCHITECTURE.** `HomeView`'s section headings are byte-identical to the canvas — *"Did you get the shares?"*, *"Apply in four steps"*, *"Built for Indian investors"* — and the designs' `#ipos` tri-view block is `IpoExplorer2` plus the existing hubs. So there is **no new data, no new API and no new page**; only the utility bar, the RM card and the app band are genuinely new. **Check whether a design is a new product or a new arrangement before costing it.**

### Where the template id lives, and why that decided the price
- **IN A `site` ROW OF `TenantContent`, NEVER A COLUMN ON `Tenant`.** `prisma migrate diff` reports an **empty migration**, so there is no `db push`, no `prisma generate` and no operator action for the schema — and **the web build already fetches `content` on the `/tenants` call it makes to resolve the brand**, so it gets the template free with no new endpoint. This repo has already paid for adding a column that had a home (the `sponsorBank` one, started and reverted).
- **`site` JOINS `TENANT_CONTENT_KEYS` AND NOT `TENANT_PAGE_KEYS`**, which is the judgement that decides whether the feature costs anything at runtime: it is a template id, a font id, three booleans and a dozen short strings — the same class of payload as `contact`, on a response every page load already makes. A reader of the home page downloading a partner's hero headline is free; downloading their Terms would not be. The public fold is generic over that list, so it carried the new key with no change.
- **The route is `PATCH /admin/tenants/:slug/content/site`**, matching identity and contact, which means `updateTenantContent` handles it with **no service change** — it already validates the key against `TENANT_CONTENT_KEYS`. Behind `tenant.site.manage`, so a partner self-serves, as contact and the legal pages already do. `template` and `font` are validated `@IsIn` against the **shared registries** rather than a list retyped in the DTO: a third copy would fail quietly, as a template the API accepts and the build cannot render.

### THE BOOLEAN FAULT THESE TOGGLES EXPOSED
- **`mergeContentPatch` AND `compact` WERE STRING-ONLY, SO A POSTED `true` DELETED ITS OWN KEY.** Both ran the value through `trim()`, which returns undefined for a boolean, and the writer reads undefined as *cleared*. **A toggle that could never be switched on, with no error anywhere.** Both now go through one `keep()` helper.
- **`false` IS STORED, NOT DROPPED.** A flag's default is not necessarily off, so dropping it would make *"the operator turned this off"* and *"the operator has not decided"* one stored state — the trailing-`""` ambiguity that module exists to avoid, wearing a boolean's clothes.
- **Proved against the REAL Json column inside a transaction that rolled back**, because a unit test proves the function and only that proves the column: `true` comes back a boolean and not `"true"`, `false` is stored, a blanked string is absent rather than `""`, and a second patch touching only a string keeps both booleans. 0 rows survived.

### The identity rule, which is easiest to break here
- **A HERO NEEDS A HEADLINE AND THE OBVIOUS FALLBACK IS OURS.** `resolveSite` never does that. An unsupplied headline DERIVES FROM THE PARTNER'S OWN TRADE NAME — the shape Sir's own 2a sample uses — and **our tagline is deliberately not the fallback**, because on a broker's hero that is us marketing through their domain.
- **THE NAME GOES THROUGH `shortName()`, AND THAT CAME OUT OF THE LIVE DATA.** Measured against the eight real tenants: **five store an ALL-CAPS `Tenant.name`**, so the first derived headline read *"Every IPO, applied from your ACML CAPITAL MARKETS LIMITED account"*. `shortName` is the helper this repo already uses for that on dense surfaces, so this is one more reader rather than a third name formatter. **Imperfect on 1 of the 8** — `N.V.INVESTMENTS` comes out `N.v.investments`, because `titleCase` keeps a known acronym and cannot know about a dotted one — and that is **pinned as KNOWN by a test** rather than left to surprise someone. It is survivable only because `siteGaps` chases the partner for their own headline.
- **A BLOCK IS GATED ON ITS OWN DATA, NOT ON ITS FLAG.** A view handed `showRm: true` and no name renders an empty card; `resolveSite` returns `rm: undefined` instead, so the page renders nothing — and `siteGaps` is then the ONLY thing that will ever mention the half-finished block. **The page must not advertise its own gap.**
- **EVERY SITE GAP IS ADVISORY**, a deliberate limit: `contentGaps` reserves `blocking` for the statements whose absence is survivable but whose being WRONG is not — the legal name and the grievance officer. A missing headline is a gap in the shop window. **Promoting shop-window gaps to blocking is how a blocking list stops being read.**
- **Sir's "GMP stays off" needed NO template rule** — `registerTenant` already sets `gmpEnabled: false` and **LOCKS** it for a white-label, and eight surfaces read that flag. It is keyed on the TENANT, not the layout, which is right: **a template must not override an operator's decision.**
  - **…AND THE NEW SURFACE PROMPTLY LEAKED ONE, which is what "an existing mechanism covers it" does not guarantee.** `SplitExplorer`'s detail pane rendered an `Exp. Premium` row unconditionally — and 2b's whole audience IS the gmp-disabled tenants, so it would have shown a grey-market figure on exactly the sites the flag was switched off for. **A ninth reader of a flag is a ninth place to forget it**; the fix was found by grepping the new components for it rather than by anything failing.

### MUTATING REACT-RENDERED HEAD TAGS BEFORE PAINT GIVES YOU TWO CANONICALS
- **THE CANONICAL FIX WAS WRITTEN IN THE PRE-PAINT SCRIPT FIRST AND IT DOES NOT WORK THERE.** Next renders that metadata as part of the React tree, so mutating it during parse makes React **re-insert its own server-rendered copy at hydration**: measured on a foreign host, **canonical=2, og:url=2, hreflang=6** against 1/1/3 on ours. A second canonical leaves a crawler choosing between two answers and the wrong one is still on the page — **exactly the failure the rewrite exists to avoid.**
- **THE SPLIT THAT WORKS, and it is the rule to carry forward: `<html>` ATTRIBUTES ARE SAFE PRE-PAINT, HEAD TAGS ARE NOT.** React reconciles only what it rendered, and it never rendered `data-foreign-host` / `data-template` / `data-font` — which is why the first has been safe there for weeks. The head rewrite moved to `TenantCanonical`, an effect, in the same place and by the same mechanism as `TenantSchema`, whose docblock has asked for this pass since it was written. **Nothing is lost by the later timing: these tags are never painted, and a crawler that runs JS reads the settled DOM.**
- **IT IS KEYED ON THE PATHNAME**, because Next rewrites metadata on client navigation — keyed on nothing it would have fixed only the first page of each session.
- **WHAT IT WAS COSTING, on a real domain today:** `newipo.finwave.co` is FINWAVE's white-label and the only `customDomain` in the database, so every page of their site carried `canonical → investoyard.com`, which asks Google **not to index their site at all**. The hreflang pair said their Hindi page lives on our domain too. Only tags naming OUR host are rewritten, so a partner's own alternate is never touched, and the alternates keep their own PATH — rewriting them all to the current URL would declare every language the same page.
- **KNOWN RESIDUE, unfixable from here:** a scraper that does NOT run JavaScript — most social link-preview bots — still reads the baked `og:url`, so a shared partner page previews with a link to us. **The complete answer is a per-host build or a server render.**

### `og:url` WAS THE HOME PAGE ON ALL 40 INHERITING ROUTES — and og:title still is
- **THE SAME FAULT THE CANONICAL NOTE DESCRIBES, LEFT IN THE BLOCK TWO LINES BELOW IT.** `alternates.canonical` was corrected from `'/'` to `'./'` on 2026-10-15; **`openGraph.url` sat directly underneath and kept the `'/'`.** Metadata inherits and only `/partner-api` overrides openGraph, so measured on the export every other route emitted `og:url = https://investoyard.com/`. **Share an IPO page on WhatsApp and the preview linked to the home page.** Now `'./'`, resolved per route exactly as the canonical is.
- **`og:title` AND `og:description` ARE STILL WRONG AND MUST NOT BE "FIXED" BY DELETING THEM.** They inherit the same way — the Jio page reads *"Investoyard — India's IPO companion"* while its own `<title>` is correct. The tempting one-liner is to omit them and let Next derive them from the page's `title`, **AND NEXT DOES NOT DO THAT**: `generate/opengraph.js` emits `openGraph.title?.absolute` with **no fallback** to `metadata.title`, so omitting them leaves 40 routes with **no og:title at all**, which is worse than a generic one. (twitter DOES fall back — `postProcessMetadata` fills it from openGraph then from `metadata.title` — which is why only the og side is stuck.) **Read Next's resolver rather than rebuilding to find out; it cost minutes instead of a build.** The real fix is openGraph per route: ~14 files or one shared helper, and its own change.

### How one DOM serves four layouts
- **EVERY SECTION IS IN THE PAGE ONCE, WEARING A `data-sec` NAME, AND CSS GIVES IT AN `order`.** A static export answers every host with one set of HTML, so the layout cannot be branched at build time; rendering the chosen one in the browser costs what the export buys. The arrangement is then **readable in one block per template** instead of spread through conditionals.
- **`display: contents` IS WHAT KEEPS OUR OWN LAYOUT BYTE-IDENTICAL.** `HomeView` now wraps each section in a div that was not there before; both the flow and the wrappers generate **no box at all** by default, so children lay out as direct children of `main.container` with the same margin collapsing. Verified by reading the COMPUTED value, not the rule. A template only becomes a flex column when `data-template` says so, and that attribute is never set on our hosts.
- **`.tpl-flow > .tpl-sec:empty { display: none }` CLOSES THE HOLES.** `GmpAccuracy`, `HomeNews`, `WhatsAppCta`, `RmCard` and `AppBand` all legitimately render nothing, and **a wrapper around nothing is still a flex item and still takes a gap**.
- **THE SELECTOR LIST IS SPELT OUT RATHER THAN `:not([data-template="default"])`**, because the attribute is also ABSENT on our own host and an attribute selector cannot match an absence.
- **`broker`, `dashboard` and `detail` share one order; what differs is the HERO'S SIZE** (a full 392px hero vs a 256px slim one — "IPO cards first" is the table clearing the fold, not a different order). **`landing` is the only genuinely different reading order**: steps and features ABOVE the catalogue, because it "explains as it goes".
- **2b IS THE ONE WITH ITS OWN SURFACE, not its own arrangement.** A master/detail needs SELECTION, which CSS cannot hold. `SplitExplorer` and the card grid are BOTH in the DOM and CSS shows one — so the right surface is laid out in the FIRST paint rather than reflowing after hydration. **The selection defaults to index 0 on both sides**: the server has no session, and `useState(0)` makes the client's first render byte-identical. Any other default is a hydration mismatch, and React's answer to one is to discard the server tree. Every figure comes off the enriched list item the explorer already has — **no second fetch per click.**
- **`id="ipos"` WAS A DUPLICATE AND THE FIRST ONE WINS.** `IpoExplorer2` already renders `<section id="ipos">`; putting the same id on the wrapper made two, and on our own layout the first is the wrapper, which computes `display: contents` and **generates no box to scroll to**. Removed.
- **ABSENCE OF `data-template` MEANS OURS, so `applyPresentation` REMOVES the attribute for the default rather than setting it to `'default'`.** Two reasons and the second is the one that matters: the CSS selector lists name the four non-default templates, so a present-but-default attribute is dead weight whose presence stops meaning *"a partner layout"* — and **a partner who switches BACK to our layout needs the baked attribute taken off**, which only a remove does.
- **HIDING THE CARD GRID ON 2b ALSO HID "Browse all IPOs", because that link lives INSIDE it.** So the list-and-detail layout offered sixteen issues out of 1,300 and no way to reach the rest. `SplitExplorer` carries its own archive link now. **When a template hides a container, enumerate what was inside it** — the affordances in there do not announce their own absence.

### The fonts
- **`html[data-font=…]` IS (0,1,1) AND BEATS `next/font`'s GENERATED CLASS (0,1,0).** That is the same specificity arithmetic that had this site shipping two webfonts and painting neither for months — a `:root` redeclaration ties and loses on source order.
- **EACH PAIR IS DRAWN FROM THE ALTERNATES THEMSELVES, never from Inter.** Inter's own variable **IS** `--font-sans`, so there is no stable handle to point a redefined `--font-sans` back at: `var(--font-inter)` does not exist, and giving Inter a second name means either a duplicate `@font-face` set or demoting the variable the whole site reads. Fraunces is a display serif and borrows Manrope for body.
- **A CURATED LIST, NOT A FREE-TEXT FAMILY**: `next/font` resolves and self-hosts at BUILD time, so a family named at runtime would mean a Google CDN request on every partner page's critical path, a layout shift the baked fonts do not have, and a typo that silently falls back to the system stack.

### Checks that lied, eight of them in one day
This file already tracks the family; these are new instances and the first two are a matched pair pointing opposite ways.
- **A lowercase grep for a camelCase attribute: Next emits `hrefLang`.** `grep 'rel="alternate" hreflang'` reported **zero** tags on pages that have three. The CSS selector matches either way — HTML attribute names are case-insensitive — **a grep is not.**
- **…and the grep that "corrected" it matched MY OWN SCRIPT.** `grep -rl hreflang` hit **6,697 of 6,700 files**, every one the pre-paint script's own source text. **Strip `<script>` blocks before searching built HTML for a selector you also wrote.**
- **`npx tsc … | head -8 && echo "typechecks"` IS A FALSE ALL-CLEAR**: a pipeline's exit code is the LAST command's, so `head` succeeding runs the `echo` over a failing tsc. Redirect to a file and read `$?`.
- **A font assertion against the plain family name** fails on a working font: `next/font` computes `__Figtree_a1e55b`.
- **An assertion that went stale from the fix it was validating** — it required the raw `ACML CAPITAL MARKETS LIMITED` in the headline, which is exactly what `shortName` had just stopped happening.
- **`npm run build … ; grep "✓ …" || tail` REPORTED EXIT 0 ON A FAILED BUILD**, because the trailing `tail` succeeded. The background-task notification then said *"completed (exit code 0)"*. **A compound command's exit code is the last clause's — put the build alone in the command and echo `$?` immediately after it.**
- **A HARNESS PASSED THREE CHECKS AGAINST A MISSING BUILD.** With no `out-staging`, *"no data-template is set"* is true of a 404 page, *"no wrapper generates a box"* is trivially true when there are NO wrappers, and a blank page has no overflow. **A check that reports ok on nothing is the worst of this family, because the signal looks like success** — the harness now asserts the page loaded before measuring anything.
- **A substring class match counted 48 rows where there were 16**: `class="tpl-split-row` also matches `tpl-split-row-t` and `-b`. **Anchor a class grep on a boundary** (`[ "]`).

### `as const satisfies` NARROWS AWAY AN OPTIONAL FIELD, and it bit twice
`SITE_TEMPLATES` was `as const satisfies readonly SiteTemplateMeta[]`, which narrows each entry to its **own literal type** — and a literal type does not carry an optional field that entry omits. So `t.design` was a compile error on the one entry without a design, **for every consumer that looped the list**: it broke the spec, then the admin panel, the same way. Annotated `readonly SiteTemplateMeta[]` instead. The narrow `id` literals bought nothing, because `SiteTemplate` is declared on its own rather than derived from the list.

### Still open
- **`og:title` / `og:description` per route** — see above; the one remaining half of the share-preview bug, and the trap is the deleting "fix".
- **The utility bar appears after hydration**, so a partner host takes one small layout shift at the top of the page. The pre-paint script knows the template but not whether the bar is on; baking a third field into the host map would fix it. Deliberate, not overlooked.
- **A partner's hero headline is not in the server HTML**, because the server cannot know whose domain it is on. Google and Bing render JS; the indexable substance (the catalogue) IS server-rendered. The complete answer is the same per-host build or SSR the og residue needs.
- **`TenantContent` IS EMPTY — 0 rows across all 8 tenants** (measured 2026-11-06), so no partner has entered identity, contact or any legal page. **Every white-label would render "not yet published" legal pages today**, and the blocking gaps are open for all six partners.

### The template reaches the whole site, and the preview Sir had already drawn (2026-11-06)

**THE OPERATOR ASKED WHY ONLY THE HOME PAGE CHANGED AND WHY THERE WAS NO VIEW OPTION. Both were right, and they were different failures.** Measured before answering: **every `data-template` rule targeted a class in `HomeView` / `BrokerHero` / `SplitExplorer` / `IpoExplorer2`, so of 43 consumer pages the template changed ONE.**
- **THE SCOPE WAS A NARROWING OF THE BRIEF TO THE ARTWORK.** The brief said *"site load as per selected"*; the three files in hand are home-page artwork, so a home-page system got built and was described as done. **When a brief is wider than the artwork, say so — do not deliver the artwork's scope under the brief's name.**
- **THE PREVIEW WAS IN A FILE NOBODY OPENED.** There are **TWELVE** `.dc.html` files, not the five named in the message. Five are the mobile handoff; **`Wireframes` and `White-label` are ALSO mobile** (52px app bar, `height:100%`, three brands × six app screens — so the three-brand whole-site design is the MOBILE one, not the web one); and **`Investoyard Admin - Partner Site.dc.html` is the admin screen, with a `LIVE PREVIEW` pane and an "Open full page ↗" link.** The files with *Broker Website* in the name were treated as the whole brief and the one called *Admin* was never opened. **Enumerate the design set before deciding what it contains; a filename is not an index.**

#### The site-wide look is TOKENS, which is the only reason it is affordable
- **MEASURE THE TOKEN SURFACE BEFORE DESIGNING A THEME SYSTEM.** `--border` is consumed **198** times, `--text` 122, `--surface` 112, `--bg-2` 96, `--sh-sm` 35, `--r-lg` 24, `--header-h` 10, `--maxw` 6 — so redefining a handful of tokens per template reaches **hundreds of existing rules on every page at once**: every card, panel, table, header, footer and trust page. Four hand-written variants of 43 pages is the alternative, and it drifts the week after it ships.
- **`html[data-template='x']` IS (0,1,1), WHICH BEATS BOTH EXISTING TOKEN SCOPES** — the dark-mode block is a plain `:root` (0,1,0) and the admin shell is `.admin-shell` (0,1,0). That is what makes the overrides land **and** why `html[data-template] .admin-shell` has to restore the admin scale at (0,2,1): an operator opening `/admin` on a partner host would otherwise get that partner's radii in our own console.
- **ONLY STRUCTURAL TOKENS ARE TOUCHED — radii, shadows, widths, header height, type scale, spacing. NEVER COLOURS.** Those belong to the tenant's brand (`applyBranding`) and to the theme (the dark block), and a template reaching into either would be a third opinion about what colour a surface is.
- **`--sh-sm: none` IS THE SINGLE MOST EFFECTIVE LINE IN THE SET**, because it takes all 35 rules that read it flat at once — and flat hairline surfaces are what make a site read as a tool rather than a brochure. That is the whole difference between the `dashboard` look and the `broker` one.
- **BUT A TOKEN THEME DOES NOT REACH A LITERAL, AND THIS SHEET IS ONLY HALF TOKENISED FOR THE TWO PROPERTIES THAT CARRY A LOOK.** Measured: **67 rules write a literal `box-shadow`** against 81 that read the token, and **313 write a literal `border-radius`** against 149 that read one. So `--sh-sm: none` left `.panel` — which hardcoded both — **still rounded and shadowed on the IPO detail page while every surface around it had gone flat**, which is exactly how a template check earns its keep. `.card`, `.ipocard`, `.feature` and `.stepcard` were already fine (`var(--r-lg)`); `.panel` was the gap.
  - **`.panel`'s RADIUS WAS SWAPPED TO `var(--r)` BECAUSE THE TOKEN IS ALSO 14px** — provably a no-op on our own site, and it makes the one main surface that ignored the scale follow both the templates and the admin scope. **Check whether a literal already equals its token before replacing it: that is the difference between a free fix and a visual change.**
  - **`.subv2-card` (16px) AND `.subv2-det-panel` (12px) MATCH NO TOKEN AND WERE LEFT ALONE.** Swapping them would move our own subscription surfaces by 2–4px to serve a partner feature, which is not a trade to make unasked. Their SHADOWS are flattened per template instead, since a shadow is the louder signal.
  - The other ~60 literal shadows are chips, segmented controls and focus rings — **affordances, not surfaces** — and are deliberately untouched.
- **A FREE WIN WORTH KNOWING: `scroll-margin-top` on the detail page's sections is already `calc(var(--header-h) + 72px)`**, so every template's section anchors track its own header height with no rule of their own. **When a sheet is tokenised, check what ELSE starts working before writing a rule for it.**

#### The detail page gets a SHAPE, and the section order deliberately does not change
- broker 340px rail · dashboard **296px and a tight gutter** · detail **the widest at 366px**, because on a research surface the rail is navigation the reader returns to · **landing drops it entirely**, because a column of figures beside the prose competes with what a first-time investor is trying to read.
- **EVERY OVERRIDE KEEPS `minmax(0, …)`.** A grid track's automatic minimum is its CONTENT, so a bare `1fr` grows to min-content — which is what made every IPO detail page scroll sideways on a phone (600px inside a 347px parent). This is the fourth time that mechanism is recorded here.
- **THE SECTION ORDER IS THE STAGE ENGINE'S, NOT THE TEMPLATE'S.** `IpoDetailView` reorders by stage (open / awaiting / listed); a template shuffling that would be a second opinion about what matters *today*, which is a question about the issue and not about the partner's taste.

#### The live preview
- **THE REAL SITE IN A SAME-ORIGIN IFRAME, NEVER A MOCK-UP.** A mock-up of a layout is a second implementation of it and starts lying the first time the layout changes — the trap a hand-copied `check.html` fell into on the featured panel, where it rendered a figure the real component could not produce.
- **LAZY, AND THE REASON IS A MEASUREMENT**: the home page is ~10 MB of baked markup, so auto-loading it charges the operator that for merely arriving on the screen. Mounted on a click and kept mounted, so switching layouts re-navigates one iframe.
- **RENDERED AT 1280px AND SCALED**, so the operator sees the DESKTOP layout rather than the phone one a narrow iframe would trigger — the templates differ most **above** the 1100px breakpoint, which is the same measurement that once made a grid fault invisible at every width the browser pane happened to be.
- **IT SHOWS THE DRAFT, NOT THE SAVED ROW**, so picking a layout updates the frame before Save. A preview of what is already stored makes choosing a layout a matter of saving and hoping.
- **THE PAGE PICKER IS THE POINT, not a nicety**: a template is a site-wide look, so a preview that could only ever show the home page cannot demonstrate the thing being chosen.
- **`?preview=1` RESOLVES THE BRAND WITHOUT PERSISTING IT, and that is a real hazard rather than tidiness.** **sessionStorage is shared across a tab's same-origin frames**, and `?brand=` persists to `iy_brand` — so without it the preview would write the partner's brand into the OPERATOR's own tab and the next consumer page they opened would wear it. Proved both ways: preview applies `#0b5cad` and stores nothing, a later page in the same tab is unbranded, and `?brand=` alone still persists and survives a navigation.

#### `--muted` IS USED 18 TIMES AND DECLARED NOWHERE — AND 16 OF THEM WERE MINE
The sheet declares **`--text-muted`** (203 uses). `var()` on an undeclared custom property is **invalid at computed-value time**, so those rules inherit a colour instead of going grey — and they had no dark-mode value either, since `--text-muted` has three (light, dark, admin-dark). **16 of the 18 were mine and had been live for an hour**: every muted line in the broker hero, the RM card, the app band, the split explorer and the admin panel. Same family as the `.banner.bad` class that was used by six call sites and defined by none. **A `var()` on a token that does not exist fails silently and looks deliberate — check that every token a new block consumes is actually declared.**

#### Checks that lied, three more
- **A NOT-FOUND ELEMENT READ AS A FAILING ASSERTION.** The shadow check did `querySelector('.card') || querySelector('.panel')` and compared `boxShadow === 'none'` — but those are CLIENT-rendered on the pages under test, so it found `null`, and `null !== 'none'` reported "dashboard still has a card shadow" on three good pages. **The site-wide claim is a TOKEN, so assert the token** (`--sh-sm`), which an absent element cannot defeat; check an element too, but only when one was found, and name which selector matched.
- **BACKTICKS IN A COMMENT INSIDE A TEMPLATE LITERAL CLOSED THE LITERAL** — third time in this file. The comment was explaining the fix above.
- **A SLEEP THAT NEVER SLEPT**: a retry loop "waited" by spawning `node -e "setTimeout(()=>{},500)"`, which schedules nothing and exits at once, so 20 retries ran in milliseconds and Redis never got time to bind. **A wait has to block.**

#### Still open
- **NO LIVE TENANT HAS A `brandColor` SET** (measured across all 7 on the public registry), so the live preview will show OUR colours for every partner until someone fills in Branding. Same shape as `TenantContent` being empty: the system works and the data is not there.
- **The hubs beyond the detail page get the token layer only** — `/gmp`, `/allotment`, `/performance`, `/calendar`, `/news` change their chrome, type, density and surfaces but keep their own arrangement. Worth a pass if a partner asks for one.

## Meridian — the first whole-site theme (2026-11-06)

- **THE SCOPE WAS CORRECTED TWICE AND THE SECOND CORRECTION IS THE ONE TO READ.** Sir's words: *"it's complete new look and layout I want like this 4 to 5 template for whole frontend and partner select and his site look like that and not like this Broker / Dashboard / List and detail / Landing, whole frontend with that layout and template with Unique name of template or theme"*. So each `.dc.html` is a **COMPLETE WEBSITE DESIGN** with its own chrome, not a re-skin of ours wearing different tokens; there are **4–5 of them**, each under a **theme NAME** rather than a functional label; and a theme must reach all 43 consumer pages. The four functional ids are gone. **`SITE_TEMPLATES` now carries `default` + `meridian`** (from *Investoyard Broker Website v2*), and `themeReady` gates the ones not built yet.
- **A THEME IS ITS OWN HEADER AND FOOTER, not ours with a class on it.** `ThemeChrome.tsx` — Meridian's 1240px bar with a square brand mark and a five-column dark footer. Both chromes sit in the DOM on every page and **CSS shows one, keyed on the `data-template` the pre-paint script sets during PARSE**, so the right chrome is painted first rather than swapped after hydration. Branching one component into two designs leaves neither readable and the next theme makes it three.
- **`as const satisfies readonly T[]` NARROWS AWAY AN OPTIONAL FIELD.** Each entry narrows to its OWN literal type, and a literal type does not carry a field that entry omits — so `t.design` was a compile error for every consumer that LOOPED the list. It broke the spec, then the admin panel, the same way. Plain `readonly SiteTemplateMeta[]`; the narrow ids bought nothing because `SiteTemplate` is declared separately.

### The hero card, and the pick that could not fire
- **IT RENDERED NOTHING AND THAT WAS CORRECT BEHAVIOUR.** The card looked for an open issue, and **of 1,319 records the ENRICHED stages are 1,297 listed, 20 awaiting, 3 allotment out — ZERO open and ZERO upcoming.** So the design's centrepiece was an empty second grid column and the hero collapsed to a headline beside nothing. It takes **`ranked[0]` through `compareForList`** now — the house ordering already ranks Closing Today → Open → Pre Apply → Upcoming → Allotment Out → Awaiting → Listed, so this is one read of it rather than a second opinion, and Allotment Out is a perfectly good thing to lead with.
- **I COUNTED THE DEAD `status` COLUMN AND REPORTED A BEHAVING CARD AS BROKEN.** The raw API payload says **124 upcoming**; `enrich()` overwrites it with `effectiveStatus`, which correctly reads those as long-listed historical rows, and the page's own stat tiles said **1297 Recently listed**. **This file already records that mistake against the U4 claim and I made it again within the hour.** The tell was a page disagreeing with my query, and the page was right. **Count with `stageOf` over the ENRICHED record, never with `Ipo.status`.**
- **EVERY FIGURE COMES FROM THE SHARED RESOLVERS.** `subscriptionTable`, never `ipo.subscription[].timesSubscribed` — the stored figure is demand ÷ the GROSS offer where the resolver recomputes against the NET book. Reading the raw rows is what had mobile's hub showing 14.82× against the detail page's 36.74× on 59 records.
- **THE LEAD FIGURE DUPLICATED THE STRIP.** It fell back to the price band, and *Price band* is the strip's own first cell ~100px below — so an Allotment Out card read *"₹62–66 / price band"* then *"Price band ₹62–66"*. It leads with the subscription multiple whenever there is one (*subscribed overall* open, *subscribed, final* closed) and the strip **drops its band cell** when the band is the lead. Third instance of the two-ways-of-saying-one-thing fault, after the featured panel and the Today line.
- **"Live subscription" ON A CLOSED ISSUE is a small lie on every card that is not bidding — which today is all of them.** The label branches on `open`; the page it opens serves final figures perfectly well.
- **`IpoListItem` NOW DECLARES `subscription` AND `subscriptionAsOf`.** The list endpoint has always sent both and the type never declared them, so every reader cast through `any` — the `ApplicationView.investorProfileId` hazard again. `IpoDetail`'s identical re-declarations are dropped: a parent and a child declaring one field twice is how they come to disagree the day one is widened.

### `ThemeIpoSection` — three views, one explorer mount
- **IT WRAPS THE EXPLORER RATHER THAN REPLACING IT.** `IpoExplorer2` goes in as `children`, so the heaviest component on the page mounts ONCE and the two filter states have nowhere to disagree. The other two views are tables over the same `ipos` array, so **no fetch is added**. The switch is hidden by CSS on our own layout and the default state is the calendar, so our page renders what it rendered before the wrapper existed — measured: switch invisible, 16 cards, squeeze 0.
- **BOTH NEW VIEWS WERE FILTERED ON MEASUREMENTS, NOT ON STAGES.** The Allotment table ran **10 rows of withdrawn and postponed issues with every cell a dash** (the `awaiting` bucket is back-catalogue noise), so a row now needs an **allotment date** — 132 of 1,319 carry one, and dashed cells went **32 → 12**, which is one per row and is the Listing gain column at its real **1-in-1,319** coverage. The subscription table led with 2023 issues for the same reason; within the CLOSED rows it orders by close date now, so every row is from the last fortnight with a real as-of stamp. **Open issues keep their stage rank at the top** — a live book outranks any finished one however recent — and it is NOT a blanket date sort, which is the mistake already documented against `compareForList`.
- **THE FOOTNOTE IS OURS, NOT THE CANVAS'S.** The design says *"Bids at 4:00 PM from NSE and BSE. Updated every hour while the issue is open."* Neither half is true here — the poller runs every 60 s inside 10:00–18:00 IST and every 5 s through the last ninety minutes. Copying a placeholder claim about our own refresh rate is the cover-figure fault.
- **`#ipo-views`, NOT A SECOND `#ipos`.** The hero's CTA targeted `#ipos`, which lives INSIDE the explorer and is `hidden` whenever another view is open — scrolling to a `display:none` element does nothing. A duplicate id is the fault already paid for (the first wins, and on our layout the wrapper is `display: contents` with no box), so the always-visible wrapper gets a distinct id and lands the reader on the switch.

### Four faults only a render could find
- **`st t-live` RENDERED A COLOURLESS PILL.** `.st` carries explicit modifiers (`ok`/`bad`/`mut`/`brand`/`warn`/`lock`/`vfy`) and **reads NO tone token at all**; `.status` is the one that reads `--tone-soft`/`--tone-ink`, and that is the pair measured for TEXT. (`--tone-solid`/`--tone-on` is for a solid chip and leaves Awaiting at **4.05:1**; `.hb-tag` has that pre-existing.) **A class used and not defined inherits the base rule and looks deliberate** — same family as `.banner.bad` and `--muted`.
  - **SWEEPING FOR IT FOUND EXACTLY ONE MORE IN 208 FILES**: the visitors report's `className="st"` bot marker. **20 others take their modifier from a template hole and every one has a fallback** (`?? 'mut'`, or a complete ternary chain where it is not inline), so they are safe. The sweep checks each candidate against the modifiers the **SHEET** defines rather than a list typed into the sweep, and self-tests that it can see a MODIFIED `.st` before its result is believed.
- **THE HERO'S STAT TILES MEASURED `rgb(245,245,250)` ON AN `rgb(245,245,250)` PAGE.** The design draws them `#F5F5FA` because ITS hero sits on white; ours sits on `--bg`, which under Meridian IS that hex. `--surface` plus a hairline `--border` — **the border is what makes it hold in any theme**, since a tile delineated only by its fill vanishes the moment a theme puts the hero on that fill.
- **THE HEADER RENDERED "Sign in" TWICE.** `navCta` defaulted to `'Sign in'`, which is the ghost link's own label, **on a button pointing at `/signup`** — duplicated AND describing the wrong action. `SIGN_IN_LABEL` is exported so the component and the test read one string, and a test asserts the two differ.
- **A SCREENSHOT READ BY EYE IS NOT A MEASUREMENT.** The card's body labels looked brown in the PNG; the computed values are `#5c6187` (`--text-muted`) and `#181a2e` (`--text`), exactly right. Colour is the one thing to resolve in the DOM rather than judge from an image — layout is the thing an image CAN settle.

### The phone header, and three ways a flex row refuses to scroll
- **IT PUSHED EVERY PAGE SIDEWAYS — 38px on all 21 consumer pages at 375, against 0 unthemed.** The design is drawn at 1440 and never had to fit brand + four links + two buttons into 343px. **This is why a whole-site theme gets a whole-site sweep**, measured WITH and WITHOUT the theme at each width so a pre-existing overflow is never reported as a regression.
- **THE NAV HAD BEEN `display: none` BELOW 860 — MY OWN EARLIER RULE, LATER IN THE SHEET, WHICH BEAT THE NEW BLOCK.** Its four destinations are in the footer, so nobody was stranded, but sending a reader to the bottom of the page to navigate is not navigation. **A rule that only wins by its position in the file is one the next edit silently breaks** — within the hour, here. The two blocks are merged.
- **A SCROLL CONTAINER HAS TO BE DENIED EVERY ALTERNATIVE, ONE AT A TIME.** `overflow-x: auto` alone did nothing: the row **WRAPPED** (65px tall, nothing to scroll) until `flex-wrap: nowrap`, and then **SHRANK** its links ("Open IPOs" 73px → 44px) until `.thm-nav > * { flex: 0 0 auto }`. Declaring overflow is not the same as denying wrapping, and denying wrapping is not the same as denying shrinking.
- **`flex: 1 1 auto` GROWS, and in a wrapping row a grown item takes the whole line and shoves its sibling onto the next.** The brand measured the full 343px. The intent was *shrink if you must* — `flex: 0 1 auto`. **`min-width: 0` governs only how far an item may SHRINK; it says nothing about growing.**
- **WRAPPING IS DECIDED ON BASE SIZES, SO SHRINKING CANNOT PREVENT IT.** Brand 134 + the row's 28px column gap + actions 195 = 357 in 343, and the actions wrap however the shrink is set.
- **SO THE NARROW BAR STOPPED BEING STICKY instead of being squeezed.** One row needs the gap at 12 and the name capped near 90px, which fits *"Investoyard"* with **2px to spare at 375 and fails at 360** — and fails outright for any partner whose name is longer than ours. **A layout that holds only for the shortest tenant is not a layout, and this is white-label chrome where the next name is not ours.** Below 860: `position: static` and **`--header-h: 0`**, because ten rules read that token to clear a FLOATING bar and reserving 133px under one that no longer floats pushes every anchored heading down the page. Nothing is hidden. The stated cost: a phone reader loses the always-present *Open an account*; reversible in one line.
